# Finished TODOs - 2026-11

- [x] TODO-5628: std::string conformance and speed gate for `String`
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md, Parity with `std::string`), quality gate
  - parallel_track: string-quality
  - scope: Pin `String` to `std::string` with data. Add a differential suite: each operation of the Parity table that exists is run on the VM and through `--emit=optexe` and compared with a C++ reference program that applies the same operations to `std::string` (append, insert, erase, replace, substr, reserve/shrink, find, compare, edge cases at 0, 1, 31, 32 and 64 KiB). Add benchmarks (append in a loop, concatenation, many short strings, `find` in a long text, copy) timed against `std::string` and recorded in `benchmarks/benchmark_baseline.json` style with a budget. Fix every divergence the suite finds in the current `String` API. Record the limits found (1 GiB cap from `i32` counts, no inline small-string storage) in the spec's Parity section.
  - acceptance:
    - the suite runs in release validation under the 5 second doctest guardrail (split otherwise); benchmark numbers for `String` versus `std::string` are in docs/spec/strings-and-views.md with a regression budget; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: harness, baseline numbers and fixes to the existing API; new operations belong to TODO-5619 to TODO-5627.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: Added tests/unit/backend_host/test_string_conformance.cpp (seeded edit sequences and 15 size boundaries, VM and optexe vs a std::string oracle, all agree), benchmarks/strings/ with scripts/benchmark_strings.py and budget.json, and the measured ratios in docs/spec/strings-and-views.md. Found TODO-5639 (binding a String from a binding aliases the block). No divergence in the existing String API.

- [x] TODO-5640: String heap hot paths: O(1) frees, chunk index, region cache
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md, Parity with `std::string`), speed
  - parallel_track: string-quality
  - scope: Profiling `short_strings` (110 to 140x `std::string`) showed about 90% of its time in the linear `std::find` over a chunk's free list that `VmTextHeap::release` used to detect a double free, plus a linear scan of all chunks in `chunkWithFreeBlock`, a full recount of empty chunks on every chunk-emptying free, and a `std::map` search in `VmHeapCore::byteAt` for every byte access. Fix them in `VmTextHeapBody.inc` and `VmHeapCoreBody.inc` (shared by the VM and the optimized C++ runtime): a per-block free flag, a per-size-class ordered set of chunks with free blocks (lowest chunk first, as the scan was), an empty-chunk counter, and a one-entry last-region cache in `byteAt` that `releaseByteRegion` clears. Behavior and error messages stay the same; a free in the unused tail of a chunk becomes an invalid-address error.
  - acceptance:
    - the text-heap unit suite passes with new cases (tail free rejected, lowest chunk first with exact chunk accounting, randomized allocate/free cycles with every double free rejected); the TODO-5628 conformance suite and benchmarks pass with lowered budgets; `short_strings` is under 15x `std::string`; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the string heap and the byte-region lookup; byte loops are TODO-5629.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: Heap hot paths fixed in VmTextHeapBody.inc and VmHeapCoreBody.inc: O(1) double-free flag per block, per-class ordered set of chunks with free blocks, empty-chunk counter, one-entry last-region cache in byteAt; a free in a chunk tail is now an invalid-address error. short_strings 110-140x -> 5-9x std::string; insert_front 43-61x -> 32-48x; copy_text 15-35x -> 17-24x. Three new text-heap cases; conformance suite and budgets updated. Full release gate green (1876 of 1876, AppKit drivers skipped).

- [x] TODO-5639: A binding whose declared type is an imported short name skipped the Copy helper
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md), found by the TODO-5628 suite
  - parallel_track: lifecycle-strings
  - scope: `[String mut] s{stringFrom("hello")}` then `[String mut] c{s}` shared one heap block: `c.append("!!")` grew the text in place while `s.count()` stayed 5, and at scope end the VM stopped with `string heap block freed twice` (size 1 to 31) or `invalid string heap free address` (32 and up); `optexe` aborted the same way. Spelling the type `/std/text/String` worked, and so did a user struct with `Copy`. Cause: the struct-binding branch of the lowerer (`IrLowererLowerStatementsBindingsContinued.h`) resolved the slot layout from `info.structTypeName` (the full path) but looked up the `Copy` helper, and the disarm of a temporary source, with the raw type-transform name `structTypeName` (`String`, the imported short name), so `defMap.find("String/Copy")` missed and the struct was only bit-copied. Fix: use `info.structTypeName` for those three lookups. Tests: bindings of every spelling copy and free once; a copy in a 300-iteration loop; the TODO-5628 suite now uses the binding form.
  - acceptance:
    - the repro programs copy instead of aliasing (counts differ after appending to one side) and never fault; `Copy` also runs for a user struct; the TODO-5628 conformance suite uses `[String] copy{s}`; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the three lookups in the struct-binding branch; no new move/use-after-move checking.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: Fixed in IrLowererLowerStatementsBindingsContinued.h: the struct-binding branch used the raw type-transform name (the imported short name String) for the Copy helper lookup and the temporary-source disarm while the layout used the resolved path, so a binding from a binding only bit-copied and the block was freed twice. Three lookups now use info.structTypeName. New tests: bindings of every spelling copy and free once; a 300-iteration copy loop; the conformance suite uses the binding form. Full release gate green (1876 of 1876, AppKit drivers skipped).

- [x] TODO-5629: Bulk byte operations under `String` and `Slice<u8>`
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - depends_on: TODO-5628
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md, Parity with `std::string`), speed
  - parallel_track: string-quality
  - scope: `String.reserve/shrinkToFit/appendString/replace/substr/Copy` move bytes in `.prime` loops of `textLoad`/`textStore` today (appending or comparing a string literal reads the module string table, not a byte region, so those stay per byte until the string view of TODO-5540). Add copy, move (overlap-safe), fill, compare and find-byte as IR opcodes implemented as `__pstext_move/fill/compare/find` host functions answered by the VM and the optimized C++ runtime (no new opcode, no PSIR change) and by plain byte loops in the arm64 native emitter, expose them as `/std/text` helpers (`moveBytes`, `fillBytes`, `compareBytes`, `findByte`), and rebuild the `String` operations on them. `Slice<u8>` follows with the string view (TODO-5540), since generic slice code cannot specialize on `u8` yet. Bounds stay checked once per call.
  - acceptance:
    - VM and `optexe` tests incl. overlapping moves and out-of-range faults; the TODO-5628 benchmarks improve and the numbers are recorded; PSIR version note if opcodes are added; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the byte primitives and the String operations that use them; Slice<u8> follows the string view.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: Bulk byte operations: __pstext_move/fill/compare/find answered by the VM and optimized C++ runtime (one range check per call via VmHeapCore::byteSpan, no new opcode, no PSIR change) and by byte loops in the arm64 native emitter; std/text moveBytes/fillBytes/compareBytes/findByte; String reserve/shrinkToFit/appendString/replace/substr/Copy use them. Tests agree with memmove/memset/memcmp/memchr on VM, optexe and native; out-of-range faults. insert_front 32-48x -> ~1.1x, replace_middle 34-43x -> ~1.2x, copy_text 17-24x -> ~1.5x std::string. String-literal appends stay per byte until the string view (TODO-5540); Slice<u8> follows the view. Embed fixture regenerated. Full release gate green (1876 of 1876).

- [x] TODO-5540: `StringView`: a borrowed view of literals and Strings
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-10-08
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md), first of six leaves for the text view
  - parallel_track: string-view
  - scope: A borrowed read-only view of text in any byte region: the stdlib struct `StringView` (pointer and byte count, `/std/text`), made from a String with `s.view()` and from a string literal with `textOf("...")`. A literal's bytes get an address through the new `__pstext_literal([string] text) -> Pointer<u8>` text host function, answered by the VM and the optimized C++ runtime (a NUL-terminated byte region made on first use and kept for the run; `VmTextHeap::literal`), so there is no new opcode and no PSIR change. Operations of this leaf: `count`, `empty`, `byteAt` (faults out of range), `equals(StringView)`, `equalsText(string)`. The existing `string` stays the static literal type for now (print, map keys, `File` paths, host parameters and every current program are unchanged); `StringView` is the type for text that is not a literal, and unifying the two spellings is TODO-5621. Native backends reject `__pstext_literal` until TODO-5622 follow-ups.
  - acceptance:
    - VM and `optexe` tests: view of a String and of a literal, empty text (a literal and an empty String), `byteAt` ends and out of range, `equals` and `equalsText` for equal, different and shorter text, a view after the String grew; the existing suite passes unchanged; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the view type, literals and Strings as sources, and the operations above on the VM and optexe; borrowing rules, slicing, search and parsing are TODO-5618 to TODO-5620.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: StringView (stdlib struct, pointer+count) made by s.view() or textOf("literal"); a literal gets an address from the new __pstext_literal text host function (VM and optexe; no opcode, no PSIR change); count/empty/byteAt/equals/equalsText. string stays the static literal type; unifying is TODO-5621. Release gate green (1876 of 1876 once the vm_string_lookups lint was satisfied via vmStringCount).

  - acceptance:
    - VM and `optexe` tests: slices at every boundary of mixed 1- to 4-byte text, nested slices, cuts inside a code point and out-of-range ranges stopping the program, `bytes()` round trip, and `utf8ErrorOffset` against an independent C++ validator over 24 edge vectors (overlong, surrogate, truncated, above U+10FFFF); full release gate green
  - stop_rule: construction, slicing and validation on the VM and optexe.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: StringView slice (String and view), isBoundary, bytes() as ReadSlice<u8>, utf8ErrorOffset and textFromBytes; cuts inside a code point or outside the text stop the program; the validator matches an independent C++ one over 24 edge vectors on the VM and optexe. Loan rules stay TODO-5569; Result forms wait on TODO-5642 and the result lane.

- [x] TODO-5619: `StringView` search, comparison, trimming, pieces and lines
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-10-30
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md), third of six leaves for the text view
  - parallel_track: string-view
  - scope: On `StringView`, built on the bulk byte functions: `compare` (-1, 0, 1 by byte order), `startsWith`, `endsWith`, `contains`, `indexOfByte`, `indexOf`, `indexOfFrom` and `lastIndexOf` (index or -1, like `ReadSlice`; the empty needle occurs at the start), `trim`/`trimStart`/`trimEnd` (ASCII white space), `splitCount`/`splitPart` (views of the pieces between separators) and `lineCount`/`lineAt` (line feed ends a line, a carriage return before it is dropped, no empty line after a final line feed). Forward search is linear time: Crochemore-Perrin two-way matching (no allocation), a single byte is a byte scan. Not here: `Maybe<i32>` results (a method returning a sum does not lower, TODO-5642), the `<` family of operators (use `compare`), `String` receivers (use `s.view()`).
  - acceptance:
    - VM and `optexe` tests: `indexOf`, `lastIndexOf`, `compare`, `startsWith`, `endsWith`, `contains` against `std::string` over 170 cases including random two- and three-letter-alphabet inputs (periodic needles); trim, split and lines against a C++ reference; a 200 000-byte (VM) and 2 000 000-byte (optexe) worst-case haystack searched in linear time; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the operations on the VM and optexe.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: StringView compare/startsWith/endsWith/contains/indexOf/indexOfFrom/lastIndexOf (-1), trim*, splitCount/splitPart, lineCount/lineAt; forward search is linear time (two-way). 170 search cases match std::string, pieces match a C++ reference, 2 MB worst case searched linearly on optexe. Maybe forms wait on TODO-5642.

- [x] TODO-5644: Split `/std/text` so a plain `String` program compiles fast
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md), found building TODO-5619 to TODO-5627
  - parallel_track: string-startup
  - scope: A stdlib module is spliced into the compilation unit whole (docs/LibrarySymbolManifestLazyImports.md), so the cost of `import /std/text/*` is the size of `string.prime`. Measured 2026-11-02 (macOS arm64, release `primevm`): a program that makes one `String` compiled in 0.12 s before the `StringView` work and takes 0.50 s now (the validator visits 2119 calls instead of 335): the file grew from about 400 to about 1900 lines and imports the slice and vector modules. Split it along the real dependency: keep the `String` core (struct, heap functions, bulk byte functions, number appends) in `/std/text`, and move `StringView` and everything that needs it (search, parse, format, join, files, lossy decoding, `String.view/slice/pop/resize/appendView` as explicit-self members) into its own module root `/std/text/view` named in `stdlib/std/modules.psmeta`, so `import /std/text/*` stays small. New heavy features (Unicode tables) get their own roots from the start.
  - acceptance:
    - a one-`String` program compiles within 0.2 s on the same machine (`--benchmark-semantic-phase-counters` visits fewer than 800 calls); every existing text test passes with the new imports; the rename of the type path is documented in docs/spec/strings-and-views.md; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the split and the import changes; no new features.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: Split /std/text into the String core and a /std/text/view module (modules.psmeta roots); a one-String program compiles in 0.15 s (0.50 s before, 0.12 s baseline); conformance suite split into VM and optexe ctest entries. Release gate green (1878 of 1878).

- [x] TODO-5625: Hashing text and a hash table for text keys
  - owner: ai
  - status: done

  - parallel_track: string-keys
  - scope: `StringView.hash()`, `hashSeeded(seed)` and `String.hash()`: SipHash-1-3 of the bytes through the new `__pstext_hash` text host function (VM and optimized C++ runtime, so the value is the same on both; the language has no bitwise operators to do it in `.prime`). `import /std/text/string_map/*` gives `StringMap<V>`: text keys (copied into one pooled String) to Copy values, open addressing with linear probing and backward-shift deletion, at most 3/4 full, dense entries (`keyAt(i)`, `valueAt(i)`), `insert`, `at`, `getOr`, `tryGet`, `contains`, `indexOf`, `erase`, `clear`, `setSeed`, with the key pool compacted after erases. Lookups take a `StringView`, so a literal or a slice needs no copy. Not here: making the builtin `map<String, V>` use hashing (the existing map is a linear scan over `Vector<K>`; changing it is a refactor of a core container), a `random` effect for the seed (the caller passes a seed).

    - VM and `optexe` tests: SipHash-1-3 against an independent reference for seven inputs (0 to 45 bytes) and two seeds; `StringMap` against `std::unordered_map` over 2 500 keys on the VM and 100 000 run-time keys on `optexe`: insert, overwrite, lookup, erase every third, re-insert every sixth, key bytes and values summed, `clear`; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the hash functions and `StringMap`.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: StringView.hash/hashSeeded and String.hash (SipHash-1-3 via __pstext_hash, identical on the VM and optexe, matches an independent reference); StringMap<V> (import /std/text/string_map/*): open addressing, backward-shift deletion, pooled key bytes; matches std::unordered_map over 100000 run-time keys through optexe. The builtin map<String,V> stays a linear scan.

- [x] TODO-5636: Interned `Symbol` for names and tags
  - owner: ai

  - parallel_track: string-keys
  - scope: `import /std/text/interner/*`: an `Interner` object (the language has no safe global state, so the table is not per process) interns a view into a `Symbol` (an i32 id, numbered in first-seen order so a run is deterministic): `intern(view)`, `find(view)` (an invalid symbol when absent), `text(symbol)` (a view), `count()`; `Symbol.equals`, `hash` (the id) and `valid`. Built on `StringMap<i32>`, whose dense entry index is the id.

    - VM and `optexe` tests: ids in first-seen order, symbol equality exactly when the texts are equal, `text` round trip, `find` without making one, 100 000 interns over 20 011 distinct texts against a C++ oracle; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the Interner and Symbol; a global table is not part of this leaf.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-02
  - result: Interner and Symbol (import /std/text/interner/*): ids in first-seen order, find without making, text round trip; 100000 lookups over 20011 texts match a C++ oracle on the VM and optexe. Explicit Interner object instead of a per-process table.

- [x] TODO-5642: A method of an imported struct in a sum-returning function failed to lower; `Maybe` results for `StringView`
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md), found building TODO-5619 and TODO-5620
  - parallel_track: lowering-gaps
  - scope: Calling a non-trivial method of `StringView` inside a function that returns a `Maybe` failed with `vm backend cannot resolve struct layout: /Reference</std/text/view/StringView>`, and defining `find`, `rfind` or `parseI32` as members or as explicit-`self` definitions failed or broke later method calls. Both had one cause: `inferBaseSetupSemanticFieldAccessKind` (`src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerInferenceBaseKindInternal.h`) looked the receiver's recorded type text up as a struct path, and the receiver of an inlined method is recorded as `Reference<Type>`; the failed lookup also left a layout error behind. It now unwraps `Reference<T>` and `Pointer<T>` before building the candidate paths. With that, `StringView.find(needle)`, `rfind(needle)`, `parseI32()`, `first()` and `last()` return `Maybe` (`Maybe<i32>`, `Maybe<u8>`), and `Maybe` gains `unwrapOr(fallback)` to read a value out. (`pick`, `plus` and other builtin names are not usable as user function or method names: they capture the builtin.)
  - acceptance:
    - VM and `optexe` tests: 170 search cases and 27 integer texts where the `Maybe` forms agree with `indexOf`/`lastIndexOf`/`parseI32Or`, and `first`/`last` on empty and non-empty text; every existing test passes; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the unwrap in the field-access kind inference and the `Maybe` forms; no change to sum lowering.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-03
  - result: Reference/Pointer receiver unwrap in field-access kind inference; Maybe-valued StringView find/rfind/parseI32/first/last and Maybe.unwrapOr, VM and optexe differential tests

- [x] TODO-5643: Operators and index sugar for stdlib types (`String + String`, `s[i]`)
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md), found building TODO-5568
  - parallel_track: lowering-gaps
  - scope: Done in this leaf: when the first operand of `plus`, `equal`, `not_equal`, `less_than`, `less_equal`, `greater_than`, `greater_equal` or `at` is a struct value and the second has the same type (not for `at`), the template-monomorph pass rewrites the call to the method `opPlus`, `opEqual`, ..., `opAt` of that struct (`rewriteStructOperatorCallToMethod`, run on operands first; `!=` is `opNotEqualTo`, see TODO-5647). The members are spelled with an `op` prefix because a member named `plus` captures every bare `plus(a, b)` written in the struct's own scope. `String` has `+`, the six comparisons and `s[i]`; `StringView` has the comparisons and `v[i]`; a user struct gets them by defining the members. Only names, free-function calls and operator results are accepted as operands (type inference of field accesses and method chains is unreliable and they are left alone).
  - acceptance:
    - `String + String`, `==`/`!=`/`<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=` on Strings and views and `s[i]` agree with `std::string` on the VM and through `optexe` (done); a user struct with `opPlus`/`opLessThan` works (done); a struct without the member keeps the numeric-operand diagnostic (done); full release gate green
  - stop_rule: struct operator dispatch and index sugar in the template-monomorph pass; no operator definitions for other stdlib types in this leaf; `+=` needs lexer support (the language has no `+=` token) and is not part of it.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-03
  - result: Struct operator dispatch in template monomorph: plus/equal/comparisons/at on same-type struct operands rewrite to op-prefixed members (opPlus, opEqual, opNotEqualTo, opLessThan, ..., opAt); String and StringView operators, s[i]; differential tests on VM and optexe; follow-ups TODO-5646/5647

  - scope: Done: the template-monomorph operator rewrite flattens a chain of three or more `String` operands (`a + b + c`, `a + (b + c)`) into one call `a.opPlusMany(b, c)` that sums the counts, allocates once and copies each part in order, with left-to-right evaluation; the rule is in docs/spec/transforms.md. `stringHeapAllocations()` (a new heap statistic) counts the blocks handed out. `+=` has no token in the language and is not part of it.

  - finished_at: 2026-11-03
  - result: Lexical slice loan checker (validateSliceLoans): borrowed binding on structural change of the owner before the last use, escapes via field, container and return, returns_borrow for ambiguous returns; remaining rules split to TODO-5649

- [x] TODO-5630: Request-scoped string arenas: `withStringArena`
  - owner: ai
  - status: done

  - parallel_track: string-arenas
  - scope: Done: `[StringArena] arena{beginStringArena()}` (a struct whose `Destroy` ends the arena; `Create` is not run by brace construction, so the begin is a function) in `stdlib/std/text/string.prime`, backed by `__pstext_arena_begin`/`__pstext_arena_end` in the shared heap body (bump regions that start at 4 KiB and double, large blocks of their own, no per-block free, all regions returned at the end, nesting, the memory limit counting regions, `stringArenaDepth()`/`stringArenaBytes()`). Escapes are rejected by the loan checker (`string escapes its arena via return|outer binding|container`) and any other use after the end faults. The spec decided against copying a `String` out at the boundary: moving one out is rejected. Measured: about 13x faster than the heap at the allocator (unit test), parity in the language-level request benchmark.
  - acceptance:
    - VM and `optexe` tests: many allocations then one release, nested arenas, escape negatives, a limit hit inside an arena; an allocator benchmark shows the arena beating per-string allocation for a request-shaped pattern (the language-level request benchmark is recorded and shows parity); full release gate green
  - stop_rule: arenas for `String` only; no other allocator surface.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-03
  - result: String arenas: StringArena with beginStringArena, bump regions in the shared heap body, nesting, limit, escape checks in the loan checker, use-after-end faults; VM and optexe tests; allocator about 13x faster, language-level request benchmark at parity

- [x] TODO-5655: Callee-reachable receiver addresses so `mut` methods can be real calls
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-03
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md), split from TODO-5645
  - parallel_track: lowering-gaps
  - scope: Measured 2026-11-03: the 8-call `StringMap<i32>` sample still takes 3.2 s end to end under `primevm` and `--emit=ir` writes 33 MB for it, because `insert`, `erase` and the other `mut` methods are inlined at every call site and the read-only slice of TODO-5645 does not reach them. A local address is an index into the current frame (`indirectSlot` in `src/runtime/VmFastKernel.cpp`, `AddressOfLocal` in `VmExecutionKernel.cpp`), so a callee cannot write through a caller's address. Choose one model and apply it in every consumer of the IR: (a) a copy-in/copy-out receiver (the callee returns its modified receiver slots through a result area that the call site stores back), or (b) frame-qualified addresses (a call-depth or frame-base tag that `AddressOfLocal`/`LoadIndirect`/`StoreIndirect` honor in the VM kernels, the simple and optimizing C++ emitters, the arm64 backend and the GLSL/Wasm validators). Then let `computeRealCallEligibleDefinitionPaths` accept `mut` receivers and struct parameters, including `/std/collections/` receivers once their collection-record metadata survives the call.
  - findings (2026-11-03): copy-out through the operand stack (the callee pushes its receiver slots below the result and the call site pops them back into the receiver) needs no VM change, since `Call`/`Return` share one operand stack, but it makes a function leave more than its result behind. The optimizing C++ emitter and the native backends compile each IR function to a function that returns only its result, and virtual-register lowering assumes a `Call` consumes its parameters and yields at most one value, so the trick works in the VM only. A portable copy-out needs a result area that survives the frame (the heap, or a frame-qualified address), which is the work this block scopes.
  - result (2026-11-03): for the VM target a `mut` method is a real call that receives the address of a heap copy of its receiver (`HeapAlloc`, copy in, call, copy back, `HeapFree`; `IrLowererLowerInlineCalls.cpp`), and a struct passed by value is its slots on the operand stack, rebuilt in a block of the callee's frame (`IrLowererLowerStatementsCallsStage.cpp`). The heap address is valid in every frame, so no kernel or backend changed. Eligibility (`computeRealCallEligibleDefinitionPaths(..., allowMutReceivers)`) takes these only for the VM target and keeps structs with `Copy`/`Move`/`Destroy` helpers, `/std/collections/` receivers and definitions with a generated tail value inline. Measured with the 8-call `StringMap<i32>` sample under `primevm`: 3.2 s before, 1.9 to 2.0 s after (a program that only constructs the map takes 1.0 s). The serialized `--emit=ir` module stays inlined (33 MB) because that target is backend-neutral.
  - acceptance:
    - the 8-call `StringMap` sample lowers in under 2 s and its IR is under a quarter of today's size; every existing test passes with identical output; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the addressing model and the eligibility extension; no inliner tuning.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-03
  - result: mut methods and by-value struct parameters are real calls on the VM target via a heap copy of the receiver; sample 3.2 s to 1.9 s

- [x] TODO-5645: Real calls for struct-receiver methods (library code is inlined at every call site)
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md), found building TODO-5625
  - parallel_track: lowering-gaps
  - scope: Only definitions whose parameters and result are all plain scalars can be lowered as real `Call`s (`computeRealCallEligibleDefinitionPaths` in `src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererRecursionAnalysis.h`); a method with a struct receiver, a pointer or a view is inlined at every call site. For stdlib types built from small methods this multiplies: lowering a program that makes one `StringMap<i32>` and calls it eight times took about 5 s before TODO-5653 and takes 2.4 s after it (one `insert` 1.5 s), and `vector<i32>` alone costs 0.8 s. Extend real-call emission to definitions with struct and pointer parameters (pass the address of the receiver, as the inline path does for `mut` parameters), starting with the non-recursive library methods that are called from more than one site.
  - first slice done (2026-11-03): read-only (`mut`-free) struct helper methods with scalar parameters and a scalar or void result, outside `/std/collections/`, are now real calls when they have two or more call sites (`computeRealCallEligibleDefinitionPaths`, `IrLowererLowerStatementsCallsStage.cpp`, `IrLowererLowerInlineCalls.cpp`). A local address is an index into the current frame (`VmFastKernel.cpp` `indirectSlot`), so a callee cannot read through the address of a caller's struct; the receiver therefore goes as a copy of its slots on the operand stack, rebuilt in a block of the callee's frame. Measured: the 8-call `StringMap` sample still lowers in 2.4 s because its methods are `mut` or called once. Still open: `mut` receivers and struct parameters (they need a write-back or an addressing model where a callee can reach a caller's frame, in the VM, the C++ runtime and the native emitters), the `copy`/`move` lifecycle, and the collection records whose locals carry element and key/value kinds.
  - remaining work (2026-11-03): the `mut` receiver and struct parameter part is split out as TODO-5655; this block closes with it.
  - design notes (2026-11-03): the pieces exist but are not joined. (1) Eligibility (`computeRealCallEligibleDefinitionPaths`) rejects `takesImplicitThis` helpers, `mut` parameters and any non-scalar parameter; the call site (`IrLowererLowerInlineCalls.cpp`, the `realCallReservationIndex` branch) pushes `emitExpr` values, and `IrLowererLowerStatementsCallsStage.cpp` pops them into scalar `Value` locals 0..N-1. (2) The inline path binds a struct parameter without copying when the argument is a struct local (`LoadLocal` of the struct local is its address, stored in the parameter's local; a `mut` struct parameter becomes `Kind::Reference` the same way) and copies slots only for other arguments (`emitInlineDefinitionCallParameters`, `IrLowererInlineParamHelpers.cpp`). (3) `buildCallableDefinitionCallContext` (`IrLowererStatementCallHelpers.cpp`) already builds the locals of a definition lowered as its own function, including the implicit `this`, struct layouts and bare struct names, from `inferCallParameterLocalInfo`; the real-call body loop should reuse it instead of the scalar-only loop. (4) Open questions: the `copy`/`move` parameter lifecycle (drop flags are released at the call site today), argument shapes that need a temporary, and `isStructParamMatch` aliases for bare imported names (TODO-5650). A first slice could take only struct receivers (`this`) of non-`mut`-free methods with scalar or void results.
  - acceptance:
    - the 8-call `StringMap` sample lowers in under 2 s; the IR of programs that call a method from several sites shrinks; every existing test passes with identical output; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: eligibility and emission for struct/pointer parameters; no inliner tuning beyond that.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-03
  - result: Struct-receiver methods are real calls: read-only ones by slot copy, mut ones through TODO-5655; sample 3.2 s to 1.9 s on the VM

- [x] TODO-5638: Shared immutable strings (`SharedString`)
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-02
  - phase: Strings and slices (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md, Beyond std::string)
  - parallel_track: strings
  - scope: `/std/text/shared` (`stdlib/std/text/shared.prime`, a lazy module): a `SharedString` is an immutable, reference-counted text. Copying it shares the bytes and adds one to the count, `view()` is a `StringView` over the shared bytes, `owners()` reports the count, and the last owner's `Destroy` frees the block. The count is four bytes after the text and its 0 byte, so the bytes start at the block and need no offset pointer. The count is a plain integer: sharing between threads is TODO-5656, which waits for atomics and per-thread string heaps.
  - acceptance:
    - copy, scope exit and the last owner behave as described (VM test in `test_string_owned.cpp`); full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the single-threaded type; no thread-safety claims.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-03
  - result: Single-threaded refcounted SharedString in /std/text/shared; thread-safe version split to TODO-5656

- [x] TODO-5670: A `[ref]`-captured vector of `Function`-holding structs passes to a parameter correctly
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-06
  - phase: Web server blockers (found probing closures for a request router)
  - parallel_track: closures-ref-captures
  - scope: With `[struct] Route { [i32] code  [Function<i32, i32> mut] handler }`, a local `[vector<Route> mut] routes{...}` holding two routes, `call_all([vector<Route> mut] routes, [i32] input)` that sums `routes[i].handler(input)`, and `[mut] app{[ref routes]([i32] x) { return(call_all(routes, x)) }}`, `app(10i32)` compiles but faults on VM and optexe with `VM error: unaligned indirect address in IR: 68`. Calling `call_all(routes, 10i32)` directly returns the right sum, and a `[ref values]` capture of a `vector<i32>` passed to a `[vector<i32> mut]` parameter works, so the fault is in forwarding the closure's reference field when the element type is a struct holding a `Function`. In the same area, indexing a `[ref values]` capture of a `vector<i32>` inside the lambda body (`values[input]`) is rejected with `unknown call target: /std/collections/map/at`.
  - implementation_notes: ref captures are built in place as `/main__lambdaN{[name] location(name)}` (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Lambdas; `src/semantics/SemanticsValidateLambdaClosures.cpp`); compare the lowered IR of the direct and the closure call with `--dump-stage`.
  - acceptance:
    - both repros run on VM, native and optexe with the expected values (matrix cases in `tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_closures.cpp`)
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: forwarding and indexing `[ref]`-captured vectors; no change to capture semantics.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: inlined vector<struct> parameters take the inline element stride from a Reference/Pointer argument (and Reference<vector<T>> locals record it); a bare at/count/... inside a struct method or namespace now routes to the borrowed vector helper; matrix cases closure_ref_capture_struct_vector_parameter (35) and closure_ref_capture_vector_index (116), semantics tests in primestruct.semantics.lambdas

- [x] TODO-5672: A scalar call result is not a borrow of the call's view arguments
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-06
  - phase: Web server blockers (found probing closures for a request router)
  - parallel_track: slice-loans
  - scope: Inside a loop body, `total = total + score(req)` where `req` is a struct of `StringView`s into a block-local `String` and `score` returns `i32` is rejected with `slice escapes via outer binding total (root: buffer)`. The same happens for `total = total + f(buffer.view())`, and for a closure called with such arguments. Going through a local (`[i32] got{score(req)}` then `total = total + got`) passes. A call whose result type cannot hold a borrow (numbers, `bool`, structs without view fields) must not make its result a loan on the arguments' roots. Every request loop that counts or sums hits this.
  - implementation_notes: call roots of `validateSliceLoans` (`src/semantics/SemanticsValidateSliceLoans.cpp`), next to the carrier rules of TODO-5631.
  - acceptance:
    - positive tests for an `i32` and a `bool` result of a function and of a closure taking a view and a carrier, assigned to an outer binding; the negative test for a view-returning call stays
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: result types that cannot carry a borrow; no change to the carrier rules.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: validateSliceLoans treats a call whose declared result is a number, bool, void, String or a non-carrier struct (or whose inferred `return(...)` is arithmetic, a comparison or a count, as for a closure `call`) as lending nothing; tests in primestruct.language.string_owned (function, bool and closure results; the view-returning negative stays)

In `same_field([String] owned, [StringView] wanted) { [StringView] mine{owned.view()} return(mine == wanted) }`, it is rejected with `comparisons require numeric, bool, or string operands`, with or without `effects(heap_alloc)`; `mine.equals(wanted)` works. `==` must resolve to `/StringView/opEqual` whatever the view came from.

- [x] TODO-5678: `Pointer<u8>` host parameters reach C++ host functions as real byte pointers
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-06
  - phase: Web server (Docker image built with `--emit=optexe`), network leaf 1
  - parallel_track: host-bytes
  - scope: A `[host]` parameter of type `Pointer<u8>` lowers to `IrHostValueKind::U64` (`src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerInlineCalls.cpp`), and the optimized C++ emitter passes the raw slot, so the host receives a tagged VM heap address. A shim that dereferences it segfaults. Add a `BytePointer` host kind: semantics requires each `Pointer<u8>` host parameter to be followed by a `u64` length; `src/backend/IrToOptCppEmitter.cpp` passes `uint8_t *` resolved with `rt.heap.byteSpan(address, length)` and faults with `invalid byte pointer passed to host function` on a bad span (address 0 with length 0 passes `nullptr`). `hostCType`, the IR serializer and printer, and `VmHost`/`Script::bind` (which reject the kind with a clear message) follow. Socket `recv`/`send` and any other byte-buffer host function need this.
  - acceptance:
    - an optexe test with a small C++ shim that fills and sums a `vector<u8>` buffer through `sliceMut`/`data`; a length past the buffer faults instead of reading; IR dump shows the new kind; docs/spec/host-and-core-library.md describes the convention
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the host kind and its pairing rule; no socket code.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: IrHostValueKind::BytePointer (PSIR v32, printed `bytes`): a `Pointer<u8>` host parameter outside `__pstext_*` lowers to it and must be followed by a `u64` length; optexe passes `uint8_t *` from `rt.heap.byteSpan` and faults with `invalid byte pointer passed to host function` (nullptr for 0/0); the VM, Script::bind, the simple C++ emitter and native reject it by name; fixtures tests/fixtures/host_calls/bytes_host{,_overrun}.prime + bytes_impl.cpp, tests in primestruct.backend.cpp_strings and primestruct.ir.module_printer; spec in docs/spec/host-and-core-library.md (Byte buffers)

  - owner: ai

  - parallel_track: os-host
  - scope: A container server needs its port from the environment, a monotonic clock for idle timeouts, and a clean stop on SIGTERM. Nothing like this exists in the stdlib. Add `/std/os` over `[host]` functions in a POSIX shim linked with `--link-arg` (the scheme of `tests/fixtures/host_calls/text_impl.cpp`):

    Decide in the spec whether these need an effect.

    - full release gate green

  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: `/std/os` (`stdlib/std/os/os.prime`, module root in `stdlib/std/modules.psmeta`): `envOr`, `monotonicMs`, `stopRequested` over `[host]` `ps_os_*` functions answered by `stdlib/std/os/os_posix.cpp` (getenv, CLOCK_MONOTONIC, SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers installed at startup without SA_RESTART); no effect (documented); docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md section with a runnable example; optexe test with a real SIGTERM (exit 111) and a VM unbound-host negative in primestruct.backend.cpp_strings

- [x] TODO-5668: Struct fields of collection type keep their elements across method calls
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-06
  - phase: Closures follow-ups
  - parallel_track: collections-fields
  - scope: Found while building stateful closures (a closure that captures a `vector` and pushes to it is this shape). On VM and optexe, `[struct] KeepV { [vector<i32> mut] seen }` built as `KeepV{[seen] start}` from a local vector faults on `k.seen.push(1i32)` (`VM error: invalid heap free address in IR: 9223372036854775808`); built from `vector<i32>(5i32)` the push is lost (`k.seen.count()` is 0); a field default `[vector<i32> mut] seen{vector<i32>()}` is rejected with `argument type mismatch ... expected vector<i32>`; copying such a struct and pushing to the copy faults with `invalid heap realloc address`. Same on master before the closure work.
  - acceptance:
    - each repro runs on VM, native and optexe with the expected counts; a closure capturing a vector by `[mut values]` can push and count; matrix test
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: collection-typed struct fields (construction, method calls through the field, copy); no new collection API.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: `v.count()` on a struct vector field (`k.seen.count()`) fell into the array-count fallback, which loaded slot 0 of the inline vector record instead of its count; it now steps to the count slot as for vector literals. Pushes, field defaults, struct copies and a closure owning a vector by init capture then all run on VM, native and optexe (matrix cases struct_vector_field_push_count 231, struct_vector_field_copy_and_closure 213; diagnostic test in primestruct.language.string_owned). Split off: construction from a named vector local double-frees and `vector<T>` local copies alias (TODO-5681); a native operand bug with a mutating vector method inside an arithmetic expression (TODO-5682)

Documented in stdlib-reference.md (Network); install already ships stdlib/. test_net.cpp builds tests/fixtures/host_calls/net_echo.prime on optexe and checks the VM reports unbound net_listen.

  - owner: ai

  - parallel_track: effects
  - scope: A call to a definition annotated `[effects(...)]` compiles from a caller whose active effects lack them (`listen` from `/std/net` in a plain `main` passes semantics and fails at run time with `unbound host function`). Semantics checks that every effect a callee declares is active in the caller, for plain calls, method calls, monomorphized template calls and calls inside lambdas (docs/spec/language-core.md, Callee effects).

  - result: Semantics rejects a call when the caller lacks an effect the callee declares with [effects(...)] (plain, method, monomorphized template and lambda calls; closure calls exempt). Chose the narrow rule: the full transitive rule failed 527 of 2840 semantics cases (mostly inside stdlib Vector/SoaColumn lifecycle helpers) versus 47 for the narrow rule. Those, about 75 compile-run and IR-pipeline cases, the text editor example and a few stdlib helpers (png decoding, `MapValue.Move`, generated `SoaSchemaStorageClear`) gained the effects their calls need; `/File/close` now declares `file_read` like the builtin. Full rule deferred as TODO-5688.

- [x] TODO-5691: `/std/http` `Router` dispatch
  - owner: ai

  - parallel_track: http-router
  - depends_on: TODO-5689, TODO-5690
  - scope: `Router` in `/std/http`: a `vector` of routes holding a method, a literal pattern (exact path, or a prefix ending in `*`) and a `Function<void, Request, Reference<Response>>`. It has `get`, `post`, `put`, `delete`, `route(method, pattern, handler)`, and `call(req, res)`, so a `Router` is itself a handler. The first match wins; a path that matches only under another method answers 405, and no match answers 404.

    - full release gate green

Compiler fixes on the way: an imported callable struct named by its short name is callable through a generic parameter; template parameters substituted with imported short names, and Reference/Pointer of imported structs, get full paths; a vector-of-struct field of a namespaced struct resolves its bare element type in that namespace (element size and field receivers). Filed TODO-5696.

  - parallel_track: http-server
  - scope: Add `stdlib/std/http/server.prime` (`import /std/http/server/*`) with `ServerConfig` (port, backlog, maxRequestBytes, accept/read/write timeouts, loopbackOnly, announce), `ServeError` (a `/std/net` code with `why()`), `serve<Handler>(handler, config)` (until `stopRequested()`) and `serveUntil<Handler, Stop>(handler, config, stop)`, each returning `Result<ServeError>`. The loop is single-threaded: accept, read until the parse is complete or rejected, call the handler, write, close. Rejected requests get a bare status, and connection errors close that connection. -4 (EINTR) and accept timeouts re-check the stop condition, and only a failed `listen` returns an error. `announce` prints `listening on <port>` to stderr. Add `/std/os` `logLine(text)` (an unbuffered stderr line from a view).
  - acceptance:
    - an optexe loopback test that forks a server on an ephemeral port and checks, with a timeout on every call: a routed 200, a 404, a malformed request (400), an oversized one (413), a client that sends nothing (cut off by the read timeout, server keeps serving), and exit 0 on SIGTERM; exit 77 / skip when sockets are denied
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: one connection at a time, `Connection: close`; no keep-alive, chunked encoding or TLS.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: stdlib/std/http/server.prime (/std/http/server): ServerConfig, ServeError, serve (until stopRequested) and serveUntil; single-threaded accept/read/parse/handle/write/close with a read deadline, bare answers for rejected or oversized requests and a short drain before close. Forked loopback test (200, 404, 405, 400, two 413s, a silent client cut off at the read timeout, POST echo, exit 0 on SIGTERM). Compiler fix: a Function value's call needs none of its closures' effects (spec updated, semantics test). logLine moved to TODO-5693; filed TODO-5697 for ? over vector-field structs.

Program-matrix case result_try_vector_field_struct (ok and error paths, exit code from on_error). examples/apps/http_hello and tests/fixtures/http/http_server.prime use serve(...)? with on_error<ServeError, log_serve_error>; stdlib-reference HTTP server section and example updated.

Inside a stdlib namespace the same read compiled but faulted at run time (`invalid heap free address`), apparently because the indexed element is copied out through `/std/collections/vector/at__t...` and destroyed with its `Function` field. `/std/http` `Route` stores `StringView`s instead, which read correctly.

Full gate 1888/1888.

- [x] TODO-5695: Definitions with struct parameters become real calls on the optimizing backends
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-06
  - phase: Web server, HTTP layer (found while testing)
  - parallel_track: ir-real-calls
  - scope: Only definitions whose parameters and result are all scalar become real calls (`hasOnlyScalarParameters` / `hasScalarOrVoidReturn` in `src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererRecursionAnalysis.cpp`). Every other call is a copy of the callee's body, and so is every call of a struct method with `this`. `/std/http` `parseRequest` (a `StringView` in, a `Request` out) adds about 0.9 MB of C++ per call site under `--emit=optcpp`. A matrix fixture with 22 call sites generated 23 MB and did not compile within 5 minutes, and even a `bool`-returning helper with `string`/`i32` parameters around it was copied at each site. Pass small read-only structs (views, carriers) by value or by address in a real call, at least for the C++ emitters.
  - acceptance:
    - a program that calls `parseRequest` from 10 sites generates less than twice the C++ of a program that calls it from one, and gives the same results on VM and optexe
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: call lowering for struct parameters and results; no change to the inliner heuristics for scalars.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: Plain struct parameters and results are real calls on the VM and the optimizing C++ emitters (optcpp/optexe validate as OptCpp, which now takes the VM path: heap copy of the result, call site copies it into its frame). parseRequest under --emit=optcpp: 1 call site 889,100 B, 10 sites 1,027,656 B (1.16x; before 891,390 B and 8,771,206 B), same results on VM and optexe. Full gate 1887/1888 green; the one failure was the todo-reference audit, fixed by dropping ticket citations from comments.

- [x] TODO-5698: Serve static files with traversal-safe paths
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-06
  - phase: Web server (built with `--emit=optexe`)
  - parallel_track: web-static-files
  - scope: A handler that answers `GET /static/<path>` from a directory. Two parts are missing. (1) A run-time file read: `File<Read>` opens a module-table `string` path and reads one byte at a time, so a request path (a `String`/`StringView`) cannot name a file, and there is no `readAll`. (2) A path check: reject `..` segments, an absolute path, a NUL byte and a backslash before joining the request path to the root. The check is plain text work and can be written first (`safe_relative_path(StringView) -> bool`, tested on VM). Native file I/O still has TODO-5514 open (`writeLine` newline, errno), which does not affect `optexe` reads but should be fixed before documenting `--emit=native` servers.
  - options for (1): a. host calls `ps_os_read_file([String] path) -> String` and `ps_os_file_size` in the `/std/os` shim (small, works on optexe only, no change to `File`); b. teach `File<Read>` to open a `String` path and add `readAll` in `/std/file` (all backends, but touches the Result/File lowering and TODO-5514 first); c. open and `sendfile` in the net shim (zero copy, but couples `/std/net` to the file system). Recommendation: a for the first slice.
  - acceptance:
    - `safe_relative_path` with tests for `..`, absolute, NUL, backslash, empty and nested paths; `serve_static(root)` handler with content types for html, css, js, json, png and text, 404 for a missing file and 403 for an unsafe path; a curl-style loopback test
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the static handler and the file read it needs; no directory listings, ranges or caching.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: safe_relative_path, content_type_for and serve_file in /std/http/files over new /std/os fileSize and readFile host calls (option a: no change to File); web_app serves /static/*; real-client test covers 200, 404 and 403. Gate 1887/1888: PrimeStruct_net timed out under load (suite grew to four compiles, 138 s alone), passed alone, ctest timeout raised to 900 s.

- [x] TODO-5679: Stdlib structs can be move-only
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-06
  - phase: Web server (built with `--emit=optexe`)
  - parallel_track: lifecycle-move-only
  - scope: Owning handles such as a listening socket and a connection must not be copied (a copy would close the descriptor twice). `File<Mode>` gets this from builtin code (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md: `Clone` is a compile error); a struct written in the stdlib has no way to say it. Specify a spelling in docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, reject copies at compile time, and keep moves, `[T move]` parameters and returns working. Interim for a first network module: a `Copy` helper that panics. Deferred: needs the spelling decided.
  - acceptance:
    - a move-only struct moves into a parameter, a field and a return; copying it is a compile error with a clear diagnostic; spec text with a runnable example
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the move-only marker and its checks; `File` keeps its builtin rule unless switching it is free.
  - options (written down for the decision; nothing is implemented):
    1. A `[move_only]` struct transform. Explicit and greppable; a copy of a binding of that type (a by-value pass without `move(...)`, `[T copy]` parameters, assignment from a place, a field initializer from a local) is `move-only type '<T>' cannot be copied`. Needs a new transform name and a copy-site audit in semantics (the use-after-move analysis already tracks the sites).
    2. Implicit rule: a struct that declares `Destroy` and no `Copy` is move-only. No new spelling, and it matches how `File<Mode>` already behaves, but it changes existing stdlib types that declare only `Destroy` and are bit-copied today (an audit of those is part of the cost), and a type cannot say "trivially copyable despite Destroy".
    3. An explicit deleted copy: `Copy(other) = delete` or a `[no_copy]` marker on the helper. Closest to C++, needs parser support for the deleted body.
    Recommendation: option 1 for the first network module, with the interim panicking `Copy` helper until it lands.
    Decision (2026-11-06, implemented): option 2 with user-code scope. A struct that defines `Destroy` and no `Copy` is move-only; the check skips the stdlib and template instances because containers copy elements of any type bitwise. Option 1 would need ~15 transform-registry edits and the same copy-site checks.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: Move-only structs (Destroy, no Copy): copying a place into a binding, an assignment or a copy parameter is a compile error naming move(...); moves, temporaries, returns, move and borrowing parameters work. Stdlib and template instances are exempt. Spec in value-lifecycle.md, semantics test, gate 1888/1888 (an earlier run found generic-container breakage, which the exemption fixed).

- [x] TODO-5527: Stdlib Result locals destroy their payload once
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-10-06
  - phase: Correctness audit 2026-10
  - parallel_track: lifecycle-result-payloads
  - scope: Sum locals now destroy their active payload at scope end, except stdlib `Result` sums: `[Result<Noisy, i32>] r{...}` never destroys an ok `Noisy` (or a `Vector` payload's buffer), because `try(r)` and `[Noisy] v{try(r)}` copy the payload out without a copy helper, so destroying `r` too would destroy twice. Make `try` on a Result local copy (or move out and disarm) the payload, then register Result locals like other sums, and copy Result payloads taken from a place.
  - acceptance:
    - matrix cases with struct and `Vector` ok payloads read through `try`, `pick` and `Result.error` destroy each payload once on VM, native and C++
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: stdlib Result payload ownership only.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-06
  - result: Stdlib Result locals now register payload destruction like other sums, and try(<named Result>) is a copy of a place (Copy helper runs) instead of a bitwise temporary. Matrix cases with struct and vector-holding struct payloads through try, unread locals, Result.error and a failing try: each payload destroyed once on VM, native and C++. Limit found, not fixed: a Result local with a builtin vector<T> payload built from a call fails in lowering (stale semantic-product sum initializer type metadata) with or without this change; pick on Result was not exercised separately. Gate 1888/1888.

  - owner: ai

  - parallel_track: lifecycle-result-payloads
  - scope: `[Result<vector<i32>, i32>] r{make_vec()}` (a call returning `Result.ok(vector<i32>(...))`) fails in IR lowering with `stale semantic-product sum initializer type metadata: /std/result/Result__arity2__...` (`src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpers.cpp`, the initializer struct-path resolution). `try(make_vec())` on the temporary works (tests/unit/compile_run/vm/test_compile_run_vm_core_results_structs.cpp). The payload lifecycle itself (TODO-5527) is done for struct payloads, including structs that hold vectors.

The sum payload storage resolves vector<T> to the specialized vector record, so the local is an aggregate sum payload; try picks the sum path (it matched only packed collections before) and the stdlib Vector Copy/Destroy helpers run. Matrix case "a Result local with a vector payload copies on try and frees once" passes on vm, native, optexe and exe. Full release gate green (1888 tests).

- [x] TODO-5514: Native file I/O writes newlines and reports errno
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-10-06
  - phase: Correctness audit 2026-10
  - parallel_track: native-io
  - scope: Native `writeLine` never writes the newline (`emitFileWriteNewline` pops the fd into a register that `emitWriteNewlineReg` overwrites with `'\n'`, so it writes to fd 10); native file open/read errors always report 1 (EPERM) instead of the syscall errno (`emitFileOpenPlaceholder`, `emitFileOpenDynamicPlaceholder`, `emitFileReadByte` in `src/native_emitter/NativeEmitterInternalsX64Io.h`). Also `png.read` of a missing file returns `image_read_unsupported` on VM/C++ because ENOENT (2) collides with readImpl's status 2.
  - acceptance:
    - native writeLine and missing-file errors match VM and C++ (matrix cases); missing PNG reports `image_invalid_operation` everywhere
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: native I/O emitter and png status mapping.
  - notes: deferred: queued behind the Ready Now cap.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: Verified on macOS arm64 (the only hardware here): the arm64 native emitter ignored Darwin carry-flag errors, so open of a missing file succeeded with fd 2 and read errors reported 1; open/read now branch on carry and report the errno (matrix case checks status 2 on vm, native, optexe, exe). png.read of a missing file now reports image_invalid_operation everywhere (readImpl returns 1000000 for unsupported instead of colliding with errno 2; image.psmeta regenerated). The x86-64 emitter fixes (writeLine newline clobbering the fd in rbx, errno from -rax on open/read) are applied but NOT run: no Linux x86-64 hardware here; arm64 writeLine already wrote the newline. Full release gate green (1888 tests).

  - owner: ai

  - scope: `Function<R, A...>` is built only by explicit brace construction from a closure or a struct with `call` (`docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md`, Function values). Add `Function<i32, i32>{/double}` for a named definition (a generated closure struct whose `call` forwards to it), and accept a closure where a `Function` parameter or field is expected by inserting the conversion.

Function<i32, i32>{/double} and bare handler names go through a generated forwarder /double__fnref (SemanticsValidateFunctionForwarders.cpp). The function-values pass reports arity and certain type mismatches ("Function<i32, i32> cannot hold /add in /main: it takes 2 arguments, the Function passes 1"); a ref lambda or borrowing closure at an implicit site gives "closure escapes via Function". Not covered: generic parameters and fields, vector<Function> push, move(name) (TODO-5715). Matrix cases for a router of named handlers and a closure passed to a [Function<i32, i32> mut] parameter pass on VM, native and optexe; five semantics cases; web_app, http_hello, CodeExamples.md and the /std/http reference use the short form. Follow-ups filed: TODO-5712..5715. Full release gate green (1888 tests).

Convert a `string` literal (and a `string` value) to a `StringView` implicitly where a `[StringView]` parameter, field or binding is expected, as a `String` already converts with an implicit borrow (docs/spec/strings-and-views.md, Making one). Then drop the `textOf` calls from `/std/http`, `/std/json`, examples/apps/http_hello, examples/apps/web_app and docs/CodeExamples.md.
  - acceptance:
    - `response.text("hello", "text/plain")` and `request.header("Host")` compile and run on VM and optexe
    - spec text in docs/spec/strings-and-views.md with a runnable example; a negative test for a non-text argument

Template monomorphization inserts the call once the declared type is known; the site discovery of TODO-5669 moved into src/semantics/TemplateMonomorphExpectedTypeSites.cpp, shared by the Function and the StringView conversion (TemplateMonomorphTextViewConversions.cpp). A user type named StringView and an owned String do not convert. At same-arity overloads the literal converts only when every overload takes a StringView there, so `describe([string])` still wins over `describe([StringView])`; the rest is TODO-5716. `/std/http`, `/std/json`, http_hello, web_app, CodeExamples.md and the stdlib reference drop `textOf` around literals. - [x] TODO-5701: `/std/html` writer, `Response.html` and `redirect`
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: web-html
  - scope: Add `/std/html`, a `Writer` in the style of `/std/json` (`stdlib/std/json/json.prime`): it builds a `String`, escapes text (`& < > "`) and attribute values by default, and has `raw(text)` as the only unescaped call. Cover elements with attributes, void elements, text, the document head (`<!doctype html>`, title, a stylesheet link) and the form vocabulary (`form`, `input`, `button`, `label`, `select`, `textarea`). Add `Response.html(writer or String)` (sets `text/html; charset=utf-8`) and `Response.redirect(status, location)` (303 by default for post/redirect/get) to `/std/http`. Document both in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md.
  - acceptance:
    - a VM test writes a page with a form and checks the exact bytes, including escaped text and attributes
    - the same program gives the same bytes through optexe
    - a redirect response serializes with `Location` and no body
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the writer and the two Response helpers; no templates, no DOM model.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: `/std/html` (stdlib/std/html/html.prime) is a `Writer` that builds a page in its public `out` String. Text and attribute values are escaped (`& < > "`, and `'` in attribute values), element and attribute names keep only name bytes, and `raw` is the one unescaped call. A start tag stays open after `beginElement`, `voidElement`, `input`, `beginForm`, `beginSelect` and `stylesheet`, so `attribute`/`booleanAttribute` calls follow the element they belong to and the next other call writes the `>`; the spec says why this beats an attribute list or an `endAttributes()` call. It covers `element`, `text`, `beginPage`/`beginBody`/`endPage` (doctype, meta charset, title), `stylesheet`, and `beginForm`, `label`, `input`, `button`, `beginSelect`/`option`, `textarea`. `Response.html(String)` sets the body and `text/html; charset=utf-8` (a String, so /std/http does not depend on /std/html); `redirect(location)` is a 303 and `redirect(status, location)` takes another status, both with `Location` and an empty body; `reasonPhrase` knows 303, 307 and 308. The redirect overloads needed struct methods to overload by arity: a body method now counts its implicit `this` like a method call counts its receiver (TemplateMonomorphSourceDefinitionSetup.cpp), a method call bound to one overload resolves by its path, and the StringView conversion finds overloaded method callees; documented under Helper overloading in docs/spec/language-core.md. Tests: tests/fixtures/http/html_page.prime and two redirect cases in response_bytes.prime on VM and optexe, a program-matrix case for arity-overloaded methods and two semantics cases. Importing /std/html and /std/json together is a name conflict (TODO-5717). Full release gate green.

- [x] TODO-5702: Request query and form parameters with percent-decoding
  - owner: ai
  - status: done

  - phase: Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: web-params
  - scope: Add `Request.queryParam(name)` and `Request.formParam(name)` (`application/x-www-form-urlencoded` bodies) to `/std/http`, with `+` and `%XX` decoding. The value is a `StringView` into the request when nothing needs decoding and an owned `String` otherwise; pick one return type and document it (a `String` always is acceptable if the view case is not worth the API). Malformed escapes and non-UTF-8 results are an empty or absent value, never a fault. Replace `query_value` in examples/apps/web_app/main.prime with it.

    - VM and optexe tests for plain, `+`, `%20`, multi-byte UTF-8, repeated, missing and malformed parameters
    - web_app's `/hello?name=Ada%20L` greets `Ada L` in its loopback test
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: query and urlencoded form bodies only; no multipart.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: `Request.queryParam(name)` and `formParam(name)` return the decoded value of the first parameter whose decoded name is `name` as an owned `String` (`+` is a space, `%XX` the byte), always a String: one return type is simpler than a view-or-copy and parameter values are small. A missing parameter, one without a value, a malformed escape and bytes that are not UTF-8 all give an empty String; `hasQueryParam`/`hasFormParam` tell missing from empty, like `header`/`hasHeader`. `formParam` reads the body only when `isFormBody()`: `Content-Type` is `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` (ASCII case-insensitive, parameters such as `; charset=` ignored). web_app uses `queryParam` and its loopback test greets `Ada L` for `?name=Ada%20L`. tests/fixtures/http/query_params.prime (23 cases) runs on the VM and through optexe. Documented in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md. Full release gate green (1890 tests).

- [x] TODO-5703: `Router` path parameters
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: web-routes
  - scope: `Router` patterns are an exact path or a prefix ending in `*`. Add named segments (`/todos/:id`) and `request.pathParam(name)` (a `StringView` into the path). The first matching route still wins; 405 versus 404 keeps its meaning. Document in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md.
  - acceptance:
    - VM and optexe tests: `/todos/:id` matches `/todos/7` and not `/todos` or `/todos/7/x`; two parameters; a literal route listed first wins
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: segment parameters only; no regex or typed parameters.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: A `Router` pattern that does not end in `*` is now matched segment by segment, and a `:name` segment matches any one non-empty segment: `/todos/:id` matches `/todos/7` but not `/todos`, `/todos/` or `/todos/7/x`. Patterns without `:` still compare as one text. `request.pathParam(name)` is a `StringView` into the path as sent. Captured segments are not stored: `Request` gained one `route` field, the matched pattern (a view into the string table), which `Router.call` sets on its copy of the request before calling the handler, and `pathParam` walks the pattern and the path again. That keeps `Request` a carrier of views with no parameter limit; the spec explains the choice. The first matching route still wins and 405 versus 404 is unchanged. tests/fixtures/http/router_params.prime (two parameters, a literal route listed first, 405, empty segments, unrouted requests) runs on the VM and through optexe; the PrimeStruct_http timeout went from 600 to 900 s, since the suite took 390 s. Decoded path parameters and every value of a repeated parameter are TODO-5718. Full release gate green (1890 tests).

- [x] TODO-5704: A todo-list web app with pages and forms
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: web-example
  - depends_on: TODO-5702, TODO-5703
  - scope: Add examples/apps/todo_web: a single-threaded optexe server that lists todos as an HTML page, adds one through a form POST, toggles and deletes through button forms, and redirects back after each POST. App state is a struct passed to the handlers as a parameter (docs/WebAppsPlan.md, principle 2), not captured by reference. Test it end to end over loopback like the http_hello test in tests/unit/backend_host/test_net.cpp.
  - acceptance:
    - the loopback test adds two todos, toggles one, deletes one and checks the page HTML after each step
    - the example is the reference in docs/CodeExamples.md for a web page with buttons
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the example, its test and its CodeExamples entry; library gaps found on the way become their own TODOs.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: examples/apps/todo_web/main.prime is a single-threaded optexe todo app: GET / writes the page with the /std/html Writer (inline CSS, an add form, a Done/Undo and a Delete button form per todo), POST /todos adds the decoded `title`, POST /todos/:id/toggle and /todos/:id/delete act on a todo by its stable id, and every POST answers 303 to /. State reaches handlers as a parameter through the new `/std/http` `AppRouter<State>`: it holds the state in a field and calls each `Function<void, Reference<State>, Request, Reference<Response>>` handler with a reference to it; routes, patterns, 404/405 and `call` match `Router`, which is unchanged, so `serve` and middleware take it as is. A stored handler cannot hold a `[ref]` capture and a value capture would copy the state per route, hence the parameter. Implicit lambda and named-definition conversion works at methods of a generic struct, so routes need no explicit `Function{...}`. Two compiler fixes were needed: the IR lowerer resolves a struct named bare in a generic struct of another namespace (`AppRouter<Counter>` from the root) through enclosing namespaces, and the validator no longer checks a user `values[i]` against an `at` specialization made for another element type (any program importing /std/http failed to index a vector). Tests: tests/fixtures/http/app_router.prime on the VM and optexe (state shared across routes, a named handler, path parameters, 404/405), the loopback test "the todo_web example adds, toggles and deletes todos through forms over loopback" (checks the page HTML after two adds, a toggle and a delete, plus no-op posts), and resolver unit checks. docs: /std/http spec section, value-lifecycle limits, CodeExamples "A Web Page With Buttons", WebAppsPlan. Gaps filed: TODO-5719 (`location(this.field)` as a Function call argument), TODO-5720 (a binding from a vector element bit-copies owned fields: double free), TODO-5721 (string to StringView at a generic struct field). Full release gate green (1890 tests).

- [x] TODO-5720: A binding from a vector element copies the element's owned fields
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Collections (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Copies; found by TODO-5704)
  - parallel_track: collections-fields
  - scope: `[Todo] todo{todos[0i32]}`, where `Todo` has a `String` field and no `Copy` of its own, bit-copies the element and destroys the copy at scope end, so a later `remove_at` or the vector's own destruction stops with `string heap block freed twice` on the VM and optexe. A binding from a local Todo copies correctly, as do `[String] title{todos[0i32].title}` and passing `todos[0i32]` to a `[Todo]` parameter. The binding branch treats the builtin element access as an owned temporary (`shouldDisarmStructCopySourceExpr` in src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) and skips the field-wise Copy. The todo example passes the element to a function instead.
  - acceptance:
    - a binding from a vector element of a struct with String and vector fields copies and frees once on the VM and optexe, checked by mutating the copy and the element apart
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: bindings from builtin element access only.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: Root cause: `shouldDisarmStructCopySourceExpr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) called every element access a temporary since aef440df9, so a binding from `v[i]` bit-copied the element, skipped the field Copy helpers and destroyed the copy at scope end. An element access (builtin `at`/`at_unsafe`, `v[i]`, `v.at(i)`) is now a place: no disarm, the Copy helpers run. A call shaped like one that resolves to a definition (a user `at`) stays a temporary (`isTemporaryStructCopySourceExpr`, used by bindings, assignments, returns, brace fields and sum payloads). Also fixed: a struct of one scalar kind (`Point{x, y}` i32s) lives as an array local, and a binding from an element or a local aliased it; it now gets its own slots. Matrix suite primestruct.program_matrix.element_copies (7 cases): bindings from `v[i]`, `v.at(i)` and a field of an element with String, vector and Copy/Destroy fields, a brace field, an assignment and a return from an element, moves and temporaries not copied, a user `at`, on the VM and optexe; a Function-holding element (the copy clones the closure) and the scalar struct also on native. Each Noisy destroyed once. Spec Copies paragraph names elements. TODO-5681 is a different root cause (a vector record copied from a named local) and stays open; filed TODO-5726. The todo_web example keeps `write_todo(page, list.todos[index])`, which reads better than a binding. Full release gate 1891/1891.

- [x] TODO-5705: An idle connection does not stall the server
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: web-server-loop
  - scope: `serveUntil` reads one connection to completion before accepting the next, with `readTimeoutMs` (5 s) per request. Browsers open spare connections and send nothing on them, so one idle connection should delay every other client by up to the read timeout. Confirm this with a loopback test first (open a connection and send nothing, then time a second client's GET). If confirmed, wait on the listener and every open connection together through one new host call (`poll` in stdlib/std/net/native/net_shim.cpp), keep a small fixed table of connections with their buffers, and serve whichever is ready. Still one thread.
  - acceptance:
    - the loopback test serves the second client in under 100 ms while the first stays idle, on optexe
    - existing http_hello, web_app and server tests pass unchanged
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: readiness-driven accept and read in the existing loop; no threads, no keep-alive.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: Confirmed first: with tests/fixtures/http/http_idle_server.prime (read timeout 1500 ms) built on the old loop, a GET sent 50 ms after a silent connection took 1444-1451 ms in three tries and the silent connection was then dropped unanswered; on the new loop the same GET takes 1-2 ms and the silent connection is still answered when it sends its request. The /std/net shim gains `net_poll(interest, length, timeoutMs)`, wrapped as `poll(interest, length, timeoutMs)`: `interest` is a byte buffer with one byte per descriptor (host calls pass only byte buffers; it takes a pointer like `recv` so /std/net still needs no collections import), watched bytes come back 2 when ready and 1 when not, and the result is the ready count, timeoutCode() or interruptedCode(), so a signal still reaches `stopRequested()`. `serveUntil` keeps a table of `maxConnections` slots (new `ServerConfig` field, default 16, appended last): it polls every open connection and, while a slot is free, the listener, up to the first deadline or `acceptTimeoutMs`; a ready connection is read with a zero timeout and its request parsed, handled, written and closed; a slot past `readTimeoutMs` is closed unanswered. A full table stops watching the listener, so new connections wait in the backlog (no 503). Rejected requests are answered as before and drained inside their slot (50 ms pauses, at most 8 rounds) instead of blocking the loop. Writes still block for at most `writeTimeoutMs`, as the spec now says. Each slot's `maxRequestBytes` buffer is a region of one `vector<u8>` grown the first time the slot is used (at most 1 MiB with the defaults). `serve`, `serveUntil`, the handler signature, the rejection statuses and `announce` are unchanged, and the existing http_server, http_hello, web_app and todo_web loopback tests pass untouched. New test: "an idle connection does not delay other clients over loopback" (three GETs beside a silent connection, fastest under 100 ms; a request sent in two parts beside a fast client; a full two-slot table that queues a client until a read timeout frees a slot). docs: /std/net `poll` and the /std/http/server loop, rejected requests, memory and `maxConnections` in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md; docs/WebAppsPlan.md. Gaps filed: TODO-5728 (`slice`/`sliceMut` on a vector field of a struct do not resolve), TODO-5729 (the ctest PrimeStruct_net nears or passes its 900 s timeout under load; todo_web's compile took the same clang++ time, 79 s CPU, before and after this change). Full release gate green (1891/1891); an earlier run lost program_matrix_closures to a 300 s timeout under load, which passed alone in 67 s.
