Gate: 1889 of 1894 passed; the five failures were timeouts under that load (PrimeStruct_net_todo_web, PrimeStruct_http, PrimeStruct_string_conformance_vm, reflection_codegen_23_23, vm_maps_map_helpers), and all of them, with the new http entries and the registration and inventory checks, passed in a rerun (todo_web 235 s, http 58 s, http_optexe 99 s, http_loopback 132 s).

- [x] TODO-5723: Shared UI event structs for web and native handlers
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Native UI handlers (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: ui-events
  - scope: Add `/std/ui/events` (`stdlib/std/ui/events/events.prime`) with the event values every control handler receives (docs/WebAppsPlan.md, principle 7): `PointerEvent` (`button` as a `PointerButton` enum: none, primary, secondary, middle; control-local `x`, `y`; `modifiers`; `clickCount`), `KeyEvent` (`key` as a `Key` value: named keys and characters; `modifiers`; `repeat`), `ChangeEvent` for check boxes, popups and lists (the new value or index), and `Modifiers` (shift, control, alt, meta). The retired `/std/ui` prototype's `UiEventStream` records (stdlib/std/ui/ui.prime) had the same fields and modifier bits; reuse their meaning. Pure data plus small helpers (`modifiers.shift()`), no host calls, so it runs on every backend. Document in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md.
  - acceptance:
    - a VM and optexe test builds each event, reads every field and compares enum values
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the event types only; no dispatch.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: Added `/std/ui/events` (stdlib/std/ui/events/events.prime), pure data with small helpers and no host calls. `PointerButton` is an `[enum generate(Equal, NotEqual)]` (none -1, primary 0, secondary 1, middle 2, the retired `/std/ui` record values): an enum rather than a sum because the alternatives carry no payload and an entry reads as a value in a comparison (`event.button == PointerButton.secondary`) where a sum would need `pick`. `Modifiers` keeps the prototype's bits (1 shift, 2 control, 4 alt, 8 meta) in `bits`, named `Modifiers.shiftBit` and so on, with `shift()`, `control()`, `alt()`, `meta()` and `any()`; the language has no bitwise operators, so a bit is tested by division and a set is built by adding bits. `Key` is a struct with `code`: a Unicode code point for a character (`characterKey(97i32)`), or -1..-13 for the named keys `Key.enter`, `tab`, `escape`, `backspace`, `delete`, the arrows, `home`, `end`, `pageUp`, `pageDown`; `isCharacter()`, `character()`. `PointerEvent` has `button`, control-local `x`/`y`, `hasPosition` (false for a web form post), `modifiers` and `clickCount`; `KeyEvent` has `key`, `modifiers` and `isRepeat` (`repeat` is a loop keyword and does not parse as a field name); `ChangeEvent` has `checked` and `index`. One compiler change: `!=` on a struct with a generated `NotEqual` now routes through `/Type/NotEqual`, as `==` already did through `Equal` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprNumericPredicates.cpp, SemanticsValidatorExprCallResolution.cpp; a semantics case checks the resolved target and that `Equal` alone still rejects `!=`), so handlers write `event.button != PointerButton.none`. tests/fixtures/ui/events.prime builds every event, reads every field and compares the enum, keys and modifiers; it runs on the VM, the native backend and optexe (ctest PrimeStruct_ui_events). Spec: docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md "UI events", the enum note in type-system.md and the `!=` routing in transforms.md. Found on the way: a `vector<String>` field of a struct faults when an element is read (TODO-5730). Full release gate green (1897/1897).

- [x] TODO-5722: Web controls take click closures
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: web-controls
  - scope: A button on a web page should take its click handler directly, a closure that may have its own `mut` state (docs/WebAppsPlan.md, principle 7), instead of being joined to a hand-routed POST path by a string. Add a page layer over `AppRouter<State>` (`stdlib/std/http/http.prime`): controls are registered once at setup, `app.button(id, title, handler)` with `handler` a `Function<void, Reference<State>, PointerEvent>`, each under a generated route (for example `POST /_on/<id>/click`); rendering writes the control as a form posting there (post/redirect/get), and the router calls the stored closure with the state and a `PointerEvent` (primary button, no position, for a plain form POST). Registration at setup is required: a closure made while rendering would lose its `mut` captures every request. Rework examples/apps/todo_web or add examples/apps/hello_button (text on a page, a button that adds a line per click) to use it, with a loopback test, and add the CodeExamples entry.
  - acceptance:
    - a closure with a `[mut said{0i32}]` capture counts clicks across requests in a loopback test; the page HTML after each click is checked
    - VM and optexe tests for registration, the generated routes and a duplicate id
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: click handlers over form POSTs; hover and key events are TODO-5724.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: Added `/std/web` (stdlib/std/web/web.prime) with `WebApp<State>`, chosen over more methods on `AppRouter` so that `/std/http` still depends on neither `/std/html` nor `/std/ui/events` (its `Response.html` takes a `String` for that reason) and the page layer, where routes, HTML and events meet, has one place to grow (TODO-5706, TODO-5724). `WebApp` routes exactly as `AppRouter` (same `state` field and construction, `get`/`post`/`put`/`delete`/`route`, patterns, 404/405, `call`); `/std/http` gained a public `routeMatches(pattern, path)` so it matches the same way. `app.button(id, title, handler)`, with `handler` a `Function<void, Reference<State>, PointerEvent>`, registers the button at setup and returns a `Control` (`id` and `title` as `string`s, so it owns and borrows nothing). A page gets the control from registration, by a value capture of the page lambda (`[hello](...)`) or a parameter, and writes it with `hello.write(page)` as `<form method="post" action="/_on/<id>/click"><button type="submit">title</button></form>`; so a page can only write a control that has a handler. `call` sends `/_on/` paths to the controls: a POST to a control's route calls its closure with the state and a `PointerEvent` (primary button, clickCount 1, no position or modifiers) and answers 303 to `backPath(request)`, the path and query of the `Referer` or `/` (only the path is kept, so the redirect stays on the server); another method is 405, an unknown id 404. A duplicate id prints `duplicate control id: <id>` and panics at setup: a Result would put a `?` after every registration. examples/apps/hello_button/main.prime: text on a page and a "Say hello" button whose closure keeps `[mut said{0i32}]` and adds a line per click; the loopback test "the hello_button example adds a line per click through its button closure over loopback" (ctest PrimeStruct_net_hello_button) checks the page after each of three clicks, the 303, a GET of the route (405) and an unknown id (404). tests/fixtures/http/web_controls.prime (registration, routes, a capturing closure and a named handler, the event fields, Referer paths including off-site ones, 405/404, the HTML of two controls) and web_duplicate_control.prime run on the VM and through optexe (ctest PrimeStruct_web). docs: /std/web in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md, CodeExamples "A Button That Takes Its Click Handler", WebAppsPlan. todo_web keeps its per-todo forms: a control is one button with one handler, and its Done/Delete buttons name a todo by id in the route. Found: reading a `string` field of an element of `vector<ClickHandler<State>>` whose `Function` holds a capturing closure faults on the VM (TODO-5731), so `ClickHandler` keeps its id as a StringView. Release gate: 1897 of 1899 passed; program_matrix_closures timed out at 300 s under load (96 s alone) and PrimeStruct_spec_docs failed on a `[hello](...)` code span it read as a link, since reworded; both passed in a rerun.

- [x] TODO-5706: Fragment-swapping buttons without user JavaScript
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: web-fragments
  - scope: Add the fragment layer of docs/WebAppsPlan.md (principle 4): `/std/html` attributes that name a route and a target element, one small fixed script served by a stdlib route, and responses that are HTML fragments. A button click calls a PrimeStruct handler and replaces part of the page. Without the script the same forms still work by post/redirect/get.
  - acceptance:
    - the todo example toggles an item through a fragment; a test checks the fragment bytes and that the plain form path still works
    - the script is a fixed asset under stdlib/ with a size budget stated in the spec
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the attributes, the script and one example use; no client-side state or templating.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: `/std/web` regions answer the fragment script's requests. `app.region(id, render)` registers a `Function<void, Reference<Writer>, Reference<State>>` that writes the contents of the element with that id; the page writes the element and calls the same function inside it, so one function writes the region in the page and as a fragment (a duplicate id panics, as for buttons). A form names the region with the new `/std/html` `Writer.fragmentTarget(id)` (`data-ps-target="id"` on the form), or a control with `Control.swapping(id)` (a new `target` string field). The fixed script stdlib/std/web/app.js (1543 bytes; budget 5120 bytes, unminified with comments, chosen so TODO-5724's events fit too) is carried as a raw literal in stdlib/std/web/fragment_script.prime and served at `GET /_web/app.js` (`/_web/` paths belong to the app: other method 405, other path 404); `writeFragmentScript(page)` and `/std/html` `Writer.script(src)` write the tag. The script intercepts the submit of a POST form with `data-ps-target`, posts its fields with `fetch` (urlencoded, `redirect: "manual"`) and the header `X-PrimeStruct-Fragment: <id>`, one request at a time, and replaces the element's `innerHTML` when the answer carries the same header; any other answer reloads the page. On the server, `WebApp.call` runs the route or control as before and then `answerFragment`: a 303 that answers a request naming a registered region becomes a 200 with the region's HTML and the header. The region id travels in the header rather than in a route-to-region table so that ordinary routes (todo_web's per-todo `/todos/:id/toggle`) answer fragments with no change; handlers stay post/redirect/get, so the plain form path and the fragment path share one handler and one render function. examples/apps/todo_web moved to `WebApp<TodoList>` with the region "todos" (`<ul id="todos">`, written by `write_todos`); its Done and Delete forms swap the list. Tests: tests/fixtures/http/web_fragments.prime on the VM and optexe (a browser's fetch request as bytes, the fragment bytes, the plain 303, an unknown region, an ordinary POST route, a GET with the header, the script route) and web_duplicate_region.prime on the VM (ctest PrimeStruct_web); a test that app.js is within budget and that the literal holds its bytes; the todo_web loopback test (PrimeStruct_net_todo_web) checks the page's script tag and targets, that the served script is app.js, the exact fragment answer of a toggle, the list after it, an unknown region's 303 and a fragment delete. Found TODO-5732: `response.clear()` on a reference parameter does not resolve in a generic struct's method, so `answerFragment` resets the status and header lines by hand. Release gate: 1899 of 1899 passed.

  - owner: ai

These events have no HTML-only form, so the fragment script of TODO-5706 sends them with the event's fields and swaps the returned fragment.   - finished_at: 2026-11-07
  - result: `/std/web` controls take hover, press, release and key handlers. `app.onMouseOver(control, handler)`, `onMouseDown` and `onMouseUp` take a `Function<void, Reference<State>, PointerEvent>`, `onKeyDown` and `onKeyUp` a `Function<void, Reference<State>, KeyEvent>`; `control` is the `[Control mut]` that `button` returned, whose new `events` bits (`Control.mouseOverEvent` 1 to `keyUpEvent` 16) each registration sets, so `Control.write` puts `data-ps-id`, `data-ps-on="mouseover ..."` and, with key handlers, `tabindex="0"` on its button (it now writes the button itself rather than through `Writer.button`). A duplicate handler prints `duplicate handler: <id> <event>` and a control of another app `unknown control: <id>`; both panic at setup. `callControl` now parses `/_on/<id>/<event>`, finds the handler (`handlerIndex`), decodes the urlencoded body with the new public `pointerEventOf(request)` (`button` -1..2, `x`, `y`, `hasPosition` when `x` is present, `modifiers`) or `keyEventOf(request)` (`key` code, `modifiers`, `repeat`), calls it and answers 303, which `answerFragment` turns into the region's fragment when the request names one. The fixed script (stdlib/std/web/app.js, now 4914 of its 5120 bytes) listens on the document: a pointer must rest on a control 100 ms before one mouseover is sent (leaving sooner cancels it), presses and releases map the DOM button to `PointerButton` and send the control-local position, key events go to `document.activeElement` only and map `Enter` .. `PageDown` to the named `Key` codes and single characters to their code point (other keys send nothing), a press focuses a control with key handlers, and a swap gives focus back to the control with the same `data-ps-id`. Click positions are not sent (stop_rule: the listed events). Tests: tests/fixtures/http/web_events.prime on the VM and optexe drives each event kind with the bytes the script's fetch sends and checks the decoded `PointerEvent`/`KeyEvent` and the fragment answer, plus a redirect without the header, 405, 404 for an unknown event, a control without that handler and a path without an event, and the HTML of controls with and without events; web_duplicate_handler.prime on the VM; a test that app.js writes the route, field names, button mapping, named key codes, debounce and focused-only key handling the app decodes; the budget test still passes. Spec: /std/web "Hover, press, release and key handlers" in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md (request table, round-trip cost, CSS for visual hover, focus); WebAppsPlan. Found TODO-5733: an `i32` field of the second element of `vector<PointerHandler<State>>` faults on read, so the kinds live in parallel `vector<i32>` fields. Release gate: 1899 of 1899 passed; a rerun after a machine restart passed 1898, with PrimeStruct_native_ui_editor_acceptance (AppKit, untouched here) dying of SIGPIPE under load and passing alone in 207 s.

- [x] TODO-5709: Native controls take handler closures and `App.run`
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Native UI handlers (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: native-ui-handlers
  - scope: Native UI events reach code as integer command ids and widget handles compared in an if-chain (examples/apps/text_editor/main.prime, the `pick(app.waitEvent())` loop). Give the `/std/ui/native` controls handler closures over the events ABI version 0 already reports (docs/WebAppsPlan.md, principle 7): `Button.onClick`, `Menu.item(title, shortcut, handler)` and toolbar items (`PointerEvent`), `CheckBox.onChange`, `Popup.onChange`, `ItemList.onChange` (`ChangeEvent`), and window close and text change. Each handler is a `Function<void, Reference<State>, Event>`, so a closure may keep its own `mut` state while shared state comes in as the parameter. `App.run(state)` allocates command ids, keeps the handler table and dispatches until quit; app code never sees an id. `waitEvent` stays. Document in docs/NativeUiPlan.md and docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md.
  - acceptance:
    - examples/native_ui/hello_window.prime has a handler form with a counting `[mut]` closure on a button; it runs on the VM against the headless backend and as a compiled program with the same call log
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: handlers over the existing C ABI events; pointer and key events are TODO-5725.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-08
  - result: Added stdlib/std/ui/native/handlers.prime: an `AppHandlers<State>` holds the app's state (a `state` field, as `WebApp<State>` does) and the handler table, and `app.run(handlers)` loops on the version-0 events and calls each handler until the app quits; no C ABI change. The table lives in a generic struct beside `App` because `App`, `Button` and `Menu` are bare `u64` handles with nowhere to keep closures and there is no global state, so registration goes through the one object that knows `State`. Each handler is a `Function<void, Reference<State>, Event>` with a `/std/ui/events` struct, the web control's type. `handlers.command(handler)` returns a `Command` (ids from `first_command_id()`, 1000000, so they stay clear of hand-picked ids) that `Menu.commandItem(title, shortcut, command)`, `Toolbar.commandButton(title, symbol, command)` and `Menu.setCommandChecked(command, bool)` take, so a menu item and a toolbar button share one closure and its captures; the event is a primary-button `PointerEvent` with no position and `clickCount` 1. `onClick(button, handler)` gets the same event; `onCheckBoxChange`, `onPopupChange`, `onListChange` get a `ChangeEvent` read from the control (`checked`, the chosen index, the first selected row or -1). `onWindowClose` and `onTextChange` (app-wide, they get the `Window`), `onOpenFile` (the path) and `onQuit` (`Function<bool, Reference<State>>`, true lets `run` return) complete the events. Defaults: no close handler closes the window, `run` returns after a close request when no window is open and on a quit request no handler refuses, events without a handler are dropped. `waitEvent` and `AppEvent` are unchanged. The planned spellings `Menu.item(..., handler)` and an overloaded `onChange` did not work: same-arity overloads of struct methods do not resolve (receiver misaligned, struct locals and calls typed unknown, overloads in generic structs not found), which would also have broken the editor's `item(title, shortcut, command_save())`, so separate names are used (TODO-5735). Also found: `pick` and namespaced sum parameters in a generic struct's method do not lower, so `deliverNext` reads the ABI event kinds itself (TODO-5736); implicit template inference through `[Holder<S>]` fails, so `App.run` is `run<Handlers>([Handlers mut])` (TODO-5737); `clear()` on a handler-vector field of a generic struct does not resolve (note on TODO-5732). examples/native_ui/hello_button.prime moved to the handler form with a counting `[mut said{0i32}]` closure that retitles the button (hello_window kept as the `waitEvent` example its tests pin); tests/fixtures/ui/handlers.prime exercises every event kind, a shared menu/toolbar command, an unhandled command, a refused close and a refused quit. CTest PrimeStruct_cpp_native_ui runs both on the VM against the headless backend and as optexe programs with identical call logs and exit codes, and checks the logged titles; the fixture also gave the same log through `--emit=native` with the headless dylib (checked by hand). native_ui_headless_runner gained `--click HANDLE`. Docs: docs/NativeUiPlan.md section 5 (handlers table and rationale), docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md (Native UI, Handlers), docs/CodeExamples.md "A Native Button That Takes Its Click Handler", docs/WebAppsPlan.md. Unblocked TODO-5710 and TODO-5725. Release gate: 1899 of 1899 passed.

- [x] TODO-5734: Web apps build in seconds, not minutes, on optexe
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-08
  - phase: Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: ir-real-calls
  - scope: Nearly every call in a web app is still a copy of the callee's body. Measured 2026-11-08 with `primec --emit=optcpp` at 24b0650d0: examples/apps/todo_web generates 12.7 MB of C++ (509,680 lines), of which the entry function `ps_f0` is 507,773 lines and only about 41 functions exist at all; primec takes over 2 minutes and clang about 16 CPU minutes (the `--emit=optexe` build took 20 minutes wall). The `AppRouter` version of todo_web (983137899) generates 5.7 MB in 40 s, hello_button 6.6 MB in 83 s, web_app 2.3 MB and http_hello 1.8 MB. TODO-5695 made plain struct parameters and results real calls; struct methods with `this`, methods of generic structs (`WebApp<State>`, `AppRouter<State>`), closure `call` methods, the `Function` dispatch written by the `function-values` pass and stdlib helpers such as `String.append` or `Writer.element` evidently still inline at every call site. Find which call shapes still inline (an IR dump of todo_web listing inlined callees by count), make them real calls on the VM and the optimizing C++ emitters, and keep small leaf helpers inlinable where that is cheaper.
  - acceptance:
    - todo_web's generated C++ is under 1.5 MB and its `--emit=optexe` build takes under 2 minutes of CPU on this machine; hello_button and the other web examples shrink similarly; record before/after numbers in docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md
    - the web examples' loopback tests and the program matrix give the same results on VM and optexe
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: call lowering for the shapes found; no change to the language surface.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-08
  - result: Real calls on the VM and optcpp/optexe now use an address ABI: a local address numbers all live frames' locals (step and fast kernel, optcpp registers addressable frames), the call site binds parameters as an inlined call would and passes each parameter local (scalar or address; the receiver by address, mut or not), struct results (lifecycle structs too) return through a caller block, the first call site reserves any non-tiny definition with that shape, and runtime errors in called functions end the program with the new Trap opcode (PSIR v33). optcpp before/after: todo_web 12.7 MB/1.38 MB, hello_button 6.60/1.25 MB, web_app 2.27/0.92 MB, http_hello 1.83/0.75 MB; optexe build CPU todo_web about 16 min/7.3 s, hello_button 290 s/7.6 s, web_app 72/4.9 s, http_hello 42/4.9 s. VM benchmark run times unchanged or faster (call_fib 54.4/54.1 ms, json_parse 71.0/69.2 ms). Numbers in docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 9. Filed TODO-5738, TODO-5739, TODO-5740, TODO-5741.

- [x] TODO-5725: Native pointer and key events for control handlers
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-07
  - phase: Native UI handlers (docs/WebAppsPlan.md)
  - parallel_track: native-ui-events
  - scope: The C ABI (include/primec/ui/NativeUi.h, version 0) reports only menu commands, control changes, window close and text changes. Add hover enter and leave, mouse down and up with button, position and modifiers, and key down and up for the focused control, as a versioned ABI extension, in the AppKit and headless backends, and add `onMouseOver`, `onMouseDown`, `onMouseUp`, `onKeyDown`, `onKeyUp` to the `/std/ui/native` controls with `/std/ui/events` structs. Text views keep typing inside the widget; key handlers on them see only keys the widget does not consume.
  - acceptance:
    - headless scenarios deliver each event kind to a closure and check the struct fields; the compiled program gives the same call log
    - docs/NativeUiPlan.md section 4 and the coverage matrix describe the new events
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the listed events on the existing controls; no new widgets.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-08
  - result: The C ABI stays version 0; the events come behind a feature: `ps_ui_supports(PS_UI_FEATURE_INPUT_EVENTS)` and `ps_ui_widget_watch(widget, mask)` (mask bits 1 hover, 2 press, 4 release, 8 key down, 16 key up, the bits of a `/std/web` control's events) for a control or text view, then the kinds `pointer_enter` (7), `pointer_leave` (8), `mouse_down` (9), `mouse_up` (10), `key_down` (11), `key_up` (12) with accessors `ps_ui_event_button/x/y/modifiers/click_count/key/repeat` (button -1 none, 0 primary, 1 secondary, 2 middle; position from the widget's top left corner, a text view's visible part; key a code point or a named key below 0 as `/std/ui/events` `Key`, function keys not reported). A program that never watches gets exactly the version-0 events, and `App.waitEvent` and `deliverNext` (which treat an unknown kind as quit) are safe; the bindings bind 128 functions. Headless (src/ui/headless/NativeUiHeadlessInput.cpp): `pushPointerEnter/Leave`, `pushMouseDown/Up`, `pushKeyDown/Up`, scenario entries `enter`, `leave`, `down`, `up`, `keydown`, `keyup` (and `--enter` ... options in native_ui_headless_runner), delivered only when watched at replay time; a text view types a character, Enter or Tab without Control and Command, reports Escape and Command keys, keeps the rest, and reports a key up only after it reported the key down. AppKit: nothing at launch; `dispatchEvent` hands each pumped event to NSApp at once while nothing is watched; a hover watch adds an `NSTrackingArea` (owner `PSHoverOwner`) to the widget's view and removing the bit removes it; with any watch, presses are hit-tested to the innermost watched widget before AppKit sees them, and when a control's tracking loop takes the release the release is reported after the loop, ahead of the click; a key goes to the watched widget holding the first responder, and a text view's key reaches the program only when its delegate sees `noop:`, `cancelOperation:` or a command the view does not implement while the key is handed to it. `PRIMESTRUCT_UI_CLICK` gained `enter:H:X:Y`, `leave:H:X:Y`, `press:H:X:Y` (hit-tested, not handed to the control) and `key:H:K:M` (sent to a window that is not key, so nothing takes focus). `/std/ui/native`: `AppHandlers.onMouseOver`, `onMouseDown`, `onMouseUp`, `onKeyDown`, `onKeyUp(widget, handler)` take a `Widget` (`TextView.widget()` added) because same-arity overloads do not resolve (TODO-5735); registering watches the widget with the bits that have handlers, the last handler of an event wins, `deliverNext` dispatches kinds 7-12 (the pointer leaving has no handler). Tests: tests/fixtures/ui/input_handlers.prime runs on the VM and as optexe against the headless backend with the same call log and exit code 127 (PrimeStruct_cpp_native_ui), on AppKit compiled natively through the snapshot harness with exit code 127 (PrimeStruct_native_ui_compiled_app), and the headless ABI case in PrimeStruct_native_ui_headless checks fields, skipping and unwatching. Found: `==` on a generated-`Equal` struct or an enum does not resolve in a lambda body (TODO-5742); the fixture compares codes. Docs: docs/NativeUiPlan.md sections 4, 5, 9 and 12, docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md, docs/WebAppsPlan.md. Release gate: 1898 of 1899 passed; the one failure, PrimeStruct_primestruct_program_matrix_parameter_modes on native, comes with e52fdaa9d (TODO-5734) and is filed as TODO-5743.

- [x] TODO-5743: A runtime error in a native real call ends the program
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-08
  - phase: Optimizing backends (found while gating TODO-5725)
  - parallel_track: native-real-calls
  - scope: TODO-5734 added the `Trap` opcode so that a runtime error (an index out of bounds) inside a called function ends the program, but `emitRuntimeError` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererRuntimeErrorHelpers.cpp) emits it only under `RuntimeErrorsTrapScope(input.realCallAddressAbi)`, which is false for `--emit=native`, and the native emitters (arm64, x64) and the Linux JIT have no `Trap`. On native a real-call callee's runtime error prints its message and returns 3 to the caller, which carries on: the program of "a runtime error inside a real call stops the program" (tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_parameter_modes.cpp) prints `20 30`, the error, `3`, `99` and exits 0 instead of exiting 3 after the error. CTest PrimeStruct_primestruct_program_matrix_parameter_modes fails on native-O0 and native-O2 since e52fdaa9d (docs/failing_tests.md). Emit `Trap` for runtime errors in every real-call body on native and lower it in the arm64 and x64 emitters (exit with the code, as the entry's return does) and the JIT, or keep such bodies inline on targets without it.
  - acceptance:
    - the program matrix case passes on native-O0 and native-O2 (exit 3, stdout `20\n30\n`), on macOS arm64 and Linux x64
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: runtime errors in real calls on the native targets only.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-08
  - result: Root cause: `emitRuntimeError` traps only under `RuntimeErrorsTrapScope`, which the real-call body loop set from `realCallAddressAbi` (VM and optcpp only), while `--emit=native` still makes scalar definitions with two or more call sites real calls (TODO-4747), so a callee's bounds error pushed 3 and returned it to the caller. Native now runs `Trap`: the statements-calls stage takes a separate `runtimeErrorsTrap` (address ABI targets or `IrValidationTarget::Native`), IrValidation accepts `Trap` for Native, and the emitters lower it as the exit syscall from any frame: arm64 `mov x0, code; mov x16, SYS_exit; svc` (macOS), x64 `exit_group(code)` in both the template and the register-allocated paths (Linux). Output needs no flush (native prints are write syscalls). The Linux x86_64 JIT runs `Trap` inline as a new `JitFault::Trap` (10) whose argument is the exit code; `runNativeJit` reports it as a normal result, so `primevm` exits with it as the VM does (before, the JIT refused modules with `Trap` and they were interpreted). `Trap` is also a scheduling barrier in IrVirtualRegisterScheduler/Verifier. The matrix case passes on native-O0, native-O2, the VM and optexe on macOS arm64; the x64 and JIT changes are not compiled or run here (Linux only). No serialized IR change (the VM and serialized targets already had `Trap`, PSIR v33). Docs: docs/spec/vm-design.md (Errors, native execution tier) and docs/spec/runtime-model.md. TODO-5517 (division by zero, the C++ exit code) is a different fault path and stays open; the simple C++ `exe`, Wasm and GLSL targets still return 3 from a real-call callee (TODO-5744). The validation case "borrowed helper-return experimental wrapper bare conversion alias lowers through generic wildcard import" lowers for the default (native) target and now validates as Native, since its real-call body has a `Trap` the backend-neutral target rejects. Release gate: 1899 of 1899 passed.

- [x] TODO-5742: `==` on generated-`Equal` structs and enums resolves in lambda bodies
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-08
  - phase: Language surface for handlers (docs/WebAppsPlan.md; found by TODO-5725)
  - parallel_track: lambda-struct-compare
  - scope: docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md (/std/ui/events) says `event.key == Key.escape` and `event.button == PointerButton.secondary` compare through the generated `Equal`; they do in a free function (tests/fixtures/ui/events.prime), but the same comparison in a lambda body is `comparisons require numeric, bool, or string operands` (PSC1005) at `/main__lambda0/call`: `[Function<bool, KeyEvent>] check{[]([KeyEvent] event) { return(event.key == Key.escape) }}`. Handlers are lambdas, so this is where the comparison is written; tests/fixtures/ui/input_handlers.prime compares `event.key.code == Key.escape.code` and a `button_code` helper instead. Make the `==`/`!=` rewrite to the generated `Equal`/`NotEqual` (and the enum comparison) run for lambda bodies as for definitions.
  - acceptance:
    - a lambda compares a `Key` with `==` and a `PointerButton` with `!=` on the VM, native and optexe; tests/fixtures/ui/input_handlers.prime uses `event.key == Key.escape`
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: generated comparisons in lambda bodies only.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-08
  - result: Root cause: not lambdas as such but every struct method, and a lambda body is the `call` method of its closure struct. `resolveCalleePath` resolves the builtin `equal`/`not_equal` in a definition with a namespace prefix to the scoped path (`/main__lambda0/equal`, `/Holder/equal`), and `resolveExprConcreteCallPath` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprCallResolution.cpp) only tried the generated `Equal`/`NotEqual` helper for the root spellings `/equal` and `/not_equal`; the call then fell back to the numeric comparison check, PSC1005. It now also takes a non-method call that `getBuiltinComparisonName` names `equal`/`not_equal` when the scoped path names no definition, so a user definition named `equal` in that namespace still wins. Enums take the same path (an enum is a struct with generated helpers). Tests: semantics cases for a struct method (`/Holder/same` resolves to `/Pair/Equal`) and a lambda (`/Pair/NotEqual`), a lambda comparing a struct without `Equal` still fails with PSC1005, and a namespaced `equal` keeps its calls; the program-matrix case "a lambda compares generated-Equal structs and enums with operators" compares a `Key` with `==` and a `PointerButton` with `!=` in lambdas and a `Key` in a struct method on the VM, native and optexe. tests/fixtures/ui/input_handlers.prime now writes `event.key == Key.escape` and `event.button == PointerButton.primary` (`button_code` stays for the log line); no stdlib or example code compared `.code` values as a workaround (the `err.code == N` tests compare error codes). No doc change: docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md already describes the comparison. Release gate: 1899 of 1899 passed.

  - owner: ai

  - parallel_track: native-ui-handlers
  - scope: Move the text editor's menu, toolbar and settings-window dispatch from the if-chains on command ids and handles to the handlers of TODO-5709, one menu at a time.
  - acceptance:

A program uses either `run` or its own `waitEvent` loop, not both.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-08
  - result: The editor runs on `AppHandlers<Editor>`: every menu item and toolbar button is a `handlers.command` or one of a `handlers.commandRange` (new: a row of ids whose handler gets the offset, taken by `Toolbar.commandSegments`, `Menu.addRecentFileCommands` and used for scroll modes, view modes, format operations, headings, tables, alignment, Show Tab 1 to 9), the end of a script run is a command (`Window.runScriptCommand`), the settings window controls built on first use are found through the state (`onClickOf`, `onCheckBoxChangeOf`, `onPopupChangeOf`, `onListChangeOf`), and one `afterEvent` handler does what followed every event in the old loop. No dispatch loop is left. Command ids now come from AppHandlers (1000000 up); with the old ids mapped, all 141 editor scenario call logs match the old ones except for pure queries the old loop made for nothing. `AppHandlers.run` calls each handler type from one place, since native inlines every closure of a Function type at each call site; the native executable still grew to 15.1 MB of unused inlined copies (TODO-5747).

- [x] TODO-5748: Methods with a `Function` parameter become real calls on the VM and optexe
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-08
  - phase: Optimizing backends: real calls (found in TODO-5710)
  - parallel_track: ir-real-calls
  - scope: The real-call shape check (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererRecursionAnalysis.h) refuses a parameter type with template arguments, so `AppHandlers.command`, `commandRange`, `onClick` and the other registrations, which take a `Function<...>`, inline their vector push and growth at every call: about 1,500 VM instructions per registration. PrimeScroll registers 43 handlers, and its VM bytecode grew from 28K to 122K instructions (`main` from 7K to 68K). A `Function` value is a struct (its layout is a closure pointer and a table), so it can pass like one.
  - acceptance:
    - a method taking a `Function` by `move` is one real call on the VM and optexe; the editor's VM instruction count is measured before and after; the editor scenarios keep their call logs
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: `Function` parameters in the shape check and the real-call ABI only.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: The address-ABI shape check (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererRecursionAnalysis.cpp) takes a Function parameter by move or copy (other structs taken that way stay inline). The call site binds it as an inlined call does, and the real-call body owns it: registerRealCallOwnedParameters (IrLowererLowerSetupStage.cpp) gives it a drop flag in the body's scope, so the body destroys it unless it moves it on, and the call site no longer registers it (IrLowererLowerInlineCallsBody.h). The template-argument rule was not the blocker: after monomorphization the parameter is the plain struct /__FunctionN__t..., only the move transform kept it inline. PrimeScroll on the VM target: 121,989 to 58,274 lowered instructions (107,534 to 44,950 optimized); optcpp source 2.38 MB to 1.17 MB; optexe headless editor 1.26 MB to 1.03 MB; native unchanged at 5.7 MB (no address ABI, TODO-5740). Web examples unchanged (no closures taken by move): todo_web 1.38 MB, hello_button 1.25 MB, web_app 0.92 MB, http_hello 0.75 MB. Tests: a program-matrix case (closures moved into methods, moved on, captures destroyed once) and two shape-check cases; the editor scenarios keep their call logs. Release gate: 1893 of 1893 passed.

- [x] TODO-5750: Native heap blocks share pages
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Native PrimeScroll, direct native backend (found in TODO-5749)
  - parallel_track: native-heap
  - scope: The arm64 native `HeapAlloc` (src/native_emitter/NativeEmitterInternalsArm64Core.h, `emitHeapAllocFromSlotCountReg`) maps every block on its own (`mmap` of the slot count plus a header, `munmap` on free), so each struct box, vector buffer and `Function` closure box costs at least one 16 KiB page and a syscall. PrimeScroll on handlers holds about 80 such pages at exit against 17 for the loop version: 1.31 MB against 0.28 MB of untagged dirty memory, the bulk of its 2.90 MB native footprint (1.62 MB for the loop version), measured with a probe dylib (`DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`) that sums `mach_vm_region_recurse` dirty pages by region tag at exit, scenario `close:1`. Pool small slot blocks in chunks per size class, with the allocator state in the writable data segment, as TODO-5586 plans for text blocks (and share the pool if that lands first); `HeapRealloc` and `HeapFree` follow, a double free faults; x86_64 and the JIT keep working.
  - acceptance:
    - the native editor's peak footprint for `close:1` drops by at least 0.8 MB with the same call logs; a loop that allocates and frees 100000 small structs stays within a few MB; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the native slot-heap allocator (alloc, realloc, free); no change to text blocks unless shared with TODO-5586.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: The arm64 heap instructions now call three routines emitted once after the functions (src/native_emitter/NativeEmitterInternalsArm64Heap.h) instead of an inline `mmap` per allocation. A block of up to 512 slots (8 KiB, header included) is pooled: rounded up to a size class (exact up to 8 slots, then four classes per doubling, 31 classes), carved from 256 KiB chunks that every class shares, put on its class's free list when freed and handed out again zeroed; larger blocks keep a mapping of their own. The allocator state (chunk cursor and end, 31 free-list heads) is the data segment's runtime state (`MachOData::stateBytes`), addressed by ADRP/ADD pairs the image builder patches. The block header is unchanged (magic, total bytes); pooled blocks have their own magics, so `HeapRealloc` keeps a region's kind and copies the leading slots, and a double free or a realloc of a freed block prints `invalid heap free address: ...` / `invalid heap realloc address: ...` and exits with 3. x86_64 and the JIT are unchanged. Native PrimeScroll, `close:1`, same call logs: peak footprint 2.90 MB to 1.97-1.98 MB (untagged dirty memory 1.31 MB to 0.41 MB); the loop version 1.62-1.67 MB both before and after; executable 5.72 MB to 5.39 MB (the loop version 2.22 to 2.19 MB). Tests (PrimeStruct_primestruct_compile_run_native_backend_pointers): 20000 live list nodes and 100000 alloc/free pairs under 16 MB with reuse zeroed, realloc across pooled and mapped blocks, and the double-free and realloc-after-free faults. Spec: docs/spec/vm-design.md (Heap). Release gate: 1893 of 1894 passed; the one failure was the stale test inventory (regenerated, PrimeStruct_test_inventory passes).

- [x] TODO-5751: The VM heap of optexe keeps the handler table without a doubled high-water
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Native PrimeScroll (found in TODO-5749)
  - parallel_track: vm-heap
  - scope: The optexe headless editor on handlers peaks at 1.90 MB against 1.54 MB for the loop version (after TODO-5748; 2.0 MB before). Its stack differs by 16 KB (main's frame is 3632 slots against 1541); the rest is 0.51 MB against 0.21 MB of dirty MALLOC_SMALL pages, the optexe runtime's `VmHeapCore`, which keeps every heap block in one `std::vector` of slots (and one of states) that grows by doubling and appends a block when no free range fits, so the 26 vectors of `AppHandlers` growing in turn leave holes and the buffers of earlier doublings. Measure the slot high-water and the free ranges at exit, then cut it (for example coalescing and reusing freed ranges for growing blocks, or growing the slot vector in chunks instead of reallocating it).
  - acceptance:
    - the optexe editor's peak footprint for `close:1` is within 0.2 MB of the loop version's with the same call logs; the VM heap's own tests and the VM benchmarks are no slower; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: `VmHeapCore` growth and reuse (VM and optcpp runtime); no change to the handler API.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: Measured first (an instrumented optcpp build of the editor): at exit the heap held 13,318 slots for 7,559 live ones in 63 allocations (seven 1024-slot vectors live, four freed after a copy, holes of 1,658 and 4,096 slots) in slot storage grown by doubling to a capacity of 16,384, whose earlier buffers stayed dirty in the allocator; the loop version allocates no heap slots at all. `VmHeapCore` (include/primec/runtime/VmHeapCoreBody.inc) now reserves its slot and state storage ahead (2^20 slots, then doubling) from calloc'd memory through a `ZeroedAllocator` that leaves value-initialized elements alone, so growing the heap writes nothing and pages of slots a program never stores to (most of a 1024-slot vector record) stay untouched; a free clears only the slots that are not zero. A `HeapRealloc` that grows an allocation whose next slots are free (a free run long enough, or the end of the heap) grows it in place under a new generation, so the old address still faults. The generated C++ preambles include `<cstdlib>` and `<new>`. optexe editor, `close:1`, 15 runs each, same call logs: median peak footprint 1.88 MB to 1.75 MB against 1.57 MB for the loop version (gap 0.31 MB to 0.18 MB; MALLOC_SMALL 0.49 MB to 0.26 MB plus 64 KB of touched reservation). A VmHeapCore churn benchmark (doubling vectors and out-of-order boxes) runs in the same time with the old and new code (0.73-0.75 s against 0.71-0.74 s); the runtime benchmarks of scripts/benchmark.sh stay under their baselines. Tests (PrimeStruct_vm_byte_regions): growth in place into a free run and at the end of the heap with the old address faulting, a move when the next slots are live, and the reservation keeping the storage in place over 64,000 slots. Spec: docs/spec/vm-design.md (Heap). Release gate: 1893 of 1894 passed; the one failure was the stale test inventory (regenerated, PrimeStruct_test_inventory passes).

- [x] TODO-5740: Native and the Linux JIT number local addresses across frames
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-08
  - phase: Optimizing backends (found while finishing TODO-5734)
  - parallel_track: native-real-calls
  - scope: The address ABI of real calls (docs/spec/vm-design.md, Real calls) needs a local address to name the same slot in every frame. The VM and optcpp/optexe do that now; `--emit=native` and the Linux x86_64 JIT still resolve a local address against the current frame, so native keeps the scalar ABI (struct methods inline) and the JIT refuses a module with a function that takes a local address and calls. Give native frames real stack addresses (or the VM numbering) and enable the address ABI there. Deferred: web apps build with optexe; no native web target yet.
  - acceptance:
    - native and JIT run the real-call matrix cases with struct, `mut` and Reference parameters; the native web_app build is measured
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: local addressing and real-call shapes on native and the JIT.
  - notes: since TODO-5710 PrimeScroll's native build (the installed app) inlines its whole handler dispatch: each `Editor` method into its closure, and every closure of a `Function` type at each call site of that type; `AppHandlers.run` calls each handler vector from one place to keep that to one copy, and TODO-5747 drops the unused copies.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: Split: the arm64 native backend is done here, x86_64 native and the Linux JIT moved to TODO-5752 (they need a Linux machine to run). The arm64 emitter already addressed a local by its machine address (x27 plus its offset), and a caller's frame stays in place on the machine stack while its callees run, so a local address names the same slot in every frame; the lowering now takes the address ABI for `IrValidationTarget::Native` on an arm64 macOS host (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSetupStage.cpp), with no emitter change. The address-ABI shape check also accepts a parameter with a default (`[i32] limit{3i32}`), which the call site binds as an inlined call does; without it a recursive definition with a defaulted parameter, a real call on native before, was refused (on the VM too). Native PrimeScroll, `close:1`, same call logs: executable 5.39 MB to 0.79 MB (loop version 2.19 to 0.36 MB); peak footprint 1.98 MB to 1.65-1.75 MB (median 1.70 MB of 15 runs) against 1.47-1.56 MB for the loop version (median 1.52 MB), stack 459 KB to 115 KB. The native web_app does not build natively for a reason outside this task (`string heap statistics and limits are not available on the native backend yet`, TODO-5586). Tests: a program-matrix case checks that ten call sites of a struct-taking, struct-returning definition lower to under five times one call site's instructions on arm64 native (`--opt-report`); the real-call matrix cases (struct, `mut` and Reference parameters, struct results, a runtime error in a real call) now run as real calls on native; a recursive `mut` out-parameter keeps its writeback as a real call (test_ir_pipeline_serialization_calls.h), and two map-lookup cases validate the native-lowered module for the native target, whose real-call bodies use `Trap`. Docs: docs/spec/vm-design.md (Frames & stack, Real calls), docs/NativeUiPlan.md, docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md, stdlib/std/ui/native/handlers.prime. Release gate: 1894 of 1894 passed (an earlier run also timed out PrimeStruct_primestruct_program_matrix_closures under load; it takes 67 s alone).

- [x] TODO-5749: PrimeScroll on handlers uses no more memory than its loop did
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-08
  - phase: Native PrimeScroll, direct native backend (found in TODO-5710)
  - parallel_track: native-ui-memory
  - scope: Since TODO-5710 the headless editor's peak memory footprint (`/usr/bin/time -l`, scenario `close:1`) is 3.0 MB natively against 1.6 MB before, and 2.0 MB against 1.5 MB with optexe; the AppKit native editor's peak resident set is 118.1 MB against 114.3 MB, with the same time to the first window. The likely cause is frame size: the native backend inlines `AppHandlers.run`, every closure (twice per `Function` call) and their `Editor` methods into a few functions whose frames hold every inlined local. Measure where the memory goes (frame sizes of `main` and the `Function` call bodies, heap blocks of the handler vectors) and cut it, for example by reusing the slots of inlined scopes that have ended.
  - acceptance:
    - the editor's native and optexe peak footprint is back within 0.2 MB of the loop version, measured the same way; the editor scenarios keep their call logs
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the memory of the handler dispatch; no change to the handler API.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: Measured with a probe dylib (`DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, private dirty pages per VM region tag and `phys_footprint` at exit) and `/usr/bin/time -l`, both versions of examples/apps/text_editor/main.prime (current, and the loop version at 279a3d4b3) built by the same primec, headless scenario `close:1`. The cause was not mainly frame size: natively the heap mapped every block on its own (TODO-5750: untagged memory 1.31 MB against 0.28 MB), optexe's `VmHeapCore` kept doubled slot storage (TODO-5751: MALLOC_SMALL 0.51 MB against 0.21 MB), and native inlined every registration and closure into a 40,312-slot main frame (TODO-5740: stack 459 KB against 229 KB). After the three, medians of 20 runs each, same call logs as before: native 1.70 MB against 1.51 MB for the loop version (was 2.90 against 1.62), optexe 1.74 MB against 1.56 MB (was 1.88 against 1.54); native executables 0.79 MB and 0.36 MB (were 5.72 and 2.22 MB), optexe 1.03 and 0.86 MB. What is left natively is mostly text blocks, which still take a mapping each (untagged 0.41 MB in 11 regions against 0.28 MB in 5; TODO-5586), and 33 KB of stack; on optexe the heap's 64 KB of touched slot storage, 16 KB of stack and allocator noise. The AppKit editor's resident set was not measured (it needs a window). Release gate: 1894 of 1894 passed at b101f558a (TODO-5740); this closes it with docs only.

- [x] TODO-5753: PrimeScroll draws its first window before it restores the session
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Native PrimeScroll startup (docs/NativeUiPlan.md section 1)
  - parallel_track: startup-session
  - scope: `main` in examples/apps/text_editor/main.prime calls `restore_session` (and `editor.syncToolbars()`) after `first.show()` but before `app.run(handlers)`, so every restored tab, its toolbar and a markdown preview are built before the first frame; and a launch from Finder delivers its document as an open-file event (args has one element), so the session is restored first and the requested file then opens in a new tab. Move the restore behind the first frame: the backend reports when the first window has been drawn (an addition to the C ABI behind `ps_ui_supports`, for instance an event after the first display, or a command the program posts to itself that the backend delivers after the next display), `AppHandlers` gets a handler for it, and PrimeScroll restores the session and syncs toolbars there. A document named at launch (argument or the first open-file event) loads into the first window, and restored tabs are added behind it without taking the selection from it. Keep the behavior otherwise (same tabs, same order, active tab when no document was requested).
  - implementation_notes: measure with `scripts/measure_startup.sh --session <a:b:c> --previews 0:0:1 <bundle>` (the probe answers `session.files`/`session.previews` in snapshot mode) and with `--file`; the AppKit backend is src/ui/appkit/NativeUiAppKit.mm (`ps_ui_wait_event`), the headless backend and src/ui/NativeUiBindings.cpp need the same addition; handlers in stdlib/std/ui/native/handlers.prime.
  - acceptance:
    - with a 3-file session (2 KB text, 50 KB `.prime`, 70 KB markdown in split preview) the snapshot `loop` and `text` medians are within 15 ms of the no-session run (2026-11-09: loop 559 against 167 ms, +392)
    - a document opened at launch with a session present is drawn within 100 ms of the first frame and is the selected tab; the restored tabs are all there after it
    - an AppKit snapshot test covers restore-after-first-frame (tab count and order) and a headless test the new event; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the ordering of launch work; the cost of a tab or a toolbar itself is TODO-5754.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: The UI C ABI stays version 0 and gains feature 2, `PS_UI_FEATURE_FIRST_FRAME`: `ps_ui_watch_first_frame()` asks for `PS_UI_EVENT_FIRST_FRAME` (13), which arrives once after the first window the program showed has been drawn, with that window, behind the events that came before it. AppKit adds a main run loop observer (before waiting and on exit, highest order, so after Core Animation's commit; the first display is often on the way out of the first pump) that delivers it once the window's views no longer need display, and a 0.5 s timer for a window that is not drawn soon; nothing is installed until a program asks. The headless backend delivers it at the first wait after the first shown window, or where a scenario places it (`headless::pushFirstFrame`, `frame`). `AppHandlers.onFirstFrame(handler)` gets the window; on a backend without the feature it runs before the first wait. PrimeScroll shows its window (with a document named on the command line and its toolbar) and restores the session in `onFirstFrame`: a file the system asks to open at launch arrives first and opens in the first window, the session's tabs open behind it (each after the one before, which also fixes AppKit's reversed order of restored tabs: a tab joins its host's group right after the host) without that file twice, and the first window stays selected; the session is not saved over before the restore. Snapshot mode gained `PRIMESTRUCT_UI_LISTS` (list preferences), `PRIMESTRUCT_UI_OPEN_FILE_EVENT=!path` (a request present before the first wait) and `PRIMESTRUCT_UI_TABS_REPORT`; the probe and `scripts/measure_startup.sh` report the `frame` and `settled` phases and the ps_ui calls after the loop. Measured (direct exec, snapshot mode, 7 runs each, load about 10, same machine and session of small.txt 2 KB, a 50 KB `.prime` and a 70 KB markdown in split preview): before, loop 621, first text 273 ms; after, loop 158, first text 211, first frame event 239, restore done 613 ms; no session after: loop 146, first text 187 ms (loop +12, text +24 against the 15 ms target, within the run-to-run noise of about 30 ms). Tests: headless first-frame delivery, placement and the cases without it; editor scenarios for restore-after-first-frame order, a launch document with a session, and a quit before the first frame; the compiled AppKit editor's session and launch-document tab order (NativeUiCompiledAppTest). Call logs of the session scenarios changed on purpose (the watch call and the first_frame event; restore calls after it); the toolbar sync now runs before `first.show()`. Release gate: 1892 of 1894 passed; PrimeStruct_native_ui_appkit_smoke timed out under load (load average about 100, 100 s alone) and PrimeStruct_native_ui_compiled_app failed on the size check of the new session picture (the first window is a background tab there; the check now only asks for a drawn PNG), both then passed alone.

- [x] TODO-5820: Snapshot runs and the UI tests show nothing on screen
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Native UI tests (docs/NativeUiPlan.md section 12)
  - parallel_track: ui-tests-invisible
  - scope: Since a0b1ed5f1 AppKit snapshot runs (`PRIMESTRUCT_UI_SNAPSHOT`: the three UI test drivers, `scripts/measure_startup.sh`, the editor scenarios' bundles, a bundle's warm-up launch) take no focus, but they order their windows in on the user's screen (transparent only once `presentWindow` ran, after a tab group could already have ordered them in), and the bundles the drivers launch through Launch Services are regular apps. The user still saw things appear during gates. Make snapshot windows invisible from creation and keep them out of the way of the screens, make test bundles agent apps, and make a visible window an explicit opt-in.
  - acceptance:
    - a `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` poll (every 5 ms) during the three AppKit drivers sees no window of theirs that is on screen, not transparent and of a nonzero size on a display
    - the drivers' pictures are the same pixel for pixel (or the difference is explained); the snapshot launch phases do not change; the focus guard (exit 3) stays
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: where and how snapshot windows show; nothing about what they draw. `scripts/measure_startup.sh --open` (a person's real launch) and the opt-in open-menu step stay visible.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: A snapshot window is transparent, shadowless, mouse-transparent and out of the window cycle from `ps_ui_window_create` on (before joining a tab group can order it in), and once ordered in it is parked past the bottom right corner of the screens. AppKit keeps a corner of a titled window on a screen (40 by 32 points on this machine), transparent; a window subclass that refuses the constraint does leave every screen, but then takes another screen's backing scale (2x instead of 1x here, so different pictures) and is reported occluded (a web view stops drawing there), so it was not used. `PRIMESTRUCT_UI_SHOW_WINDOWS=1` shows snapshot windows (opaque, in place) for a person watching. The UI drivers set `PS_UI_ELEMENT=1` (cmake/NativeUiTestGuards.cmake), which makes `scripts/bundle_macos_app.sh` write `LSUIElement`, so the editor smoke test's Launch Services launch (`open -n -W`) shows no Dock icon. Checked: a `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` poll every 5 ms during the smoke, scroll and compiled-app drivers (387 distinct window states) saw no window with alpha above 0 and a nonzero size on a display (the only non-transparent ones were 0 by 0 point windows that AppKit fades in near a control); all 34 smoke, 18 scroll and 13 compiled-app pictures are the same pixel for pixel as before (one scroll picture, blocks_text, differs between a run under load and a quiet run of the old code as well; quiet runs of old and new are identical); snapshot launch medians with and without it are within noise (first text 195 against 186 ms, 9 runs each, load 15). Docs: docs/NativeUiPlan.md section 12.

- [x] TODO-5754: Restored tabs, toolbars and previews cost less in the AppKit backend
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Native PrimeScroll startup (docs/NativeUiPlan.md section 1)
  - parallel_track: startup-backend
  - scope: Measured on a launch that restores three files: `ps_ui_window_add_tab` 80 to 130 ms per tab (tab-group layout under `-[NSWindow _addTabbedWindow:ordered:]`), `ps_ui_window_set_toolbar` 30 to 60 ms per markdown or script toolbar (61 ms to open one `.prime` file at launch), `ps_ui_window_set_preview_mode` 95 to 250 ms for a markdown preview (WebKit and a WKWebView). Make a tab that is not shown cheap (add it to the tab group without laying it out until it is selected), build each kind of toolbar's items once and reuse them, and create a preview's web view when its tab is first shown. Profile with `scripts/measure_startup.sh --sample` first.
  - implementation_notes: src/ui/appkit/NativeUiAppKit.mm; the editor builds toolbars in `sync_toolbars` (examples/apps/text_editor/main.prime); keep the snapshot tests' images unchanged.
  - acceptance:
    - with the 3-file session of TODO-5753, the ps_ui calls of the launch (before the event loop, and the restore after the first frame: the `late_` calls of `scripts/measure_startup.sh`) total at most 120 ms (2026-11-09: about 430 ms in add_tab, set_toolbar and set_preview_mode alone); `add_tab` at most 20 ms per background tab; opening a `.prime` file at launch spends at most 15 ms in `set_toolbar`
    - switching to a restored tab shows its text, toolbar and preview as before (AppKit snapshot tests); full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the cost of tabs, toolbars and previews; their order at launch is TODO-5753.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: Sampled (`scripts/measure_startup.sh --sample`) and timed in a scratch AppKit program: `-[NSWindow addTabbedWindow:ordered:]` lays out the whole group (SwiftUI's AttributeGraph under `NSPerformVisuallyAtomicChange`; 65 to 80 ms for the first tab, 20 to 30 ms after), while `-[NSWindowTabGroup insertWindow:atIndex:]` costs about 30 ms for the first (the tab bar appears) and 2 to 5 ms after, and a tab that is not ordered in is not laid out. The UI C ABI gains `ps_ui_window_add_background_tab(host, tab)` (version 0, an added function): the tab joins right after `host`, neither shown nor selected; AppKit inserts it into the group and, while a tab is behind the selected one, `ps_ui_window_set_toolbar` keeps the toolbar waiting (with the selected segment) and `ps_ui_window_set_preview(_mode)` puts a plain placeholder where the page goes; both are built when the tab is shown (`ps_ui_window_select`, `ps_ui_window_add_tab`, or a click or shortcut, checked on every pass of `ps_ui_wait_event` before the next display). `ps_ui_window_add_tab` inserts into the group the same way (then selects). Headless: the tab joins after its host and the active window stays. PrimeScroll restores a session's tabs with `Window.addBackgroundTab` and selects the active one at the end. Measured (snapshot, the 3-file session of TODO-5753, interleaved 3 rounds of 7 runs, load 40 to 77): loop 494 -> 174 ms, first text 231 -> 212, restore done at 297 ms (613 after TODO-5753 alone); ps_ui calls of the restore 42 ms in all (load 9: two background tabs 21 ms, two window creations 9, the preview 5, toolbars 0.1) against about 455 ms; `ps_ui_init` (73 to 97 ms) is TODO-5756. Not met: a `.prime` file opened at launch still spends 45 to 70 ms in its toolbar, the cost of the first `NSToolbar` of a process (no cheaper empty, without images, in any style, or as a titlebar accessory view); filed TODO-5821. Toolbar sync went back to after `first.show()` (TODO-5753 had moved it before; interleaved runs showed no gain). Tests: headless background tabs (order after the host, selection kept; refusals); the editor's restore uses no `ps_ui_window_add_tab`; the compiled AppKit editor's tabs report shows the waiting toolbar and page of a background tab and both built after Command-3 shows it (NativeUiCompiledAppTest). The session scenarios' call logs changed on purpose (`ps_ui_window_add_background_tab` instead of `add_tab` and `show` for restored tabs).

- [x] TODO-5755: The first launch after an install does not wait on Gatekeeper
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Native PrimeScroll startup (docs/NativeUiPlan.md section 1)
  - parallel_track: startup-install
  - scope: Every build is new ad-hoc signed code, so the first exec of the installed executable waits for syspolicyd's Gatekeeper assessment (a notarization-ticket lookup over the network, request timeout 3 s, then an XProtect scan): +320 to 500 ms for the executable and +170 to 250 ms for a new `libps_ui_appkit.dylib`, up to seconds on a slow network. Make the install pay it instead of the user: `scripts/bundle_macos_app.sh` (and the reinstall recipe it serves) runs the signed bundle's executable once in snapshot mode with no document right after signing, so the assessment is cached before anyone opens the app; and the dylib is copied and re-signed only when its bytes changed, so an install that does not touch the backend keeps its cdhash. Record whether a first Launch Services launch after this is as fast as a later one (a person runs `scripts/measure_startup.sh --open --runs 1` right after an install, twice).
  - implementation_notes: the cache is per cdhash; the warm-up must run the installed copy's code (same bytes as the bundle, so running the bundle before the `ditto` is enough only when the files are copied byte for byte). Snapshot mode never activates and exits by itself (`PRIMESTRUCT_UI_SNAPSHOT=<scratch png>`); tests that build bundles set `PS_NO_DOCUMENT_TYPES=1`.
  - acceptance:
    - after `scripts/bundle_macos_app.sh --native`, the bundle's next direct launch has `main` within 30 ms of a repeated launch (2026-11-09: 428 against 26 ms)
    - a first `--open` launch after an install is within 50 ms of the second (owner records both numbers in docs/todo_log.md)
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the install path; notarization or a Developer ID signature is out of scope.
  - notes: linking the AppKit backend into the executable (one image, one assessment) would also halve it but needs a linker on the direct native path; file it only if the warm-up does not cover Launch Services launches.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: `scripts/warm_macos_app.sh <App.app>` launches a bundle once in snapshot mode (no window on screen, no focus, no Dock icon, no preferences or session; it ends by itself once its window is drawn, killed after 30 s otherwise) and starts the script runner beside a native editor without arguments; `scripts/bundle_macos_app.sh` runs it on every bundle it makes (`PS_NO_WARMUP=1` skips it), adding about 3 s to a first bundling of new code and 1 to 2 s otherwise. Measured with `scripts/measure_startup.sh` (direct exec, the first launch is its warm-up line, load 8 to 10): warmed bundles started `main` at 17.7, 17.4 and 14.8 ms against 28 to 38 ms for the repeated launches (one at 81 ms right after a new dylib, against 37); a bundle made with `PS_NO_WARMUP=1` at 383 ms. Found: the verdict is kept per file, not per code hash alone: a moved bundle started at 20 ms, one re-signed in place at 45 ms, but a `ditto` copy of a warmed bundle at 357 ms. So the install recipe (copy, re-sign, then launch from ~/Applications) must run `scripts/warm_macos_app.sh` on the installed copy after its last `codesign`; a warmed copy started at 22 ms. Ad-hoc signing identical bytes gives the identical code hash (checked: the same CDHash for two signings), so a dylib whose bytes did not change keeps its hash with no check in the script. Not measured here (a person's run, now in TODO-5603): the first Launch Services launch after an install, `scripts/measure_startup.sh --open --runs 1 ~/Applications/PrimeScroll.app` twice. Docs: docs/NativeUiPlan.md section 1.

- [x] TODO-5770: arm64 native constants take one instruction where they fit
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Optimizing backends, native code quality (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 12)
  - parallel_track: native-arm64-isel
  - scope: `Arm64Emitter::emitMovImm64` always writes `movz` and three `movk`, so 0 or 1 is four instructions; `movk xN, #0` is 22% of PrimeScroll's `__text` (42,845 of 192,191 words). Emit `movz` or `movn` plus only the `movk`s whose half differs, use the 12-bit add/sub/cmp immediate forms when a pushed constant feeds AddI*/SubI*/Cmp*, and make `emitAdjustSp` use `#imm, lsl #12` plus a remainder (the entry function's 1 MB value stack is 257 `sub sp` today, and as many `add sp` at every return).
  - implementation_notes: src/native_emitter/NativeEmitterInternalsArm64Core.h (`emitMovImm64`, `emitAdjustSp`), NativeEmitterInternalsArm64Arithmetic.h; pin the new encodings in `primestruct.ir.native_codegen` through `primec/testing/NativeEmitterEncodings.h`. Measure with `otool -tv` and `/usr/bin/time -l` (instructions retired), as in section 12.
  - acceptance:
    - PrimeScroll (`primec --emit=native examples/apps/text_editor/main.prime`) `__text` at most 610 KB (2026-11-09: 768,764 bytes)
    - `benchmarks/aggregate.prime` native retires at least 15% fewer instructions (2026-11-09: 467.7M); every benchmark prints the same result
    - full release gate green on macOS arm64
  - stop_rule: constant materialization, immediate operand forms and stack-pointer adjustment only; no change to the value-stack cache.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: `emitMovImm64` writes `movz`/`movn` of one half plus a `movk` per differing half, `movn w` for a 32-bit value with one 0xFFFF half, or `orr` from xzr for a bitmask immediate; constant pushes build in x26; a constant feeding AddI*/SubI*/integer Cmp* is a 12-bit (or `lsl #12`) immediate, negated through `sub`/`add`/`cmn` when only the negation fits, and +/- 0 emits nothing; `emitAdjustSp` uses `#pages, lsl #12` plus the low 12 bits. Measured (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.6): PrimeScroll `__text` 787,480 -> 483,008 bytes (-38.7%; it had grown from the 768,764 of the TODO), executable 824,362 -> 512,458; aggregate 469.0M -> 317.8M instructions retired (-32.2%), call_fib -30.6%, float_series -26.0%, json_scan -39.1%, json_parse -43.0%; aggregate 1.49x fewer cycles back to back; same output on every benchmark. Encoder tests in `primestruct.ir.native_codegen`.
