- [x] TODO-5772: arm64 native allocates registers for integer code
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Optimizing backends, native code quality (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 12; Phase 3.6)
  - parallel_track: native-arm64-regalloc
  - scope: x86_64 native allocates registers for every value of a function (`planNativeRegisterAllocation`, NativeEmitterInternalsX64RegAlloc.h); arm64 skips all of it (`if constexpr (!kIsArm64)` in NativeEmitterFunctionEmit.cpp) and PrimeScroll reports `register_allocated=0`. Give `Arm64Emitter` the same entry point for functions without calls, floats or `AddressOfLocal`: an arm64 pool, integer arithmetic and `SextI32` on registers, compares fused with `JumpIfZero` (`cmp`/`b.cond`, `cset` otherwise), constants as immediates, frame locals, edge parallel moves, and every other opcode through its template with operands pushed and live registers saved, as on x86_64. Other functions stay on templates with a fallback reason in `--opt-report`.
  - implementation_notes: the plan is target-independent; the emission is new. Registers taken today: x19/x20 argc/argv, x21 the caller's frame in the prologue, x26 the operand cache, x27 the frame, x28 the value stack, x16 the syscall number, x29/x30; templates use x0-x13 as scratch. `PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=0|1` forces it off or on as on x86_64. Variant D of section 12.4 is the target shape (aggregate about 1 cycle per iteration).
  - acceptance:
    - `/main` of aggregate, json_scan and json_parse is register-allocated on arm64 (`--opt-report` `native_register_allocation_v1`, pinned by a CLI test)
    - aggregate native at most 80M instructions retired (2026-11-09: 467.7M) and json_parse at least 2.5x fewer cycles than before, back to back with the load average recorded; same output
    - `scripts/differential_opt_check.py --native` and the release gate green on macOS arm64, also with `PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=1`
  - stop_rule: functions without calls, float opcodes or local addresses; those are TODO-5774, TODO-5773 and TODO-5775.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: `Arm64Emitter::emitRegisterAllocatedFunction` (src/native_emitter/NativeEmitterInternalsArm64RegAlloc.h) emits the target-independent plan of `planNativeRegisterAllocation` (now 64-bit register masks and a `floatRegisters` switch, off on arm64) for functions without `Call`/`CallVoid`, float opcodes or `AddressOfLocal` (`arm64RegisterAllocationBlocker` names the reason in `--opt-report`). Pool x21-x25 first (kept by every template, host call and heap routine), x19/x20 when no function reads argc/argv, then x9-x15 and x3-x8 (saved around templates while live); x0/x1 scratch, x17 for far frame offsets. Integer add/sub/mul/div/neg, SextI32, ZextU8, compares fused into `cmp`+`b.cond` or `cset`, 12-bit immediates, word and byte loads/stores, LoadStringByte, pinned frame locals, edge parallel moves with trampolines, returns and Trap are inline; every other opcode runs its template on the value stack with live x3-x15 saved. Conditional branches are one word (`b.cond`/`cbz`); a function whose branches do not reach is rolled back (`Arm64Emitter::checkpoint`) and emitted again with two-word branches. Measured back to back at load 11 (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.8): aggregate 317.3M -> 52.1M instructions retired, 49.3M -> 12.9M cycles (3.8x); json_scan 446.8M -> 89.2M, 3.3x fewer cycles; json_parse 564.7M -> 122.6M, 99.5M -> 44.8M cycles (2.2x against the TODO-5770 build, 4.4x against the 196.6M of the TODO's 12.3 baseline); call_fib and float_series unchanged (calls, floats); PrimeScroll `register_allocated` 0 -> 109 of 245 (35 call, 101 take a local address), `__text` 483,008 -> 479,688 bytes. Same output on every benchmark; `differential_opt_check.py --native` (also with PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=1) differs only in the six pre-existing -O0 template cases; release gate green (PrimeStruct_cpp_host_calls flaked once, passed alone). Tests in `primestruct.ir.native_codegen` (VM-differential suites now also on arm64, loop disassembly shape, spills past the pool, fallbacks, far branches and the re-emit path) and `primestruct.ir.optimizer_cli`.

- [x] TODO-5774: arm64 register allocation spans calls and passes arguments in registers
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Optimizing backends, native code quality (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 12)
  - parallel_track: native-arm64-regalloc
  - scope: Take functions with `Call`/`CallVoid` and the return opcodes into the arm64 allocator: save the registers live across a call, take the result from x0, and when every caller of a callee is register-allocated pass its first arguments in registers instead of the memory value stack (x86_64 uses three; arm64 can use more).
  - implementation_notes: the TODO-5772 allocator (NativeEmitterInternalsArm64RegAlloc.h) keeps nothing across a call: its callees use x21-x25 (which every template keeps) and x3-x15, so an allocated caller must save its live registers around a `bl` as x86_64 does (`regAllocRegistersLiveAcross`), or the pool must be split into caller- and callee-saved halves. Entry values arrive on the x28 value stack today.
  - acceptance:
    - call_fib's `/fib` register-allocated and at least 1.5x fewer cycles than the TODO-5772 build, back to back with the load average recorded
    - the struct-method and closure-call loops of docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 12.3 retire fewer instructions than before; same output
    - release gate green on macOS arm64
  - stop_rule: calls between functions without local addresses; address-taking functions are TODO-5775.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: arm64 register allocation takes functions with `Call`/`CallVoid` (src/native_emitter/NativeEmitterInternalsArm64RegAlloc.h). A call saves every register live across it (`regAllocRegistersLiveAcross`) in its frame save slot and reloads it after, since any callee may change x0-x25; the result comes from x0. A callee that is not the entry and whose callers are all register-allocated takes its first eight arguments in x0-x7 (parallel copies at the call and at entry, cycles broken through x16), the rest on the value stack as template callees take them all; the prologue stores the caller's frame and link through x17 so far frame slots cannot overwrite an argument. Caller-saved was kept over a callee-saved split: call_fib already beats the target with it, and leaf functions (the hot loops) pay nothing. Measured back to back at load 10.4-10.9 (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.9): call_fib 337.6M -> 160.3M instructions, 69.9M -> 30.9M cycles (2.3x); aggregate, json_scan, json_parse, float_series unchanged; the struct-method and closure-call loops of 12.3 unchanged within noise (553.3M -> 553.2M and 206.4M -> 204.8M instructions; their loops are in a `/main` that takes a local address, TODO-5775). PrimeScroll `register_allocated` 109 -> 131 of 245 (the other 114 take a local address), `__text` 479,688 -> 473,796 bytes; snapshot launch medians within run-to-run noise at load 9-14. Same output on every benchmark; `differential_opt_check.py --native` (also with PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=1) 490 match, the same six -O0 differences as before. Tests in `primestruct.ir.native_codegen` (16 values live across two calls of a 16-parameter callee with reversed arguments, a template caller of an allocated callee, exact disassembly of a recursive fib) and `primestruct.ir.optimizer_cli` (call_fib's `/fib` and `/main` register-allocated).

- [x] TODO-5822: The std/os SIGTERM test does not race the program's start
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Test reliability (found in the release gates of TODO-5754 to TODO-5756)
  - parallel_track: test-flakes
  - scope: `std/os reads the environment, sees the clock advance and stops on SIGTERM` (tests/unit/backend_host/test_cpp_strings.cpp, CTest `PrimeStruct_cpp_host_calls`) failed in two of four release gates under load (load average 50 to 150), with exit code 11 instead of 111: the program printed `ready` and ended without seeing the SIGTERM as a stop (100). Both times it passed alone. Find the race (when the handler is installed against when `ready` is printed and the signal sent, or the program finishing first) and make the test wait on what it needs.
  - acceptance:
    - the case passes in 20 runs of `PrimeStruct_cpp_host_calls` while a release gate runs, and in the gate; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: this test and the std/os signal handling it checks.
  - notes: not listed under Open Failures: it passes alone, and docs/failing_tests.md fails on a listed case that passes.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: The race was the handshake itself. The program printed `ready` to stdout, which an optexe program writes through stdio, and stdout redirected to a file is fully buffered, so the line reached the file only at exit. The script therefore never saw `ready`: it always gave up after 200 polls of `sleep 0.05` plus a `grep` (10 s idle, far more under load) and only then sent SIGTERM, while the program stopped waiting by itself after 20 s. Under load the polls outlasted the 20 s and the program exited unsignalled (11 instead of 111). Now the fixture logs `ready` with `/std/os` `logLine` (stderr, written at once), waits for the stop with no time limit, and the script waits for that line with no poll limit, ending the wait early only if the program has died; the test also checks that stdout stays empty. A run takes about 2 s instead of over 10 s. 20 consecutive runs of the case passed (39 s, load 8-16), and it passed in all four release gates of the change (256 s for the whole entry at load 300). Of those gates, the first, second and fourth were green; the third ran at load average 300 from other work and 65 entries timed out, among them three AppKit smoke parts whose snapshot apps outlived their timeout (TODO-5823). Found while fixing the flaky tests of the same commit: `PrimeStruct_primestruct_program_matrix_closures` (67-85 s alone, 144 s and timeouts under load) is now five suites and CTest entries, and `PrimeStruct_native_ui_appkit_smoke` (100 s alone) is ten `PrimeStruct_native_ui_appkit_smoke_<part>` entries; AGENTS.md now forbids flaky tests.

- [x] TODO-5775: arm64 register allocation takes address-taking functions
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Optimizing backends, native code quality (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 12)
  - parallel_track: native-arm64-regalloc
  - scope: Under the address ABI most PrimeScroll functions take a local address, so all their locals are pinned. Allocate them with `promoteLocals = false` (operands and edge values in registers, every local in its frame slot), with `AddressOfLocal`, `LoadIndirect` and `StoreIndirect` inline, keeping caller-frame addresses valid across calls (docs/spec/vm-design.md, Real calls).
  - acceptance:
    - at least 80% of PrimeScroll's emitted functions register-allocated (`--opt-report`); its `__text` smaller than before; the headless editor's call log unchanged
    - PrimeScroll launch no slower (the startup guard of TODO-5603)
    - release gate green on macOS arm64
  - stop_rule: register allocation with pinned locals; finer escape analysis is TODO-5776.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: arm64 register allocation takes functions that execute `AddressOfLocal` (src/native_emitter/NativeEmitterInternalsArm64RegAlloc.h). `IrLocalEscape` already pins every local of such a function, so the plan keeps all of its locals in their frame slots while operands, call results and edge values live in registers; `AddressOfLocal` is emitted inline (`add xd, x27, #offset`, the shifted form, or through x17), and `LoadIndirect`/`StoreIndirect` were inline already. A write through an address, in the function or in a callee given it, lands in the frame slot the next `LoadLocal` reads; values live across a call stay in their save slots, which no address reaches. Measured against the TODO-5774 build, back to back at load 5.5 (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.10): PrimeScroll `register_allocated` 131 -> 245 of 245 (100%), `__text` 473,796 -> 250,788 bytes (-47%); snapshot launch medians (ten launches each, alternated twice) loop 185/178 -> 168/165 ms, first frame 253/250 -> 237/230 ms. The struct-method loop of 12.9 552.4M -> 327.2M instructions, 91.9M -> 47.9M cycles (1.9x); the closure-call loop 204.3M -> 116.5M, 35.0M -> 24.0M cycles (1.46x); call_fib, aggregate, json_scan, json_parse and float_series unchanged. Same output on every benchmark; `differential_opt_check.py --native` (also with PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=1) 490 match and the same six -O0 differences; the compiled native editor's headless call logs match the VM in `PrimeStruct_native_ui_editor`. Tests in `primestruct.ir.native_codegen`: a function whose local is written through its address by itself and by a callee and read as a local in a loop, register-allocated with the address built inline; `/address` of the fallback test now allocated, floats still on templates with their reason.

- [x] TODO-5823: AppKit snapshot runs finish under an overloaded machine and never outlive their test
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Test reliability (found in the release gates of the flaky-test fixes after TODO-5774)
  - parallel_track: test-flakes
  - scope: In a release gate at load average 300 (other work on the machine; the same code passed the gates before and after it at load 8 to 237) `PrimeStruct_native_ui_appkit_smoke_editor_scroll`, `_editor_scroll_uneven` and `_open` timed out, and CTest reported them after 1426, 518 and 927 s against their 300 s limit: CTest kills the `cmake -P` driver, but the snapshot app it started keeps the output pipe open until it ends by itself. Their markdown preview runs wait for WebKit in 0.05 s run-loop slices (`snapshotIdleSlices`, up to 400 slices for a loading page), so they take 25 to 35 s each under load 100 and far longer at 300. Give a snapshot run a wall-clock bound that ends it with a clear error (instead of counting slices), have the drivers end the app when their own step times out, and cut what the preview runs wait for, so each entry stays well inside its limit at load 300.
  - acceptance:
    - each `PrimeStruct_native_ui_appkit_smoke_<part>` entry under 100 s at load 300 (measured while other gates run), and a run killed by its timeout leaves no app behind
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the snapshot harness of the AppKit backend and the smoke and scroll drivers.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: A snapshot run now watches the process that started it, its parent or `PRIMESTRUCT_UI_WATCH_PID` (for the `open -a` launch of the open part, whose parent is launchd), with a kqueue `NOTE_EXIT` on a thread of its own, and exits with status 70 and a message on stderr once that process is gone (`guardSnapshotRun` in NativeUiAppKit.mm), so a driver CTest kills at its limit leaves no app behind, whoever kills it. New entry `PrimeStruct_native_ui_appkit_smoke_orphan` proves it: `scripts/start_orphaned_snapshot_run.py` starts the hello window held (`PRIMESTRUCT_UI_SNAPSHOT_HOLD=1`, never takes its picture) and exits once the app says its guard watches; the step returns when the app has ended (it keeps the script's stderr) and checks the guard's message (0.5 s; with the guard switched off the app stays alive). The waits are events, not clocks: a snapshot waits for a loading preview without the old 400-slice cap (a refresh with no page to load, a preview switched off and an ended web content process now clear the loading flag, so nothing waits for a page that never comes), and the AppKit drivers (smoke, scroll, compiled app, startup guard) have no `execute_process` TIMEOUT of their own any more; CTest's limit bounds each entry. What was slow: compiling the editor to bytecode takes 9.5 s of CPU and every editor part and the scroll driver did it again (75 s of the bundle entry under gate load). A CTest fixture now bundles the editor once (`PrimeStruct_native_ui_appkit_editor_bundle`; `_editor_bundle_cleanup` unregisters it from Launch Services and deletes it, and the open part unregisters it right after its Launch Services launch), and the scroll driver is four entries, one per mode (`PrimeStruct_native_ui_appkit_scroll_<mode>`). Alone (load 12 to 28): editor parts 6 to 10 s each instead of 15 to 19, scroll modes 8 to 19 s instead of 54 s in one entry, all AppKit entries 111 s instead of 165. In a full release gate (load average up to about 40): every AppKit entry 1.4 to 43 s, the bundle entry 75 s. Not measured at load 300; the entries are a quarter of their 300 s limit or less at gate load.

- [x] TODO-5776: A local address that never leaves its function becomes local accesses
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Optimizing backends, native code quality (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 12)
  - parallel_track: ir-local-sroa
  - scope: `IrLocalEscape` pins every slot of a function that executes `AddressOfLocal`, which turns off `copy-prop`, `dead-store` and register promotion in most struct and vector code (101 of PrimeScroll's 108 optcpp functions). Add the shared IR pass `local-sroa` (-O2, every non-GPU target): when every value derived from an `AddressOfLocal` (through `LoadLocal`/`StoreLocal` copies and constant `AddI64` offsets) is used only as the address of `LoadIndirect`/`StoreIndirect` at a constant, aligned offset inside the frame, rewrite those to `LoadLocal`/`StoreLocal` of the named slot and drop the address. A derived value that reaches a call, a store as data, a comparison, a print or a return leaves the function as it is.
  - implementation_notes: src/ir/optimizer/ (new IrPassLocalSroa.cpp), manifest in src/ir/IrOptimizer.cpp before `copy-prop`. Exact: an in-range, aligned constant address cannot fault on the VM, so results and fault order do not change. Add a vector-sum benchmark (`benchmarks/vector_sum.prime`, the loop of section 12.2) with C and C++ references.
  - acceptance:
    - the vector-sum loop's IR has no `AddressOfLocal` in `/main` and at least 30% fewer instructions per element; native and VM retire fewer instructions, optexe no more
    - program-matrix cases for struct fields, vector access, an address passed to a call (left alone) and an out-of-frame offset (left alone) agree on every backend
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: addresses that stay in their function; pointer arithmetic that may leave an object is decision 5 of docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 11.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: New shared IR pass `local-sroa` (src/ir/optimizer/IrPassLocalSroa.cpp, -O2, every non-GPU target, before `copy-prop`): a forward analysis over the CFG tracks frame addresses from `AddressOfLocal` through `LoadLocal`/`StoreLocal` copies and constant `AddI*`/`SubI*` offsets; when every one is only the address of a `LoadIndirect`/`StoreIndirect` (or popped), names one aligned in-frame slot on every path and never meets another value at a join, the accesses become `LoadLocal`/`StoreLocal` (`StoreLocal` plus the dropped `Pop`, or `Dup; StoreLocal`) and the addresses go; otherwise the function is unchanged (all or nothing), and also when it reads or writes through an address built only from constants (the gfx smoke programs read a field through a reference left at 0; the VM resolves `0 + 16` to slot 1 of the entry frame, and the first gate run showed `dead-store` then changing their VM result from 90 to 2). benchmarks/vector_sum.{prime,c,cpp} added. vector_sum `/main` has no `AddressOfLocal`; inner loop 47 -> 33 IR instructions, 41 -> 27 executed per element (-34%). Back to back at load 9.8-14.6 (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.11): native 732.9M -> 292.7M instructions, 134.3M -> 66.9M cycles (2.0x); optexe 2154.5M -> 754.0M, 360.5M -> 94.4M cycles; primevm 97.9G -> 63.0G instructions; other benchmarks unchanged. PrimeScroll: 21 of 114 address-taking functions freed (the rest pass the address to a real call or return it), IR 46,180 -> 43,398, `__text` 250,788 -> 241,316 bytes, register_allocated 245/245. `differential_opt_check.py --native --levels 1,2` (also PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=1): 491 match, same six -O0 differences. Tests: goldens in `primestruct.ir.passes` (rewrites, Dup form, loops; twelve left-alone shapes including a call, stores as data, a constant (null) address, out-of-frame/unaligned/non-constant offsets and joins), `primestruct.ir.optimizer_cli` (vector loop on native, vm and optexe), four `primestruct.program_matrix.optimizer` cases.

- [x] TODO-5825: Definitions that stay inline get no standalone body
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Optimizing backends (found while checking TODO-5776)
  - parallel_track: ir-real-calls
  - scope: `lowerCallableDefinitionOrchestration` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererStatementCallHelpers.cpp) lowers a body, with `parameter_count` 0, for every definition in `loweredCallTargets`, including those that stay inline at every call site. No `Call` reaches them (141 of PrimeScroll's 246 functions on `--emit=native -O2`), yet every backend compiles them, and the 48 of them with a struct result return an address of their own frame (harmless only because they are never called). Emit only bodies that a `Call`/`CallVoid` reaches (or that a tool such as the debugger names), keeping function indices deterministic.
  - acceptance:
    - PrimeScroll's native `__text` and the IR function count shrink, measured; no function left in any example or benchmark module returns an address of its own frame
    - same results on every backend; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: dropping uncalled bodies only; no change to which definitions are real calls.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: The lowering emits no function for a definition it inlines: `lowerCallableDefinitionOrchestration`, `finalizeEntryFunctionTableAndLowerCallables` and the `loweredCallTargets` set are removed; the function-table step only makes the entry function 0, and the calls stage appends the real-call bodies, then drops any reserved body no `Call` reaches from the entry (the scalar ABI's up-front set; `/std/panic/panic` in features_overview), keeping the order of the rest. IR validation rejects a function a `Call` reaches that returns an address derived from its own `AddressOfLocal` (forward analysis over the CFG; addresses through memory not followed). PrimeScroll: IR functions 245 -> 104 (native and vm), IR instructions 60,242 -> 54,154; `--emit=ir` 396 -> 13 functions, 900,955 -> 214,489 instructions, `app.psir` 123.0 MB -> 29.5 MB, `--emit=ir` compile 11.4 s -> 5.5 s. The native executable is byte-identical (`__text` 241,316 bytes both): the native emitter and the C++ emitters already emitted only reachable functions, so launch is unchanged and the acceptance's `__text` shrink did not apply. Examples and benchmarks: vm 1,123 -> 634 functions, native 572 -> 258, every one reached. differential_opt_check.py --native --levels 1,2 (also PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=1): 491 match, same six -O0 differences. GLSL helper-call and primevm debug-dap tests now use a real call; embed bytecode fixture regenerated. Tests: `ir keeps the body of a called definition and none for inlined ones`, `validateIrModule rejects a called function that returns an address of its own frame`, updated serialization/metadata cases (inlined helpers have no function); orchestration unit tests removed with the code. docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.12, docs/spec/vm-design.md Real calls.

- [x] TODO-5771: arm64 template operands stay in the register that computed them
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Optimizing backends, native code quality (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 12)
  - parallel_track: native-arm64-isel
  - scope: The arm64 template emitter copies every pushed value into x26 and every popped value back out (`add x26, x0, #0; add x0, x26, #0`, 25% of PrimeScroll's code), bumps x28 separately from each value-stack load and store, materializes compare results as 0/1 constants with two branches before `JumpIfZero` tests them, and pads every conditional branch with a `b` to the next instruction. Let the cache name whichever register holds the top, use pre- and post-indexed `str`/`ldr` on x28, fuse a compare that feeds `JumpIfZero` into `cmp` + `b.cond`, use `cset` for a compare used as a value, and emit one-word conditional branches, relaxing only those out of range.
  - implementation_notes: models variant B of section 12.4 (aggregate 1.5-1.7x fewer cycles, 142.6M instructions). Since TODO-5775 the allocator takes every PrimeScroll function (245 of 245, `__text` 250,788 bytes); what still runs these templates is float code (until TODO-5773) and the opcodes an allocated function runs as templates on the value stack (prints, heap, host calls), so re-measure the gain and restate the acceptance against the TODO-5775 build before starting.
  - acceptance:
    - aggregate native at most 170M instructions retired and at least 1.4x fewer cycles than the TODO-5770 build, measured back to back with the load average recorded
    - PrimeScroll `__text` at least 25% smaller than after TODO-5770; same output on every benchmark and the native compile-run suites
    - full release gate green on macOS arm64
  - stop_rule: template emitter only; no register allocation and no IR changes.
  - restated_acceptance (2026-11-09, against 96e2a234a, before starting): the acceptance above was already met by the allocator work (aggregate 52.4M instructions and 3.6x fewer cycles than after TODO-5770; PrimeScroll `__text` 241,316 bytes, -50% against the 483,008 after TODO-5770). Left on templates: whole float functions (float_series; none in PrimeScroll), which TODO-5773 moves onto the allocator, so their operand cache and compare-branch fusion are not built here (float compare fusion moved into TODO-5773); and template opcodes inside allocated functions, whose operands go through the x28 value stack (1,651 pushes and 1,651 pops at two instructions each, 11% of PrimeScroll's 60,329 instructions, plus 368 `b` to the next instruction). No benchmark loop runs a template, so the restated goal is code size: PrimeScroll `__text` at least 7% smaller, one-word pushes, pops and template branches, same output everywhere, full release gate green.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-09
  - result: The arm64 value stack keeps its top at `[x28]`: a push is `str reg, [x28, #-16]!`, a pop `ldr reg, [x28], #16`. A pop that follows its push (also a run of pushes popped in reverse) becomes `add rd, rn, #0` or nothing, unless a move of the run writes the register an earlier push saved; any other word or a read of `currentWordIndex()` (every label, branch site and instruction offset) ends the run. Template-internal conditional branches are one `b.cond` word (`emitFarCondBranchPlaceholder`/`patchFarCondBranch` keep the two-word form for `JumpIfZero` and far register-allocated branches), and template comparisons use `cset`. Back to back at load 28-31 (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.13): PrimeScroll `__text` 241,316 -> 220,928 bytes (-8.4%), 60,329 -> 55,232 instructions, value-stack pushes/pops 1,651/1,651 at two instructions -> 224/224 at one, `b` to the next instruction 368 -> 0; float_series 1433.6M -> 1293.4M instructions in unchanged cycles (394.5M/395.7M, store-reload bound); other benchmarks unchanged. Same output on every benchmark; `differential_opt_check.py --native --levels 1,2` (also PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=1): 491 match, same six -O0 differences. Tests (`primestruct.ir.native_codegen`): fused pushes and pops as exact words, a label and a clobbered register that keep the store, the disassembly of a print in an allocated function (`add x0, x22, #0x0; cmp x0, xzr; b.ge`, no x28 access), updated immediate-operand and float-compare encodings.

- [x] TODO-5773: arm64 register allocation keeps floats in d-registers
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Optimizing backends, native code quality (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md section 12)
  - parallel_track: native-arm64-regalloc
  - scope: arm64 float templates move both operands from general registers to d0/d1 and back for every operation (`fmov`), and float_series runs 8x the cycles of optexe. Colour float-only classes from v8-v31 (saving them around templates and calls), emit float arithmetic, compares, negation and conversions on them, and float constants without a general-register round trip, as the x86_64 allocator does with xmm2-xmm15. A float compare that feeds `JumpIfZero` becomes `fcmp` + `b.cond` (taken over from TODO-5771, whose template operand cache and compare-branch fusion would only have served float functions; docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.13).
  - acceptance:
    - float_series `/main` register-allocated on arm64 and at most 2x optexe's cycles, back to back with the load average recorded (2026-11-09: 8.2-8.4x)
    - float compile-run and math conformance suites green on macOS arm64
  - stop_rule: float values in functions TODO-5772 already allocates.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: The arm64 allocator takes functions with float values; every function of the benchmarks and PrimeScroll is register-allocated. `planNativeRegisterAllocation` takes the target's float registers (`RegAllocFloatRegisters`: pool and float compare-branch fusion; x86_64 keeps xmm2-xmm15 and no fusion). arm64 colours float-only classes from d8-d15, d16-d31, d2-d7 (d0/d1 scratch); d8-d15 are never saved around templates (AAPCS64 keeps them in C), d16-d31/d2-d7 are; across a call every live float register is saved like every general one; such functions get 64 save slots (dN at 32 + N). Values hold the general-register bit pattern (f32 in the low 32 bits) and move with `fmov`; real-call arguments stay in x0-x7. Inline `fadd`/`fsub`/`fmul`/`fdiv`/`fneg`, `scvtf`/`ucvtf`/`fcvtzs`/`fcvtzu` on 64-bit registers, `fcvt`, `ldr`/`str dN`, returns, `fcmp` + `cset` or fused `fcmp` + `b.cond` (MI/LS for </<=, false on NaN), constants as `fmov dN, xzr`/`#imm` or through x1. Back to back at load 14-16 (docs/OptimizingBackendsPlan.md 12.14): float_series 1294.8M -> 192.3M instructions, 410.5M -> 54.4M cycles (7.5x), 1.07x optexe's 50.7M; the other benchmarks and PrimeScroll (no floats; `__text` 220,928 bytes, 104/104 allocated) byte-identical. Tests (`primestruct.ir.native_codegen`): exact disassembly of a float loop with a fused compare-branch, floats live across a print and a call with mixed int/float arguments, every f32/f64 comparison of signed zeros, NaN and infinity (cset and fused, register and immediate), arithmetic and conversions against the VM. Full release gate green; `differential_opt_check.py --native --levels 1,2` (also PRIMESTRUCT_NATIVE_REGALLOC=1): 491 programs match, the same six -O0 programs differ as before (missing host libraries, VM fault checks).

- [x] TODO-5807: `[left right] wait(a, b)` destructures the results of several tasks
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Multi-Wait)
  - parallel_track: mt-tasks
  - scope: The prototype documents `[leftResult rightResult] wait(left, right)` as implemented, but the task-handle check rejects it with `task handles cannot escape their spawning function: left` (checked 2026-11-09 with build-release/primec); only `both{wait(left, right)}` with `both[0i32]` works. Treat a destructuring binding of `wait(...)` like any other consuming `wait`, so the handles are waited and the names bind the results in order.
  - implementation_notes: src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorTaskHandles.cpp (the escape check), the tuple-destructuring binding path; the lazy stdlib import must still find `/std/tuple` for it.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: a semantic test and a VM compile-run test destructure `wait(left, right)` of two `Task<i32>` and of a `Task<i32>` with a `Task<i64>`, and print both results
    - negative: destructuring into three names from two tasks is a diagnostic; using `left` after the destructuring `wait` is `task handle already waited: left`
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: destructuring of `wait` results only.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: In the template-monomorph statement walk, `[a b] wait(left, right)` (a `wait` call with two or more handles whose bracket entries are all fresh names) splits into a hidden `__wait_resultsN{wait(left, right)}` binding, placed at the first handle's position because local-auto type facts are keyed by position, and `[a b] __wait_resultsN`, which the existing tuple destructuring expands into `get` bindings. The handles are consumed as by any multi-wait; the VM and native print both results of a `Task<i32>` pair and of a `Task<i32>`/`Task<i64>` pair; three names from two tasks is `tuple destructuring arity mismatch: pattern has 3 names but tuple has 2 elements`; `wait(left)` afterwards is `task handle already waited: left`. Tests: three `primestruct.semantics.calls_flow.effects` cases and `runs vm with destructured multi task wait` (`primestruct.compile.run.vm.core`). Docs: MultithreadingPrototype.md Multi-Wait, TuplePrototype.md, spec/language-core.md.

- [x] TODO-5790: Spawn arguments are checked by type: only sendable values cross to a task
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Sendable Values)
  - parallel_track: mt-sendable
  - scope: `validateTaskSpawnCall` checks only argument names (a `mut` binding, a `Reference`, a task handle, `location`/`borrow`/`dereference`); a `Pointer`, a `StringView`, a borrowing closure or a struct holding one passes, and a copied `mut` binding is rejected although spawn copies its arguments. Add the structural `sendable` predicate of the design (primitives, `string`, enums, `String`, `vector`, `map`, `Maybe`, `Result`, tuples and structs of sendable types, every `Function`; not `Reference`, `Pointer`, `StringView`, slices, a closure struct with a `[ref]` capture, `Task`, `SharedString` until TODO-5656), one `[sendable]` struct transform (asserts it for a struct with `Pointer` fields; outside `stdlib/` only on a definition that also carries `unsafe`; no opt-out marker), and check the type of every spawn argument expression. Mark the stdlib `String`, `vector`, `map` and their kin `[sendable]`. Drop the `mut`-binding rejection: a `mut` argument is copied, as in any call. No marker on the native UI handles (their thread check is TODO-5808). A `String` made in an open `StringArena` passed to a spawn is `string escapes its arena via spawn`.
  - implementation_notes: src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorTaskHandles.cpp (the validator passes run after template monomorphization, so argument types are concrete); the arena escape check lives with the existing `string escapes its arena via ...` diagnostics. Diagnostics name the path to the leaf: `spawn argument `buffer` of /fill is not sendable: field `data` is Pointer<u8>`, with the chain of fields through nested structs, and `(instantiated from /pump at main.prime:12)` when the type came through a template argument; each carries the note `sharing between tasks needs Mutex, Atomic or Channel`. Make the predicate a reusable unit (`.h/.cpp`) since TODO-5791, TODO-5797 and TODO-5798 call it.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: semantic tests spawn calls taking a `String`, a `vector<i32>`, a struct of both, an enum, a `Function<i32, i32>` and a `mut` binding (the spawner's copy is unchanged after `wait`), and all examples/ and stdlib programs still compile
    - negative: semantic tests reject a `Pointer<u8>` argument, a `StringView`, a closure with a `[ref]` capture, a struct with a `Reference` field, a struct nested two levels deep holding a `Pointer` (the message names both fields), a generic callee instantiated with a `Slice<i32>` (the message carries the instantiation chain), `[sendable]` on a user struct without `unsafe`, and an arena-made `String`, each with the diagnostic shape of the design
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the sendable predicate, the `[sendable]` transform and the spawn-site check; the `Function` conversion, `Mutex` and `Channel` sites come with their own leaves.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: `src/semantics/SemanticsSendable.{h,cpp}` holds the structural predicate (`sendableViolation`, with a `SendableTypeLookup` of definition, field-type and display callbacks so TODO-5791/5797/5798 can call it): scalars, `string`, enums, `Function` (the `__FunctionN` box), builtin containers, tuples, sums and structs of sendable types are sendable; `Reference`, `Pointer`, `Task`, `StringView`, `Slice`/`ReadSlice`/`WriteSlice` and `SharedString` are not; a closure struct's fields are reported as captures, so a `[ref]` capture is `capture `total` is Reference<i32>`. `[sendable]` (struct only; outside `/std/` only with `unsafe` on the same definition) makes the walk look through `Pointer`/`Reference`/`uninitialized` fields at the pointee, so `vector<StringView>` stays non-sendable; it is on `String`, `Vector` (and so `map`), `RingBuffer` and `SoaColumn`. `validateTaskSpawnCall` drops the name-based `mut`/`Reference`/`location` rejections (task handles keep theirs) and checks each argument against the concrete parameter type of the callee instance it binds to: `spawn argument `job` of /render is not sendable: field `rows` of Job, field `bytes` of PixelRows is Pointer<u8>; sharing between tasks needs Mutex, Atomic or Channel`. Template instances now record their template, resolved arguments and first requester on the `Definition` (`instanceOfTemplate`, `instanceTemplateArgs`, `instanceRequestedBy`), which gives `/consume<ReadSlice<i32>>` and `(instantiated from /relay<ReadSlice<i32>>, instantiated from /main)`; no line numbers, since message text cannot map expanded-source positions back to files. A place passed to a borrowed `[T mut]` parameter is rejected (`... is a mutable borrow of the spawner's value: a task works on copies, take it as `[i32 copy mut]``), recorded as open question 7 of the prototype doc. Slice loans: `string escapes its arena via spawn (arena: arena)`. Tests: 15 cases in `primestruct.semantics.calls_flow.sendable`, `runs vm with sendable spawn arguments copied into the task`, and `examples/borrow_checker_negative/04_spawn_argument_not_sendable` (golden JSON diagnostic); the two old name-based effects cases are gone. Docs: MultithreadingPrototype.md (Captures, Sendable Values, open question 7, checklist), spec/language-core.md (Sendable spawn arguments, runnable example), spec/transforms.md. Found TODO-5826 (`return(wait(task))` with a struct local).

- [x] TODO-5826: `return(wait(task))` waits once in a definition with a struct local
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Multithreading (found while testing TODO-5790)
  - parallel_track: mt-tasks
  - scope: In a definition that has a struct-typed local (`[Rows] rows{Rows{}}`, or a `StringArena`), `return(wait(task))` is rejected with `task handle already waited: task` at the `wait`: the return expression is validated twice, and the second visit sees the handle consumed. `[i32] result{wait(task)}` then `return(result)` works. Validate the return's task-handle effects once. Deferred: the binding form is a workaround and no example needs the other.
  - acceptance:
    - a semantic test spawns with a struct local and `return(wait(task))`, and a VM compile-run test prints its result
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the double validation of the return expression only.
  - result: the struct local was not the cause: any definition rejected `return(wait(task))`. The parser keeps a top-level `return(value)` both as a statement and as `Definition::returnExpr`, and `validateDefinition` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorPassesDefinitions.cpp) validated `returnExpr` again after the statement loop had validated the return statement, so the second `wait` found the handle consumed. Both visits stay (skipping the second one lost diagnostics that only it reports, such as `count<bool>` on a `Vector<i32>` return), but the second one now starts from the task-handle states saved just before the last top-level return statement and restores the states after it, so the return's task-handle effects happen once. Tests: `return(wait(task)) waits once, with or without a struct local` in `primestruct.semantics.calls_flow.sendable` (with and without a struct local, and a second `wait` in the return still rejected) and `runs vm returning a waited task result next to a struct local` in `primestruct.compile.run.vm.core`.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10

- [x] TODO-5791: Every `Function` is sendable by construction: the conversion rejects non-sendable captures
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Functions Are Sendable)
  - parallel_track: mt-closures
  - scope: A `Function<R, A...>` erases its closure's type, so sendability cannot be read off a `Function` value; instead every conversion to `Function`, explicit (`Function<i64>{closure}`) and implicit (a closure at an expected-type site), rejects a closure with a capture that is not sendable (TODO-5790's predicate): a `Pointer`, a `StringView`, a slice, a struct holding one. `[ref]` captures are already rejected (`closure escapes via Function`). `Function` itself is then sendable, with no second function type and no per-instance inference. Routers, `AppRouter`, `WebApp`, `AppHandlers` and `serveWorkers` keep plain `Function`.
  - implementation_notes: the conversion sites (src/semantics/TemplateMonomorphCallableRewrites.cpp, `convertArgument`; src/semantics/SemanticsValidateLambdaClosures.cpp, `rewriteFunctionTypes`) rewrite the closure into `/__fn_box(closure)` while the closure struct's field types are still the template parameters the factory call infers, so the check runs in the `function-values` pass (src/semantics/SemanticsValidateFunctionValues.cpp, `rewriteFunctionValues`), which runs after template monomorphization and already visits every `/__fn_box` with its concrete closure struct; report at the box's source position, which is the closure's. The loan-based check in src/semantics/SemanticsValidateSliceLoans.cpp (`checkFunctionBoxes`) stays.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: a lambda with value and `mut` captures of sendable types (a `String`, a `vector<i64>`, a struct of both) and a named definition convert to `Function<i64, i64>` at a parameter, a field and a binding, and run on the VM, native and `optexe`; a `Function` passes as a spawn argument; all examples/ and stdlib programs still compile
    - negative: a closure capturing a `Pointer<u8>`, a `StringView` or a struct with a `Pointer` field is `closure cannot be a Function: capture `view` is a StringView` (with the field path for the struct), at the closure's line, at an explicit `Function<...>{...}` and at an implicit site, and under single-threaded `serve` too
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the capture check at `Function` conversion; no new function type.
  - result: the check runs in the validator, not the `function-values` pass: when it validates a call to a `/__fn_box` instance (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExpr.cpp, phase 1), `validateFunctionBoxSendable` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorTaskHandles.cpp) reads the box's concrete closure parameter type and runs TODO-5790's `sendableViolation` with the same struct, sum and field-type lookups as a spawn (`sendableTypeLookup`, shared with the spawn check), which the function-values pass cannot do for user struct fields; the diagnostic is at the box's source position, the closure's for an inline lambda and the conversion's for a named closure: `closure cannot be a Function: capture `view` is StringView`, `closure cannot be a Function: capture `rows`, field `bytes` of PixelRows is Pointer<u8>`, `closure cannot be a Function: capture `raw` is Pointer<u8>` for a route handler under a single-threaded `serve`. The message drops the "a" of the design text, to match the spawn diagnostics. A named closure with a `[ref hits]` capture now reports `closure cannot be a Function: capture `hits` is Reference<i32>` before the loan check; an inline `[ref]` lambda keeps `closure escapes via Function (root: hits)`; the two lambda tests of the named form expect the new message. Running the positive case found that any closure holding a `String` double-freed when converted to `Function` (`string heap block freed twice`): `/__fn_box` took the closure as a borrowed parameter and `init` handed the caller's value to the heap block, so both destroyed it; the box now takes a `[C copy]` parameter. No example, fixture or stdlib program is rejected (all `examples/` and `tests/fixtures/` programs were compiled with the check). Tests: five cases in `primestruct.semantics.calls_flow.sendable` (a positive one with String, vector, struct and init `mut` captures at a parameter, a field and a binding, a named definition, and a `Function` spawn argument; Pointer at an explicit `Function<i32>{...}`, a struct's Pointer field at an implicit parameter site, a StringView capture in a returned `Function`, a route handler under `serve`), `Function values own sendable captures: a String, a vector and a struct of both` in `primestruct.program_matrix.closures_callables` (VM, native, optexe), and `examples/borrow_checker_negative/05_function_capture_not_sendable` (golden JSON). Docs: spec/value-lifecycle.md (Sendable by construction, runnable example), MultithreadingPrototype.md (Functions Are Sendable, diagnostics, checklist), spec/language-core.md. The `[mut values]` vector capture fault is noted on TODO-5681.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10

- [x] TODO-5681: A struct field built from a named vector local owns its storage once
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-06
  - phase: Closures follow-ups (split from TODO-5668)
  - parallel_track: collections-fields
  - scope: `KeepV{[seen] start}` with `start` a local `vector<i32>` copies the 5-slot vector record into the field with `ownsData` still set in both, so the local and the struct both free the buffer (`VM error: invalid heap free address in IR: 9223372036854775808` on VM; same on optexe). Related: a plain local copy `[vector<i32> mut] b{a}` aliases `a` (after `b.push(2i32)`, `a.count()` is 2, not 1), while a struct copy (`[KeepV mut] copy{k}`) and the stdlib `Vector<T>` spelling copy elements. Brace construction from a named vector local must copy its elements (value semantics, docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Copies), and the `vector<T>` local copy must too.
  - implementation_notes: the vector record layout is `IrLowererVectorRecordLayoutHelpers.h` (`ownsData` at slot 4); the struct copy of TODO-5668's matrix case `struct_vector_field_copy_and_closure` already copies a vector field's elements.
  - notes: the same fault shows with a closure: `[mut values]` capturing a `vector<i64>` local, then `values.push(x)` in the body and one call, stops the VM with `invalid heap free address in IR: 9223372036854792192` (found while testing TODO-5791; an init capture `[mut seen{vector<i64>()}]` and a read-only `[values]` capture work).
  - acceptance:
    - `KeepV{[seen] start}` then pushes on both run on VM, native and optexe with independent counts and no double free; `b{a}` then `b.push` leaves `a.count()` unchanged; a test that the original stays usable
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: vector copies at brace construction and local copy bindings; no change to move semantics.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: every copy of a builtin `vector<T>` from a place shared the source's buffer: a struct field built from a vector local (`KeepV{[seen] start}`), a struct copy holding one (`[KeepV mut] copy{k}`, which only looked fine in struct_vector_field_copy_and_closure because the source never grew), a closure's `[mut values]` / `[values]` capture (a closure field), and a `vector<T>` local `[vector<i32> mut] b{a}` (which was just `a`'s record pointer). A push that grew one record reallocated the shared buffer and left the other with a freed address (`invalid heap realloc address` on VM and optexe, a segfault on native). The struct layout now records the element type of a builtin vector field (`StructSlotFieldInfo::builtinVectorElementKnown` / `builtinVectorElementStructPath`), and the copy helpers (`emitStructCopyHelpersFromPtrs`, the new `emitStructFieldCopyHelpersFromPtrs` and `emitBuiltinVectorCopyFromPtrs`, IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) give the copy a buffer of its own with each element copied, running the element's copy helpers (String fields); they now take the instruction vector to emit the copy loops. A mutable `vector<T>` local initialized from another vector local copies the same way and is dropped at scope end; a read-only one stays a view. Struct assignment counts vector fields as needing a copy. Matrix cases (VM, native, optexe): struct_vector_field_from_named_local 206, vector_local_copy_binding 242, closure_mut_vector_capture_push 183 (a `Function`, a direct closure and a read-only capture), struct_vector_of_strings_copies 186. Sibling check of the 6bb298fdc `/__fn_box` String double free: the other synthesized and stdlib `init` sites take `move` or `copy` parameters, and the Function `Copy` helper copies through a binding. Found TODO-5827 (vector fields are never freed, so each copy leaks its buffer)

- [x] TODO-5827: A builtin `vector<T>` struct field frees its buffer when the struct is destroyed
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Collections (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Copies; found by TODO-5681)
  - parallel_track: collections-fields
  - scope: A builtin `vector<T>` local frees its buffer (and destroys its struct elements) at scope end, but a `vector<T>` field of a struct, including a closure's by-value capture and a `Function` box's closure, is never freed when its struct is destroyed: `structNeedsDestroyHelpers` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) sees the field's `/vector` path, which has no destroy helper. Since TODO-5681 every copy of such a field from a place has a buffer of its own, so each copy leaks one block (and the `String` elements of a `vector<Named>` field).
  - implementation_notes: the field's element type is `StructSlotFieldInfo::builtinVectorElementStructPath` / `builtinVectorElementKnown` (set in IrLowererStructSlotLayoutHelpers.cpp); the local drop is `emitBuiltinVectorDrop` in src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerReturnInfo.h. A destroy must skip records whose `ownsData` is 0 and must not run for moved-from or disarmed temporaries.
  - acceptance:
    - a struct with a `vector<i32>` field and one with a `vector<Named>` field (String inside), each built from a local, copied and destroyed in a loop, run on VM, native and optexe without a double free, and the VM heap block count returns to its start (a test that counts live blocks)
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: destroying builtin vector fields of structs and closures; no change to copies.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: `structNeedsDestroyHelpers` now counts builtin `vector<T>` fields with a known element type (like the copy check of TODO-5681), and `emitStructDestroyHelpersFromPtr` destroys such a field with the new `emitBuiltinVectorDestroyAtDepth`: when `ownsData` is set and the data pointer is not 0 it destroys each element (recursively, so String elements and vector fields of struct elements), frees the buffer and clears `ownsData` and the data pointer; a view or disarmed temporary frees nothing, and a move hands the record over through the existing drop flags. The destroy helpers now take the instruction vector (they emit jumps). Second leak found by the new counter: a lambda converted to `Function` boxes `/__fn_box(block() { /closure_make(captures) })`, and `isOwnedTemporaryArgumentExpr` did not see the block's temporary, so the `[C copy]` parameter copied the closure and the temporary leaked its captures (one vector buffer per box, and since 6bb298fdc every String capture's bytes); a `block` whose only body expression is an owned temporary is now one too. Leak counter: `heapLiveBlocks()` in /std/text (string heap statistic 8, `VmHeapCore::liveSlotAllocationCount`), VM and optexe. Measured over 200 rounds before/after: KeepV copy+move round +400 blocks / 0, `vector<Named>` table round +400 blocks and +38400 string bytes / 0 and 0, closures and Function boxes +1000 blocks / 0, `vector<KeepV>` in a struct +1200 / 0. Tests: closures_fields matrix cases vector_field_copy_destroy_loop 141, vector_field_of_strings_destroy_loop 24, closure_vector_capture_destroy_loop 124 (VM and optexe, heap flat over 300 rounds) and vector_field_destroy_once_loop 32 (VM, native, optexe: copies, moves, `vector<KeepV>` in a struct, Function box copies, 200 rounds); runtime test for statistic 8. Spec: value-lifecycle.md (vector fields own their buffer), strings-and-views.md (`heapLiveBlocks`). Found while testing, followed up separately: a `[vector<T> copy]` parameter aliases the caller's vector, and a builtin `vector<vector<T>>` does not lower.

- [x] TODO-5829: A builtin `[vector<T> copy]` parameter owns a buffer of its own
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Collections (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Parameter Passing; found by TODO-5827)
  - parallel_track: collections-fields
  - scope: `[vector<i32> copy] v` bound the caller's vector record (the parameter local held the argument's record pointer), so `v.push(...)` in the callee grew the caller's vector, and returning `v` (`pass_on(a)`) left two owners of one buffer: `invalid heap free address` on the VM. A fresh vector argument (`grow(vector<i32>(1i32))`) or `move(a)` was never freed.
  - acceptance:
    - a named, field, fresh and moved argument to a `[vector<T> copy]` parameter, a callee that returns it or stores it in a struct, on the VM, native and optexe, with independent counts and the heap flat over a loop
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: builtin vector `copy` parameters, and fresh vectors passed to read-only vector parameters.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: the inline call binding (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerInlineCallsBody.h) now gives a `[vector<T> copy]` parameter with a known element type its own record and buffer (`emitBuiltinVectorCopyFromPtrs`, so String elements are copied) unless the argument is a fresh vector or `move(x)`, and registers a drop entry so the callee's scope frees it; a fresh vector passed to a read-only `vector<T>` parameter is freed there too. `move`, `mut` parameters are unchanged. Vector `copy` parameters are never real calls (`isAddressAbiParameter` rejects template-typed owned parameters), so only the inline path needed it. The fresh-vector test of the binding lowering moved to `isFreshBuiltinVectorExpr` (IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) for both. Before: a callee's three pushes reached the caller (`a.count()` 606 after 201 calls instead of 2), `pass_on(a)` double-freed, and a temporary or moved argument leaked one block per call. Tests: parameter_modes matrix cases builtin_vector_copy_parameter_owns_buffer 45 (VM, native, optexe; named, field, fresh, moved arguments, a struct and a vector returned from the callee) and builtin_vector_copy_parameter_heap_flat 23 (VM, optexe; `vector<Named>` copies, heap flat over 300 rounds). Spec: value-lifecycle.md (Parameter Passing). Found: a discarded constructor statement is never destroyed (TODO-5830).

- [x] TODO-5830: A discarded struct constructor statement destroys its temporary
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Collections (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Ownership; found by TODO-5829)
  - parallel_track: lifecycle-temporaries
  - scope: A struct constructor used as a statement, whose value is discarded, is never destroyed: `repeat(10i32) { Named{[name] stringFrom("...")} }` keeps 480 bytes of String on the heap and `KeepV{[seen] vector<i32>(1i32)}` one vector buffer per round (`stringHeapBytesInUse()` / `heapLiveBlocks()`, VM). The same shape hides in a definition whose body is only bindings, such as `[effects(heap_alloc)] round() { [vector<i32> mut] v{vector<i32>(1i32)} }`: the lowering treats it as an implicit struct and `round()` as a discarded constructor, so its owning bindings leak. A discarded call result (`make_keep()`) is destroyed. The spec says a discarded temporary is destroyed at the end of its statement.
  - acceptance:
    - discarded `Named{...}` and `KeepV{...}` statements and a bindings-only definition called as a statement leave `heapLiveBlocks()` and `stringHeapBytesInUse()` flat over a loop on the VM and optexe, and run without a double free on native
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: destroying discarded constructor temporaries; whether a bindings-only definition should be a struct at all is out of scope.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: the statement lowering (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerStatementsLoops.h) now destroys every discarded owning temporary at the end of its statement. `discardedTemporaryStructPath` (IrLowererLowerReturnEmitStage.cpp) adds struct constructor shapes to the discarded call results of `ownedTemporaryStructPath`: a `Named{...}` statement, and a bindings-only definition called as a statement (an implicit struct whose constructor builds its bindings), are emitted and then destroyed with `emitStructDestroyHelpersFromPtr`, so a user `Destroy` runs at the end of the statement and String and vector fields are freed. The sweep found one more shape: a discarded fresh builtin vector (`vector<i32>(1i32, 2i32)`, a user definition or method returning `vector<T>`) leaked its buffer; it is now freed through the builtin vector drop, destroying struct elements first. Already destroyed before: discarded call and method results returning structs with owned fields, String results, and `Function` boxes (closures and `make_fn()` results). Measured on the VM over 10 rounds before/after: `Named{...}` 560 string bytes / 0, `KeepV{...}` 10 blocks / 0, `round()` bindings-only 10 / 0, `make_vec()` and `vector<i32>(...)` 10 / 0 each, `keep.fresh()` 10 / 0. Tests: lifecycle matrix cases discarded_temporaries_heap_flat 23 (VM, optexe; constructor statements with fresh, named, field and moved arguments, nested constructors, a bindings-only call, discarded vectors and vector-returning calls and methods, heap and string heap flat over 300 rounds) and discarded_constructor_destroy_once (VM, native, optexe; `Destroy` runs once at the end of the statement, 500 rounds of discarded vector temporaries without a double free). Spec: value-lifecycle.md (Ownership).

- [x] TODO-5828: A builtin `vector<vector<T>>` lowers, copies and frees its inner vectors
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Collections (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Copies; found by TODO-5827)
  - parallel_track: collections-fields
  - scope: A builtin `vector<vector<i32>>` does not lower at all on the VM: `[vector<vector<i32>> mut] a{vector<vector<i32>>()}` then `a.push(vector<i32>(1i32, 2i32))` and `a[0i32].count()` stops with `VM lowering error: count() argument resolves to a non-string value`, and a struct field of that type with `vm backend cannot resolve struct layout: /vector<i32>`. So the inner vectors have no element layout (a vector record is 5 slots), and the copy and destroy of TODO-5681 / TODO-5827 skip such fields (`builtinVectorElementKnown` is false for a nested collection, IrLowererStructSlotLayoutHelpers.cpp and IrLowererLowerStatementsBindingsContinued.h). Vectors of structs with vector fields (`vector<Mid>`, `Mid{[vector<i32>] seen, [vector<Named>] names}`) already copy and free each level (matrix case nested_vector_struct_elements_destroy_loop).
  - acceptance:
    - `vector<vector<i32>>` locals and struct fields push, index, count, copy (each inner vector its own buffer) and destroy (every inner buffer freed, `heapLiveBlocks()` flat over a loop) on the VM, native and optexe
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: builtin vectors whose element is a builtin vector; the stdlib `Vector<Vector<T>>` spelling is out of scope.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: an inner vector of a builtin `vector<vector<T>>` is an inline five-slot vector record. Its element struct path is the synthetic `/vector<E>` (E the inner element's struct path or scalar name; `resolveBuiltinVectorElementStructPath`, `isNestedBuiltinVectorElementPath` in IrLowererStructTypeHelpers), which the struct layout fallback sizes as a vector record, so locals (`vectorStructElementSlotCount` 5), struct fields (`builtinVectorElementKnown`), `[vector<vector<T>> copy]` parameters, discarded vectors and returns all see the element. The copy and destroy helpers (IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) treat a `/vector<E>` element as a builtin vector: a copy gives each inner vector its own buffer (recursively, so `vector<vector<Named>>` copies its Strings) and a destroy frees each inner buffer. `push` stores the inner record inline: it takes over a fresh vector, `move(v)` or an owning binding (whose drop flag it releases) and copies any other place through the new `emitPushedVectorCopy` callback. `a[i].count()` reads slot 1 of the element (`isNestedBuiltinVectorElementAccess`, also exempting a field receiver from the wrapper-returned struct check), `a[i][j]` already indexed. A `vector<vector<T>>` return lowers like a vector of structs. A push that grows a buffer now also sets `ownsData`: an empty `vector<T>()` of struct or vector elements starts without a buffer, so a struct field holding it never freed what pushes allocated (the parameter_modes case real_call_function_move now also sees the closure in a `Table`'s `vector<Slot>` field destroyed, one more `Destroy` line). Before: `a[0i32].count()` stopped with `count() argument resolves to a non-string value`, a pushed vector was stored as a pointer to its (stack) record, so elements aliased one record and a later copy faulted (`unaligned indirect address`), and a `Grid{vector<vector<i32>>}` field leaked 2 blocks per round. Tests: container_copies matrix cases nested_builtin_vector_values 7 (VM, native, optexe; locals, fields, copy and read-only parameters, returns, `vector<vector<Named>>`, 200 rounds) and nested_builtin_vector_heap_flat 23 (VM, optexe; `heapLiveBlocks()` and the string heap flat over 300 rounds). Spec: value-lifecycle.md (Copies). Found: a mutating method on an inner vector (`a[0i32].push(5i32)`) has no semantic method target: TODO-5831.

- [x] TODO-5832: A binding pushed into a builtin vector is moved-from afterwards
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Collections (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Ownership; reported after TODO-5828)
  - parallel_track: lifecycle-moves
  - scope: `push` takes its argument as `move`, and the lowering hands a named owner's buffer or struct to the vector (its drop flag is released), but semantics never marked the name as moved for a builtin `vector<T>` receiver: after `rows.push(row)` the program could push to, read or destroy `row` again (`VM error: invalid heap free address`), also inside a loop or after an `if` that pushed it. The stdlib `Vector<T>` and user `[T move]` parameters were checked.
  - acceptance:
    - using a binding after a builtin vector push (method or direct call; vector of vectors, vector of a struct holding a String) is `use-after-move` on every path, with a diagnostic naming the binding, where it moved and what to write instead; reassignment re-initializes it; views and borrowed parameters stay usable (they are copied)
    - semantic cases, a golden diagnostic example, and program-matrix cases on the VM, native and optexe with `heapLiveBlocks()` flat
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the builtin vector push; other builtin container entry points are out of scope.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: after a call validates, `markBuiltinVectorPushMove` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorMoveOnly.cpp) records a named owning argument of a builtin `vector<T>` `push` as moved, like a `move` parameter; a borrowed parameter, a `Reference`, and a read-only collection view (new `BindingInfo::isPlaceView`: a non-`mut` collection bound from a binding, field or element) are skipped because the lowering copies them. The existing per-path tracking (if branches join, a loop that moves an outer binding) applies. `use-after-move` now says where the binding moved and what to write: `use-after-move: row was moved at 9:13; assign row a new value before using it again, or keep a copy before the move: [vector<i32> mut] kept{row}` (the copy hint is left out for move-only types); the loop form adds the position and "assign row a new value later in the loop body". `move(x)` records its site too, so a returned `move(v)` validated again by return inference is one move. Decision: the move stays implicit (`a.push(b)`, as for every `[T move]` parameter): every later use is rejected either way, so requiring `move(b)` would only add noise to the safe case; `move(b)` stays accepted. Tests: four primestruct.semantics.move cases, borrow_checker_negative/06_use_after_push_move (golden JSON), container_copies matrix cases push_moves_named_owners (VM, native, optexe) and push_moves_heap_flat (VM, optexe; heap and string heap flat over 300 rounds). Found: the direct form `push(rows, row)` on a `vector<vector<i32>>` stops in lowering with `missing semantic-product collection specialization` (the method form works).

- [x] TODO-5833: A builtin vector pushes its own elements, and its views keep it from changing
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Collections (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Copies; reported after TODO-5828)
  - parallel_track: lifecycle-moves
  - scope: `rows.push(rows[0i32])` on a `vector<vector<i32>>` or `vector<Named>` read the pushed element through its address in the buffer after the push had grown (reallocated) it (`VM error: invalid indirect address`), and a struct pushed from a place was copied slot by slot, so the vector and the place shared one String. A read-only view of an element (`[vector<i32>] view{rows[0i32]}`) borrowed nothing, so a later `rows.push(...)`, or pushing the view into `rows`, left it reading freed storage.
  - acceptance:
    - pushing an element of the same vector (vector of vectors, of structs with a String, a struct field) works on the VM, native and optexe with `heapLiveBlocks()` and the string heap flat
    - a builtin vector mutator on a root with a live view (also of an element or of a field's element) is a compile error naming the root and the view and saying what to write
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: builtin vector `push` aliasing and views of builtin vectors; the stdlib `Vector<T>` element push stays with TODO-5726.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: the push lowering (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerStatementsCallsStep.cpp) keeps the buffer's old address before it grows a vector of struct or vector elements, and when the payload address lies in the old buffer moves it to the same offset in the new one (two unsigned compares on the grow path only). A struct payload that is a place (not a temporary, `move(x)` or a named owner with a drop flag) now runs the copy helpers after the slot copy (`emitStructCopyHelpersFromPtrs`), so `names.push(names[0i32])` and a borrowed `[Named]` parameter pushed into a vector each own their String. Semantics: a non-`mut` collection bound from an element or a field's element (`containerPlaceRoot` strips field and `at` accesses) is now a container view of the root binding, replacing the `isPlaceView` flag of TODO-5832; `checkBuiltinVectorMutation` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorMoveOnly.cpp) rejects a builtin vector `push`/`pop`/`clear`/`reserve`/`remove_*` whose receiver root has a live view: `borrowed binding: rows (root: rows, sink: view); rows cannot change while view still reads it: declare view `mut` to give it its own copy`. Pushing a view into its own vector is rejected the same way; `rows.push(rows[0i32])` just works. Before: `rows.push(rows[0i32])` 40 times faulted on the VM, `names.push(names[0i32])` faulted. Tests: two primestruct.semantics.move cases, container_copies matrix cases push_own_elements (VM, native, optexe) and push_own_elements_heap_flat (VM, optexe; heap and string heap flat over 100 rounds). Found: `pop()` on a `vector<vector<i32>>` field is `unknown call target: pop`.

- [x] TODO-5831: An inner vector of a builtin `vector<vector<T>>` mutates in place
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Collections (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md, Copies; found by TODO-5828)
  - parallel_track: collections-fields
  - scope: Since TODO-5828 an inner vector of a builtin `vector<vector<i32>>` is an inline record that pushes, indexes, counts, copies and frees, but a mutating method on an element (`a[0i32].push(5i32)`, `g.rows[0i32].push(6i32)`) stops with `missing semantic-product method-call target: push`: semantics publishes no method target for a mutation through an element access. Today the only way to change an inner vector is to copy it out (`[vector<i32> mut] row{a[0i32]}`), change the copy and rebuild the outer vector.
  - acceptance:
    - `push`, `pop` and `clear` on `a[i]` and `this.rows[i]` change that inner vector in place on the VM, native and optexe, and `heapLiveBlocks()` stays flat over a loop
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: mutating methods on inner vectors of builtin vectors; assigning a whole inner vector (`assign(a[i], ...)`) is out of scope.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: semantics now resolves a mutating method on an element of a builtin vector of vectors to the canonical vector helper (`/std/collections/vector/push`, as for a named vector; the element branch of `resolveMethodTarget` published `/vector/push`), and a rewritten no-payload mutator (`a[i].clear()`) keeps its statement context, so it is no longer `unknown call target: clear`. The existing push and pop/clear lowering then works on the element record in place. Found while testing: a `mut` binding of an element or a field (`[vector<i32> mut] row{a[0i32]}`, `[vector<i32> mut] items{bag.items}`) aliased the record, so changing it changed the source (and `a[0i32].clear()` emptied the "copy"); it now gets its own record and buffer like a binding from a named vector (IrLowererLowerStatementsBindingsContinued.h). In-place mutation also made a read-only root writable through an element (`[vector<vector<i32>>] rows` parameter, `rows[0i32].push(1i32)`), and a builtin vector mutator never checked the root's mutability at all (`[vector<i32>] b`, `b.push(2i32)` compiled): `checkBuiltinVectorMutation` now rejects a builtin mutator (not a same-named user definition such as a `/soa/push` shadow) whose root is neither `mut` nor a `copy`/`move` parameter (variadic packs, `Reference` and `Pointer` roots are left to their own rules): `push changes rows, which is not mutable: declare it [vector<vector<i32>> mut] rows`; a live view of an element blocks the mutation (TODO-5833). Tests: primestruct.semantics.move case (positive locals, fields, `this.rows[i]`; negative read-only parameter and live view), container_copies matrix cases inner_vector_mutations (VM, native, optexe; push in a loop, pop, clear, methods on `this.rows[i]`, `vector<vector<Named>>`, element and field copies) and inner_vector_mutations_heap_flat (VM, optexe; heap and string heap flat over 100 rounds). Found: pop/clear on vectors of multi-slot elements and the direct `push(v, x)` form (TODO-5834).
