  - stop_rule: pop, clear and direct push on builtin vectors of multi-slot elements.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: `pop` and `clear` on a builtin vector of multi-slot elements now lower in the vector mutation step (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerStatementsCallsStep.cpp) instead of falling back to the stdlib `Vector<T>` helper: `pop` decrements the count and destroys the element past it, `clear` destroys every element and sets the count to zero, each through the struct destroy helpers (user `Destroy`, String and nested builtin vector buffers; the element type comes from the receiver's element type text). Semantics: `grid.rows.pop()` was rewritten to the direct helper and revalidated outside its statement, so it was `unknown call target: pop`; a rewritten mutator on a vector field now keeps its statement context like `a[i].clear()` (SemanticsValidatorExprCallPhasesB.cpp), and the generic method-target fallback publishes the canonical `/std/collections/vector/pop` for a vector field instead of `/std/collections/vector/vector/pop` (SemanticsValidatorExprMethodTargetResolutionGenericFallback.cpp). Direct `push(rows, row)` on `vector<vector<i32>>`: the lowerer checks a collection specialization for every binding whose type is a collection, unwrapping `uninitialized<...>`, but semantics did not unwrap it when publishing, so the monomorphized stdlib buffer helpers (`Pointer<uninitialized<vector<i32>>>` parameters) had none; the publisher (SemanticPublicationBuildersRouting.cpp) now unwraps `uninitialized` too. Tests: primestruct.semantics.move case (field pop/clear, `this` fields, direct push/pop/clear; read-only root rejected), container_copies matrix cases dropping_vector_mutations (VM, native, optexe), dropping_vector_mutations_heap_flat (VM, optexe; heap and string heap flat over 100 rounds) and dropping_vector_mutations_destroy (every config; each dropped element's `Destroy` runs once, in order).

- [x] TODO-5792: The VM runs `TaskSpawn`/`TaskWait` IR under a deterministic scheduler
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Runtime Model and VM Scheduler)
  - parallel_track: mt-vm-tasks
  - scope: Add the IR opcodes `TaskSpawn <function>` (pops the function's parameters into the new task's own call stack, pushes the handle) and `TaskWait` (pops a handle, pushes the result, 0 for a void task; the handle is consumed), PSIR v34 (a v33 module still loads; the version note in docs/spec/vm-design.md). IR validation accepts them only for the vm target and serialized bytecode and rejects a `TaskWait` whose operand is not a `TaskSpawn` result on every path. The VM runs each task on its own call stack in one OS thread: switch points at spawn, wait and a task's end; the eager default schedule (a new task runs until it ends or blocks, then its spawner continues); `--vm-schedule-seed <n>` (and `PRIMESTRUCT_VM_SCHEDULE_SEED`) for seeded schedules; `deadlock: every task is waiting` (exit 3); a trap in any task ends the program. Lowering is unchanged in this slice (TODO-5835).
  - implementation_notes: src/runtime/VmExecutionKernel.cpp (`VmTaskScheduler`, used only for a module with task opcodes; the flat kernel and the JIT decline such modules), src/ir/IrValidation.cpp (`validateTaskWaitOperands`), src/ir/IrSerializer.cpp, src/support/OptionsParser.cpp.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: hand-built IR runtime tests (tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_tasks.cpp): the default schedule prints what inline calls print; seeds 0 to 31 are reproducible, keep each task's own order and pick more than one interleaving; a task that spawns and waits its own task; a void task; 10000 tasks spawned and waited in a loop; a trap in a task
    - negative: waiting a handle twice, a value that is not a handle and a missing task function fault; an IR validation test rejects `TaskWait` on a value that is not a task handle (directly and after a join) and the task opcodes on every other target; `--vm-schedule-seed` without a number is a CLI error; a v33 module still loads and a v33 module with task opcodes does not (the deadlock fault is covered by TODO-5798's channel test, since task handles cannot cross to let two tasks wait on each other)
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the IR opcodes, their validation and serialization, and the VM scheduler; lowering `[spawn]` to them is TODO-5835.
  - result: `TaskSpawn f` / `TaskWait` (PSIR v34; v33 modules still load and are written back as v34, and a v33 module with task opcodes is refused). `VmTaskScheduler` (src/runtime/VmExecutionKernel.cpp) runs only for a module with task opcodes (the flat kernel and the JIT decline it): each task is a VM call stack numbering its locals from 0, a handle is a tagged slot plus generation (slots are reused after the wait), switches happen at spawn, wait and a task's end; default eager (the new task runs, then its spawner, else the lowest-numbered runnable task), seeded with splitmix64 (`--vm-schedule-seed <n>` on primec and primevm, `PRIMESTRUCT_VM_SCHEDULE_SEED`; `Vm::setScheduleSeed`); no runnable task is `deadlock: every task is waiting`; a trap in any task ends the program; a debug session faults on the task opcodes. Validation: only the vm target and serialized bytecode take them, `TaskSpawn` needs a valid function, and a must-analysis (`validateTaskWaitOperands`) rejects `task wait operand is not a task handle`. Tests: 10 cases in `primestruct.runtime.tasks` (tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_tasks.cpp), the seed flag case in `primestruct.options.optimization`, the task stack effects in the CFG coverage case. Docs: docs/spec/vm-design.md (Tasks, PSIR v34), MultithreadingPrototype.md status.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10

- [x] TODO-5835: `[spawn]` lowers to `TaskSpawn`/`TaskWait` for the VM
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Runtime Model and VM Scheduler)
  - parallel_track: mt-vm-tasks
  - scope: `[spawn] f(...)` is lowered inline today (IrLowererLowerStatementsBindings.h) and `Task<void>` fails in lowering (`void call not allowed in expression context`). For the vm target and serialized bytecode, a spawned callee whose parameters and result real calls take as scalars becomes a real call started with `TaskSpawn` (its arguments evaluated in the spawner and copied to the task), and every read of the handle (`wait(t)`, the multi-wait tuple, `[a b] wait(x, y)`, `return(wait(t))`) is a `TaskWait`; `Task<void>` works. Every other backend keeps running the spawned call inline, as today.
  - implementation_notes: Keep the semantic-product task facts as the lowering's input. A callee whose parameters or result real calls cannot take yet (struct and sum results, TODO-5738; `string` parameters, TODO-5741; `mut` parameters) keeps running inline with an `--opt-report` reason, which is a legal schedule.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: VM compile-run tests of two `Task<i64>` sums, a `Task<void>`, a spawn inside a loop waited per iteration, and the existing task tests give the same output on the default schedule and on seeds 1 to 8; native and `optexe` run the same programs inline with the same output; `--opt-report` names a spawn that runs inline and why
    - negative: waiting a handle twice and letting one escape are still rejected
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the lowering for the VM; threads on `optexe` are TODO-5795, on native TODO-5799.
  - result: src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererTaskSpawn.{h,cpp} decides (`taskSpawnRunsOnScheduler`: not the entry, not a host function or method, the scalar real-call shape on both ABIs) and reserves the callee as a real call (`reserveTaskSpawnTarget`); IrLowererLowerStatementsBindings.h evaluates the arguments in parameter order, emits `TaskSpawn` with the real-call placeholder (fixed up and kept reachable in IrLowererLowerStatementsCallsStage.cpp) and binds a `LocalInfo::isTaskHandle` local; every read of it in emitExpr is `TaskWait`, so `wait(t)`, the multi-wait tuple, `[a b] wait(x, y)` and `return(wait(t))` need nothing else. Inline spawns note `lowering: spawn of /f runs inline: <reason>` in `--opt-report` (IrOptimizationReport::loweringNotes, kept across the passes). A `Task<void>` spawn that runs inline (every other backend, or a non-scalar callee) is the call as a statement with a 0 in the binding. Tests: 5 cases in `primestruct.program_matrix.tasks` (two i64 sums, `Task<void>`, a spawn in a loop, multi-wait/destructured/returned waits, inline fallbacks) on the VM kernels, native, optexe and VM seeds 1 to 8 (`ExecutionConfig::vmFlags`); the five existing VM task compile-run tests now run on the default schedule and seeds 1 to 8 (`vmTaskScheduleFlags`); `the opt report names a spawned call that runs inline on the vm`. Found TODO-5836 (a spawned definition without `return` is not a `Task<void>`) and TODO-5837 (debug sessions do not step tasks).
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10

Now 0.07 s. `PrimeStruct_primestruct_semantics_move` (30 s): 1.9-2.4 s; no one case is slow, cases 16-23 validate through the stdlib import pipeline at ~0.2 s each. - [x] TODO-5839: A 17-field `SoaSchemaStorage` program builds as `exe` in under 5 s
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Test hygiene (found while doing TODO-5838)
  - parallel_track: exe-codegen-size
  - scope: The `--emit=exe` build of the SoaSchema storage program (tests/unit/compile_run/test_compile_run_reflection_codegen_runtime.cpp, case `... on exe`, CTest `reflection_codegen_24_24`) takes 19 s alone and 149 s in a loaded gate (300 s timeout); the VM and native builds of it take 1 s. The IR lowerer inlines the 17 column constructors of `/Wide/SoaSchemaStorageNew` into `/main` (84131 instructions, 9293 locals; a program that only constructs the storage is still 79k), and the exe emitter (src/backend/IrToCppEmitter.cpp, a `switch` case per instruction) turns that into 45 MB of C++ that clang++ -O0 compiles in ~18 s. Merging straight-line cases (fallthrough) and dropping the `psEnsureStack` checks cut the compile by only 25%. The test cannot be split: one build is the whole cost.
  - acceptance:
    - that build takes under 5 s alone (smaller IR for the storage helpers, or a more compact exe emission), and the case has no over-guardrail note
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the SoaSchema storage exe build; other programs' exe size only as far as the same change shrinks them.
  - notes: deferred: the case runs at a sixth of its timeout alone, with a guardrail note. Aside: a one-field `SoaSchemaStorage` program fails exe lowering with `native backend only supports arithmetic/... calls in expressions (call=/std/collections/soa_storage/soaColumnNew ...)`.
  - result: Root causes, all general: (1) an inlined call lowered its callee's `return(value)` twice: the parser keeps the value in `Definition::returnExpr` too, and runLowerInlineCallActiveContextStep appended it as an implicit return after the body, an unreachable copy that doubled the code at each inlining level (three nested struct-returning helpers: 561 instructions, now 129); it now runs only when the body has no top-level `return`. (2) A body ending in `return` emitted its scope cleanup again after the last statement (emitEntryCallableExecutionWithCleanup), an unreachable second copy of every destructor in `/main` and in real-call bodies. (3) The stdlib re-checked shapes it had just checked: `soaColumnsNCheckShape` called `soaColumnCount`/`soaColumnCapacity` (each a full shape check of a copied column) 4(N-1) times, `soaColumnsNCount`/`Capacity`, `soaColumnClear` and `SoaColumn.Destroy` checked again before `soaColumnDropRange` checks; they read the fields now (same panics). (4) On `exe`, `panic` was inlined at every site because the scalar ABI had no `never` result and the simple C++ emitter no `Trap`: it now runs `Trap` (flush, `std::exit(code)`), IR validation takes it for the Cpp target, runtime errors in real calls trap there (fixing the exe half of TODO-5744: a bounds error in a called function returned 3 to the caller, which carried on), and `never` definitions are scalar real calls on targets that trap (`LowerSetupStageState::runtimeErrorsTrap`). (5) The exe emitter wrote LoadIndirect/StoreIndirect as 26-28 lines each (46% of the file) and integer arithmetic/compares as 9: they are calls to shared helpers now (`psLoadIndirect`, `psStoreIndirect`, `CppIntegerOpHelpers`). Numbers for the 17-field program (before -> after): exe IR 172397 -> 47066 instructions; VM IR 101461 (`/main` 84131) -> 39016 (`/main` 29k), native and optexe the same as VM; `--emit=cpp` 44876498 -> 5932785 bytes; exe build 19-25 s alone -> 2.5-3.7 s alone at load ~14 (native 0.5 s, optexe 2.3 s). Separate bug: a one-field storage built its chunk with a generated `soaColumnNew<T>()` call that nothing instantiated, so it failed to lower on every backend; the chunk is `SoaColumn<T>{}` now, like wider chunks. Tests: `reflection SoaSchema storage program lowers and emits within a size budget` (exe IR < 60000, VM IR < 50000, C++ < 8 MB), `reflection SoaSchema storage with one field runs on vm, native and exe`, `ir inlines nested struct returns without an unreachable copy of each return`, `ir emits the scope cleanup of a body ending in return once`, `exe-ir emitter ends the program on a runtime error inside a called function`; emitter text checks updated for the helpers; the guardrail note on the exe case removed. Docs: vm-design.md (Errors, scalar ABI `never`), runtime-model.md; TODO-5744 narrowed to Wasm and GLSL.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10

- [x] TODO-5793: Byte-region accesses check liveness without a map search or a write
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Heaps)
  - parallel_track: mt-heap-optexe
  - scope: `VmHeapCore::byteAt` (include/primec/runtime/VmHeapCoreBody.inc) finds a byte region with a `std::map` search unless the address is in the last region found, and writes that cache on every lookup, so even a read mutates the heap and a loop over two regions searches the map on every byte. Give byte regions a state table indexed by the address, like the slots' (`states`): the region's generation for each granule, so a check is one array load and compare and writes nothing. Keep the addresses, the fault messages and the VM/`optexe` parity.
  - acceptance:
    - a VM benchmark that copies between two byte regions byte by byte (add it under benchmarks/) runs at least 2x faster; `benchmarks/aggregate.prime` and the strings benchmarks are no slower on the VM and `optexe`
    - positive: tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_byte_regions.cpp gains a case that interleaves accesses to three regions and checks every byte; negative: an access to a freed region, to a region reused with a new generation and one byte past a region's end still fault with today's messages
    - the PrimeScroll `optexe` peak footprint (close:1 median, as in TODO-5751) grows by at most 5%; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the byte-region liveness check; arenas and threads are TODO-5794.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: Byte regions have a state table like the slots: `VmHeapCore::byteStates` holds one uint32 per 64-byte granule (include/primec/runtime/VmHeapCoreBody.inc). While one live region alone has bytes in a granule its state is the region's `address >> 47` (tag, generation, byte bit), the range it fills there and a start bit, so `byteAt` is one load and one compare against a value built from the address, with no write; a granule a region only partly fills is checked against its range, and one shared by two regions (state 0) asks the live map. `byteSpan` walks the granules of a span up to 128 bytes and asks the map once for a longer one. The last-region cache is gone; allocation and free set the states of the granules they cover. Addresses, best-fit reuse and fault messages are unchanged (the exact-offset tests pass); `optexe` pastes the same code, and `ps_load_u8`/`ps_store_u8` try `byteAt` first since it checks the tag bits itself. Measuring first showed the VM spent its time elsewhere: a function with `LoadU8`/`StoreU8`/`HeapAllocBytes` or a `Trap` (every `panic`, so every `String` program) kept the whole module on the step kernel, at about 275 ns a byte, so the check alone gained nothing there. The flat loop now runs the three byte opcodes (through the same heap helpers, so the messages match) and `Trap` (src/runtime/VmFastKernel.cpp, VmFastKernelPrepare.cpp; docs/spec/vm-design.md). New benchmark benchmarks/strings/two_region_copy.{prime,cpp} (in scripts/benchmark_strings.py): copies a 1000-byte String into a 4000-byte one byte by byte, 1000 times, the two blocks in different chunks. CPU time, medians of interleaved runs against a build of the parent commit (VM 5 runs including the compile, optexe 31 runs): VM two_region_copy 1.263 s to 0.265 s (4.8x), aggregate 0.038 to 0.038, append_bytes 5.51 to 0.74, append_chunks 3.54 to 0.46, copy_text 0.82 to 0.28, insert_front 0.213 to 0.186, replace_middle 0.53 to 0.22, request_arena 12.0 to 1.6, request_heap 10.9 to 1.4, short_strings 9.3 to 1.05, every output the same; optexe two_region_copy 36.2 to 31.6 ms, aggregate 3.6 to 3.6, append_bytes 124.7 to 123.3, copy_text 28.0 to 28.3, insert_front 5.7 to 5.8, replace_middle 9.0 to 9.3, request_arena 251 to 246, request_heap 240 to 237, short_strings 182.2 to 182.5, but append_chunks 51.8 to 56.9 (+10%), filed as TODO-5840. 16-byte granules cost more on allocation and free (4x the state writes) and an inlined fast path made the optexe loops slower, so 64 bytes and an out-of-line `byteAt` stayed. PrimeScroll optexe headless editor (examples/apps/text_editor, headless backend, `close:1`, `/usr/bin/time -l` peak footprint, median of 20): 1,786,240 bytes before and after. Tests: test_vm_byte_regions.cpp gains three regions sharing granules accessed in turn on every byte (with spans and a free of the middle one) and one-past-a-region, freed and reused-generation faults with today's messages; test_ir_vm_fast_kernel.cpp gains byte loads and stores (byte regions, a u8 local, an unaligned local, a slot) and traps from main and two frames down, matched against the step kernel. Full release gate green.

- [x] TODO-5794: `optexe` heaps are per-thread arenas with remote frees
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Heaps)
  - parallel_track: mt-heap-optexe
  - scope: Make the generated program's heap usable from several threads: one `VmHeapCore` arena per thread with the arena number in address bits 41 to 46 (arena 0 keeps today's addresses), storage that does not move once a second arena exists, liveness reads with relaxed atomic loads of the state tables and a per-thread region cache, a remote-free queue per arena (behind the arena's lock, drained by the owner at its next allocation), and at most 64 arenas (a 65th thread shares the last behind its lock). Each thread gets its own `VmTextHeap` (its `StringArena` stack and literal cache), whose blocks freed elsewhere go back the same way.
  - implementation_notes: include/primec/runtime/VmHeapCore*.{h,inc}, VmTextHeap*.{h,inc}, src/backend/IrToOptCppRuntime.h (`Rt` splits into per-thread state and the shared arena table). The VM keeps one arena and no locks. Since TODO-5793 a byte access reads `byteStates` (one state per 64 bytes, indexed by the byte offset, address bits 0 to 46): arena bits 41 to 46 lie inside that offset, so each arena needs its own `bytes`/`byteStates` and the lookup must drop the arena bits first (or the table index becomes huge).
  - acceptance:
    - positive: a runtime unit test runs 4 `std::thread`s that each allocate, read and free 100000 slot and byte blocks and free a quarter of another thread's blocks, and checks live counts and contents at the end; a single-threaded `optexe` program gives the same addresses as the VM (the existing parity tests)
    - negative: a block freed twice from two threads faults with `invalid heap free address` when the owner drains; an address of a freed block read from another thread faults
    - single-threaded `optexe` benchmarks within 3% of before; the unit test passes under ThreadSanitizer in one manual run, recorded in docs/todo_log.md; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the `optexe` heap and string heap; starting threads is TODO-5795.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: A generated program's heap is one arena per thread, handed out by `VmHeapArenas` (include/primec/runtime/VmHeapArenas.h, VmHeapArenasBody.inc: `main`, `acquire`, `release`; at most 64, a 65th thread shares the last, whose `StringArena` scopes are then off). An arena is a `VmHeapCore` and a `VmTextHeap`; its number is in address bits 41 to 46, so arena 0 (the main thread, and the VM, which keeps one heap and no lock) has the addresses it had. Until a second arena is asked for, arena 0 is the old heap: growing vectors, no lock (`lock_ == nullptr` is the first test of every mutator). The first `acquire` pins it (`shareAsArena`): storage moves once into a reservation mapped without commit (64 GiB of bytes, 2^32 slots) and never again. Liveness reads are relaxed atomic loads of the state tables and write nothing, so there is no per-thread region cache; an address of another arena is answered by that arena's tables through a table of 64 arena pointers. A free of another arena's block goes on that arena's queue behind its lock (`releaseRemote`) and the owner frees the queue before its next allocation (`drainRemoteFrees`, `ps_heap_drain`), which is where a block freed twice faults with `invalid heap free address`; growing another arena's block allocates here, copies and frees there. `optexe`'s `Rt` holds references to its thread's heap, string heap and the arenas (src/backend/IrToOptCppRuntime.h); starting threads is TODO-5795. Tests: tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_heap_arenas.cpp (suite primestruct.runtime.heap_arenas, 6 cases, ctest PrimeStruct_vm_heap_arenas): arena 0 has the VM's addresses and arena 1 its number in bits 41 to 46; 4 threads allocate, read and free 100000 slot and byte blocks each and free a quarter of a neighbour's (handed over at joins, so every run is the same), live counts and contents checked; a block freed by two threads faults when the owner drains; a freed block's address read from another thread is no block; growing and measuring another arena's block; 3 threads sharing the last arena of a table of 2 behind its locks. ThreadSanitizer, one manual run (2026-11-10, `clang++ -std=c++23 -O1 -g -fsanitize=thread` of the test file and tests/unit/test_main.cpp): 6 cases, 1352 assertions passed, no report. Single-threaded `optexe` against the build before (e8a547504), same clang flags as the pipeline. Instructions (deterministic; `ri_instructions` of the program itself): append_bytes -6.9%, append_chunks +0.7%, short_strings +0.3%, insert_front +2.5%, copy_text +2.8%, replace_middle +1.2%, request_heap -1.7%, request_arena -1.4%, two_region_copy -5.8%, json_parse -1.2%, vector_sum -0.1% (aggregate and json_scan run 15M instructions, mostly start-up, and vary by 9% from run to run). CPU time, medians of 21 interleaved runs on a loaded machine (ms before, after): append_bytes 167.8 158.5 (-5.5%), append_chunks 79.1 82.4 (+4.2%), short_strings 257.9 267.0 (+3.6%), insert_front 8.5 8.0 (-5.8%), copy_text 28.3 29.4 (+3.7%), replace_middle 11.0 12.0 (+9.3%; minimum -3.1%), request_heap 281.0 303.7 (+8.1%), request_arena 334.8 352.7 (+5.3%), two_region_copy 47.2 46.5 (-1.5%), aggregate 4.1 3.9, json_parse 11.4 10.8, json_scan 3.5 3.6, vector_sum 30.1 29.8 (-1.1%). The 3% bound holds for instructions everywhere but not for CPU time: the medians moved by 10% between sessions and the cycles of one source by 15% with `-falign-functions`/`-falign-loops` (vector_sum: -9% to +14% after against before over five layouts), but request_heap, request_arena and short_strings take 4 to 7% more cycles under every layout with no more instructions, and that cost was not found (TODO-5844 lists what was ruled out). Lessons kept in comments: `ps_resolve` keeps its five arguments (a sixth, or a heap local in every function, stops clang inlining small functions: +19% instructions on append_chunks) and the byte check stays out of line in `ps_byte_at` (inlined: +8% instructions on short_strings). TODO-5840 (append_chunks since the granule table) stays open. Full release gate green.

  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Runtime Model)
  - parallel_track: mt-optexe-threads

    - negative: a fault in a task exits 3 with the VM's message while another task is still running; 4 tasks printing 1000 lines each never produce a mixed line
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: threads for `[spawn]` on `optexe`; the sharing primitives are TODO-5796 to TODO-5798.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: `--emit=optexe` and `--emit=optcpp` run each `TaskSpawn` on an OS thread. The lowering gate (`tasksRunOnScheduler`, src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSetupStage.cpp) and IR validation now take the optimized C++ target next to the VM and serialized bytecode. A module with a task opcode gets a task runtime in its source (`OptCppTaskRuntime`, src/backend/IrToOptCppRuntime.h) and one trampoline `ps_t<N>` per spawned function; a module without one has the source it had plus the fault and print changes below. `ps_task_spawn` takes a slot of a global table under a mutex (handles are the VM's: tag, generation, slot), acquires a heap arena for the task on the spawning thread (`VmHeapArenas::acquire`, so the first acquire is on the only thread) and starts a thread whose `Rt` has its own call depth, local-address frames and that arena; `ps_task_wait` checks the handle with the VM's messages (`invalid task handle in IR`, `task handle already waited`), joins, copies the result out and frees the slot; the thread releases its arena when it ends. Threads are made with `pthread_create` and an 8 MiB stack, not `std::thread`: a secondary thread gets 512 KiB on macOS and the call-depth limit is 4096 frames. `ps_fault` is single-shot (an atomic flag; a second faulting thread parks), flushes and leaves with `std::_Exit(3)`; in a task module a trap and the entry's return flush and use `std::_Exit` too, so no static destructor runs under a live thread. A print is one `fwrite` of text and newline (`ps_emit_line`; a `PrintString` literal has its newline baked in). The host compile adds `-pthread`. Docs: docs/spec/vm-design.md (Tasks on threads), docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md.
    - Tests (tests/unit/ir_pipeline/optimizer/test_ir_optexe_tasks.cpp, suite `primestruct.ir.optexe_tasks`, 8 cases, none reads a clock): race-free IR programs print what the VM prints; waiting twice and waiting on a non-handle fault with the VM's message; a task returns a heap block, a text-heap block and a slot block and the waiter reads and frees them; a division by zero in a task exits 3 with `VM error: division by zero in IR` while another task spins forever; a trap in a task (exit 7) and the entry's return (exit 5) end the program while a task spins; 4 tasks of 1000 turns of 3 lines each give 12000 whole lines; the task runtime is emitted only for task modules; `primec --emit=optcpp` lowers a scalar spawn to `ps_task_spawn`. The program-matrix task cases (tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_tasks.cpp) now run on threads in their `optexe` configs with the VM's output.
    - Manual timing (not a test), arm64 macOS, `primec --emit=optexe`, two tasks of 400 million loop turns each against one task doing both ranges, back to back, five rounds, wall clock in seconds, load average 22 to 26 at the time (other builds were running): one task 3.13, 3.04, 2.93, 3.12, 2.95; two tasks 1.52, 1.50, 1.49, 1.51, 1.54. Ratio 0.49 to 0.52 (limit 0.65).
    - ThreadSanitizer, one manual run: the 8 generated programs of the test suite built with `clang++ -std=c++23 -O1 -g -fsanitize=thread -pthread` and run alone gave their expected output and exit codes and no report.
    - Single-threaded cost, instructions retired (`/usr/bin/time -l`), source emitted before and after, `clang++ -O2 -ffp-contract=off`: append_chunks 1262851204 to 1260670348 (-0.17%), short_strings 4595585548 to 4599969889 (+0.10%), request_heap 5310810112 to 5314944175 (+0.08%), call_fib 221954535 to 222273576 (+0.14%), json_parse 118923088 to 118924495 (+0.00%).
    - Not met as written: "a task that allocates and returns a `String`" is tested at the IR level only (heap, text-heap and slot blocks), because the lowering runs a spawned call with a `String` parameter or result inline on every backend; TODO-5845 makes those tasks. `optexe` has no `deadlock: every task is waiting` fault (the program would hang); unreachable with `[spawn]` and `wait` alone, filed as TODO-5846.

- [x] TODO-5845: A spawned call with a `String` or struct parameter or result runs as a task
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Runtime Model)
  - parallel_track: mt-vm-tasks
  - scope: `[spawn] f(...)` lowers to `TaskSpawn` only when the callee has the scalar real-call shape (`taskSpawnRunsOnScheduler`, src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererTaskSpawn.h); a callee that takes or returns a `String`, a struct or a vector runs inline at the spawn, with a `--opt-report` note, on the VM and on `optexe`. So a web handler that builds a `String` on a task still runs on the spawning thread. Lower those spawns to `TaskSpawn` too: the arguments are copied (or moved) into the task before it starts, the result is handed to the waiter, and the waiter owns and frees it. On `optexe` the block was allocated in the task's arena and the waiter's free is a remote free.
  - implementation_notes: the real-call work for `string` parameters is TODO-5741; struct results come back through a caller-provided address, which for a task must be a block the task owns until `wait` copies it out. The IR-level heap handoff (a task returns a heap, text-heap or slot block and the waiter frees it) already passes on `optexe` (tests/unit/ir_pipeline/optimizer/test_ir_optexe_tasks.cpp).
  - acceptance:
    - positive: a task that builds and returns a `String` gives the waiter the text, on the VM (default schedule and seeds 1 to 8) and on `optexe`, and the heap is empty at exit (heap statistics); the same for a struct with a `String` field and for a `String` argument; `--opt-report` has no inline note for these spawns
    - negative: a spawn whose callee shape still cannot run as a task keeps its `--opt-report` note naming the reason
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: `String`, struct and vector parameters and results of spawned calls on the VM and `optexe`; native is TODO-5799.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: On the VM, serialized bytecode and `optexe` a spawned call whose parameters and result are scalars, `String`s, other structs outside the collections, or closures (`Function`, borrowed or `copy`/`move`) lowers to `TaskSpawn`. `resolveTaskSpawnShape` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererRecursionAnalysis.h) classifies each parameter and names the reason when the call stays inline. The spawner evaluates every argument first, a struct into a hidden binding of its own scope (a copy of a named value, a temporary taken over), then allocates one heap block per spawn (`LowerSetupStageState::TaskSpawnSite`): the result's slots first, then the slots of each struct argument, which alias the hidden binding. The task gets the block addresses as its parameters and, for a struct result, the block's start as the result address of the address ABI. A lent argument's drop flag is off from the spawn to the wait, so nothing destroys what a running task reads; an owned (`copy`, `move`) closure is destroyed by the task. `[Task<S>] t{...}` for a struct `S` is a struct local of the spawner's frame with its drop flag off; a read of `t` (`wait(t)`, a name in a multi-wait tuple) joins the task, copies the result slots into that local, turns its drop flag on, turns the lent arguments' flags back on and frees the block. `wait(t)` then reads a place: a binding, an assignment or a call argument copies from it and `t` destroys its value at the end of its scope.
    - Arena safety on `optexe` (asked for in the task): the block and the argument copies are allocated and freed in the spawner's arena. A `String` the task builds lives in the task's arena; the waiter's free is queued to that arena and done at its next allocation, also when the arena was released and taken by a later task, and released arenas keep their blocks. No runtime change was needed. The test "String and struct values of tasks leave the heap empty" shows it without clocks: every task of a round is waited before the next is spawned, so each runs in arena 1; a probe task (it allocates, which drains the queue, then reads `heapLiveBlocks` and `stringHeapBytesInUse`) returns the same numbers before and after 40 rounds, and so does the spawner's own heap.
    - Fixed on the way: `[String] s{wait(t)}` with an inline spawn (native, and the VM before this change) treated the wait as a temporary and freed the text twice (VM `invalid string heap free address`, native exit 139); a multi-wait of struct results failed to lower on every backend (`struct field type mismatch ... got <unknown>`), since the struct path of a task name was read from its `Task<T>` type.
    - Not done: collection (`vector`, `map`) parameters and results and `copy`/`move` parameters that are not closures still run inline with a note (TODO-5847); `string` parameters wait for TODO-5741, which gives them a real call, after which the `string` reason in `resolveTaskSpawnShape` is dropped and the string index passes as a scalar. Destructuring a multi-wait of a user struct (`[a b] wait(x, y)` then `b.field`) is rejected by the checker as before (`unknown field`), on every backend.
    - Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_tasks.cpp gains 4 cases (suite `primestruct.program_matrix.tasks`, 9 cases; each runs vm-step-O0, vm-O0, vm-O2, VM seeds 1 to 8 and optexe-O2, the first also native): `String` argument with `String` and struct results; the heap check above with results bound, read by field, passed on, dropped, assigned over an old value and returned through a function; multi-wait and loop spawns of struct results; closures lent and owned. tests/unit/ir_pipeline/optimizer/test_ir_optexe_tasks.cpp gains "primec lowers a spawned call with String and struct values to a task" (`--opt-report` on `optcpp` and the VM: no note for the `String` and struct spawns, notes naming the reason for a `vector` parameter, a `string` parameter and a `vector` result). No test reads a clock or sleeps.
    - ThreadSanitizer, one manual run: the `optcpp` output of the heap, multi-wait and closure programs built with `clang++ -std=c++23 -O1 -g -fsanitize=thread`, run alone: expected output, no report.
    - Single-threaded code: `--emit=optcpp` of benchmarks/strings/append_chunks.prime and short_strings.prime is byte for byte what it was before the change; instructions retired (`/usr/bin/time -l`) 1,263,691,664 before and 1,270,236,780 after (+0.5%, run to run) and 4,616,818,063 before and 4,616,396,450 after.

- [x] TODO-5796: `Atomic<i32>` and `Atomic<i64>` with the `atomic` effect
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Atomic)
  - parallel_track: mt-primitives
  - scope: Add `/std/sync` with `Atomic<i32>` and `Atomic<i64>` (`load`, `store`, `add` and `exchange` returning the previous value, `compareExchange`), sequentially consistent, copies sharing one heap cell with an atomic handle count, `[sendable]`, and the effect `atomic`. IR opcodes `AtomicLoad`, `AtomicStore`, `AtomicAdd`, `AtomicExchange`, `AtomicCompareExchange` (32/64-bit, heap address) on the VM (a switch point under a seed) and `optexe` (`std::atomic_ref`); arm64 native comes with TODO-5801, other backends reject them.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: 4 tasks adding 1 to a shared `Atomic<i64>` 100000 times each end at 400000 on the VM (default schedule and seeds 1 to 8) and on `optexe`; a compare-exchange spin counter gives the same total; dropping every copy frees the cell (heap statistics)
    - negative: an `Atomic` method in a definition without `atomic` is `atomic operation requires atomic effect`; `Atomic<f32>` and `Atomic<String>` are diagnostics; native and Wasm reject a module using atomics with a message naming the backend
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the two atomic types on the VM and `optexe`.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: `stdlib/std/sync/sync.prime` (`import /std/sync/*`) has `[public struct sendable] Atomic<T>` with `load`, `store`, `add`, `exchange` and `compareExchange` (each `effects(atomic)`); the handle points at a two-slot heap cell (the integer and the handle count), `Copy` adds a handle with an atomic add, `Destroy` drops one and frees the cell at zero. PSIR v35 appends `AtomicLoad`, `AtomicStore`, `AtomicAdd`, `AtomicExchange` and `AtomicCompareExchange` (imm is the width, 32 or 64; address pushed first); v33 and v34 modules still load and the loader refuses an opcode newer than the module's version (migration note in docs/spec/vm-design.md, PSIR versioning). The stdlib's `[host]` definitions under `/std/sync/__psatomic_` are not host imports: the lowerer emits one opcode for a call of one, and only that path prefix may take a `Pointer<i64>` host parameter. The VM runs them as plain slot operations and its scheduler makes each a switch point under a schedule seed only; the fast kernel leaves functions with atomics to the stepping kernel. `optexe` uses `std::atomic_ref<uint64_t>` (the `__atomic_*` builtins where the library lacks it), in a runtime pasted only into modules with atomic opcodes. IR validation rejects the opcodes for native, Wasm, wasm-browser and GLSL: `atomic operations are not supported by the native backend`. New effect `atomic` (mask bit `1 << 13`); a call of an `Atomic` method without it is `atomic operation requires atomic effect: <method> in <definition>`, and `Atomic<f32>`, `Atomic<String>`, `Atomic<bool>` and `Atomic<u64>` are `Atomic requires i32 or i64: Atomic<...>`. A spawned call with an `Atomic` argument runs as a task on both backends (`--opt-report` has no inline note; tested). Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_atomics.cpp (4 tasks x 100000 `add` and a compare-exchange spin counter end at 400000 on the VM default schedule, seeds 1 to 8 and `optexe`; every operation at both widths; `heapLiveBlocks` is 0 after the last copy is destroyed; native and Wasm reject by name), tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_atomics.cpp, tests/unit/semantics/calls_and_flow/test_semantics_calls_and_flow_atomics.cpp, three cases in tests/unit/ir_pipeline/optimizer/test_ir_optexe_tasks.cpp and the stack-effect table rows. By hand: the shared-add, compare-exchange and cell-freeing programs built from `--emit=optcpp` with `clang++ -fsanitize=thread` ran with no ThreadSanitizer report and the right output; `--emit=optcpp` of benchmarks/strings/append_chunks.prime and request_heap.prime is byte-identical to the output before the change. arm64 native atomics stay with TODO-5801. Found while testing and filed, not fixed: TODO-5848 (`return(wait(t))` of a `String` task result) and TODO-5849 (destructured multi-wait of different result types).

- [x] TODO-5797: `Mutex<T>` with scoped `locked(body)` and the `lock` effect
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Mutex)
  - parallel_track: mt-primitives
  - scope: `Mutex<T>` in `/std/sync`: owns a sendable `T` in a heap cell, `locked<Body>(body)` locks, calls `body` with a `Reference<T>`, unlocks on every way out and returns what `body` returns; copies share it (atomic handle count). The lock is a runtime host function: on the VM a scheduler-aware lock (switch points at lock and unlock, blocked tasks count for deadlock detection), on `optexe` `std::mutex` with the holder recorded. Locking it again inside its own body faults with `mutex locked again by the task that holds it`.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the design's `Tally` example prints 7 on the VM (all seeds) and `optexe`; 8 tasks each appending 1000 entries to a `vector<i64>` inside one mutex leave 8000 entries; `locked` returning an `i64` and a body with a `[ref]` capture both work
    - negative: a non-sendable `T` is `Mutex element StringView is not sendable` and, for a struct, `Mutex element Record is not sendable: field `next` is Reference<Record>`; returning or storing the body's `Reference<T>` is the existing borrowed-parameter diagnostic; `locked` without the `lock` effect is a diagnostic; a nested `locked` on the same mutex faults with exit 3 on both backends; two tasks locking two mutexes in opposite order report `deadlock: every task is waiting` on a VM seed that interleaves them
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: `Mutex` on the VM and `optexe`; `RwLock` is TODO-5804.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: stdlib/std/sync/sync.prime has `[public struct sendable] Mutex<T>` with `[effects(lock), return<auto>] locked<Body>([Body mut] body)`: the handle points at a two-slot control cell (the lock, the handle count) and a heap cell holding the value; `Copy` adds a handle with an atomic add, the last `Destroy` drops the value and frees both cells; `locked` takes the lock, holds it in a local whose `Destroy` unlocks (so every way out unlocks), borrows the value and returns `body(reference)`. PSIR v36 appends `MutexLock` and `MutexUnlock` (address popped, nothing pushed) and the `lock` effect (mask bit `1 << 14`); v33 to v35 modules still load (migration note in docs/spec/vm-design.md, PSIR versioning; golden bytecode fixtures updated). The `[host]` definitions `/std/sync/__psmutex_lock` and `__psmutex_unlock` lower to the opcodes like the atomics. VM: the slot holds the holder's task number plus one, both opcodes are switch points under a seed, a task that finds the lock held blocks and counts for `deadlock: every task is waiting`, a second lock by the holder is `mutex locked again by the task that holds it`, and the fast kernel leaves such functions to the stepping kernel. `optexe`: the slot holds the holder's thread state address; a free lock is taken and an unwaited one freed with one atomic operation, a waiter sleeps on one program-wide pthread condition (`OptCppMutexRuntime`, pasted only into modules with mutex opcodes) - not `std::mutex` as the scope said, since the slot must hold the holder for the fault either way. Native, Wasm, wasm-browser and GLSL reject the opcodes: `mutex operations are not supported by the native backend`. Diagnostics: `mutex operation requires lock effect: <method> in <definition>`; `Mutex element StringView is not sendable`; ``Mutex element Record is not sendable: field `next` is Reference<Record>`` (checked per `Mutex` instance before return kinds are built; its source position is in the expanded stdlib text, like other instantiation diagnostics). Compiler changes the feature needed: `return(f())` of a void call in a `return<auto>` definition (a forwarded void return); method calls on a parameter resolve in the type-resolution graph (`locked` returning what a closure parameter returns); a spawned `return<void>` callee whose body makes a closure is a `Task<void>` (inference answered with the closure's struct); `alloc<uninitialized<vector<T>>>` has a size. A spawned call with a `Mutex` argument runs as a task on both backends (`--opt-report` has no inline note; tested). Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_mutexes.cpp (the `Tally` example prints 7, 8 tasks x 1000 appends leave 8000 entries with the right sum, `locked` returning an `i64` and a `[ref]` body, handle copies and moves with `heapLiveBlocks` back to its start, each on the VM default schedule, seeds 1 to 8 and `optexe`; the nested lock faults with exit 3 on both; the opposite-order program deadlocks on some of VM seeds 1 to 16 and finishes on the others, each seed with one outcome; native and Wasm reject by name), tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_mutexes.cpp (hand-built IR: faults, exclusion under 32 seeds, deadlock, serialization and the v35 loader), tests/unit/semantics/calls_and_flow/test_semantics_calls_and_flow_mutexes.cpp, two cases in tests/unit/ir_pipeline/optimizer/test_ir_optexe_tasks.cpp and the stack-effect table rows. By hand: the Tally, vector-append, values and spawned-counter programs built from `--emit=optcpp` with `clang++ -fsanitize=thread` ran with no ThreadSanitizer report and the right output; `--emit=optcpp` of benchmarks/aggregate.prime, benchmarks/strings/append_bytes.prime, an atomics program and a task program is byte-identical to the output before the change. Not as the acceptance words it: 'returning or storing the body's `Reference<T>`' is the borrowed-parameter diagnostic only for a body that takes the value as a borrowed parameter (`[vector<i64>] values`) and returns it; a body that returns its `Reference<T>` parameter returns a copy, and storing it through a `[ref]` capture is not diagnosed (TODO-5853). A `[ref]` body has to be bound to a local before the call (TODO-5851); the examples use two `wait`s because `wait(left, right)` of void tasks does not compile (TODO-5852); a handle keeps a copy of its first value (TODO-5854); `locked` binds the borrowed reference to a local because of TODO-5850. `optexe` threads hang on a lock cycle until TODO-5846.

- [x] TODO-5846: `optexe` faults when every task waits on another
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Runtime Model)
  - parallel_track: mt-optexe-threads
  - scope: The VM ends a program whose tasks all wait on each other with `deadlock: every task is waiting` (exit code 3); on `optexe` the threads block in `ps_task_wait` and the program hangs. With `[spawn]` and `wait` alone a wait cycle needs a task handle to reach another task, which the sendable check rejects, so this becomes reachable with `Mutex` and `Channel` (TODO-5797, TODO-5798). Count running and blocked tasks in the task table and fault with the VM's message when the last running one blocks.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: programs free of wait cycles print what they print now, and a single-threaded benchmark keeps its instruction count
    - negative: a program whose tasks all block faults with the VM's message and exit code 3 on `optexe`, tested without sleeps
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the wait-cycle fault on `optexe`. `Mutex` (the first blocking primitive) exists, so this is ready.
  - result: done 2026-11-10. An `optexe` program counts the threads that run its code (`live`) and the ones that wait (`blocked`) in `OptCppWaitRuntime` (`src/backend/IrToOptCppRuntime.h`, pasted into a module with task or mutex opcodes). A thread waits in `ps_task_wait` for a task whose function has not returned (it used to block in `pthread_join`) or in the slow path of `ps_mutex_lock`, on one condition variable under one gate. An unlock that sees a sleeper and the end of a task signal: the round number goes up, `blocked` goes to 0 and every waiter that still has to wait counts itself again, once per round, so a wakeup without a signal is not counted twice. The thread that makes `blocked` reach `live` faults with `deadlock: every task is waiting` and exit code 3. Tests (`tests/unit/ir_pipeline/optimizer/test_ir_optexe_tasks.cpp`, no sleeps): a waiter that holds the lock its task needs and a task that ends with a lock both fault like the VM on the same module; two tasks that each take one lock, meet through an atomic slot and then ask for the other's lock fault on every schedule; a lock handed from main to a task still prints what the VM prints. By hand: the same opposite-order program from source faulted 300 of 300 runs and 20 of 20 under ThreadSanitizer with no report, a four-task `Mutex` counter printed 80000 and a task program without mutexes its results in every run, also under ThreadSanitizer. `--emit=optcpp` of `benchmarks/aggregate.prime` and `benchmarks/strings/append_bytes.prime` is byte-identical before and after (no task or mutex opcodes, no wait runtime). A program with tasks gets the wait runtime whether or not it has a mutex. A thread inside a host call counts as running, so a program whose last running thread blocks in a read for ever is not reported.

- [x] TODO-5798: Bounded `Channel<T>` with the `channel` effect
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Channel)
  - parallel_track: mt-primitives
  - scope: `Channel<T>{[capacity] n}` in `/std/sync`: a bounded FIFO of a sendable `T` in a heap cell, `send` (moves in, blocks while full), `receive` (a `Maybe<T>`, blocks while empty and open, `none` once closed and drained), `close` (a later send faults with `send on a closed channel`), copies sharing it. Built from the lock of TODO-5797 and two condition waits (VM scheduler, `std::condition_variable` on `optexe`). Backends that run tasks inline reject channels.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the design's producer and `sum_jobs` example prints 4950 for 100 jobs through a channel of capacity 4 on the VM (all seeds) and `optexe`; 3 producers and 3 consumers move 30000 `String`s and every one arrives exactly once; a `String` sent and freed by the receiver leaves the heap statistics balanced
    - negative: send after close faults with exit 3; a task receiving from a channel nobody sends on reports the VM deadlock fault; `Channel<Pointer<u8>>` and sending an arena-made `String` are diagnostics; native, Wasm and the JIT reject a module with channels
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: `Channel` on the VM and `optexe`.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: stdlib/std/sync/sync.prime has `[public struct sendable] Channel<T>` with `send([T move] value)`, `receive()` (a `Maybe<T>`) and `close()`, each `effects(channel)`. The handle is two pointers and a flag: an eight-slot control cell (the lock, the handle count, the not-empty and not-full conditions, the ring's head and count, the closed flag, the capacity) and a ring of `capacity` `uninitialized<T>` slots; `send` moves the value into a slot with `init` and `receive` moves it out with `take`, so an item is never copied and the handle keeps no value of its own (unlike `Mutex`, TODO-5854); `Copy` adds a handle with an atomic add and the last `Destroy` drops the queued items and frees ring and cell. A capacity below 1 is 1. PSIR v37 appends `ConditionWait` (pops the lock address, then the condition address; frees the lock and parks the task) and `ConditionNotify` (wakes every task parked on the slot) and the `channel` effect bit (`1 << 15`); v33 to v36 modules still load. The stdlib reaches them through the host functions `__pscondition_wait`, `__pscondition_notify` and `__pschannel_send_closed` (prints `send on a closed channel`, exit 3). On the VM the scheduler keeps the parked tasks (a switch point under a seed; a parked task counts for `deadlock: every task is waiting`; the fast kernel leaves functions with these opcodes to the stepping kernel); on `optexe` the slot counts notifies and a waiter sleeps on a `std::condition_variable` until the count changes (`OptCppConditionRuntime`, pasted only into a module with condition opcodes), and a sleeping thread counts for the same deadlock fault. Native, Wasm and GLSL reject such a module: `channel operations are not supported by the native backend`. Semantics: `channel operation requires channel effect: <method> in <definition>`, `Channel element Pointer<u8> is not sendable`, and `string escapes its arena via channel (arena: <name>)` for a `String` made in an open `StringArena` (channel bindings with an inferred type and copies of a channel are followed). A spawned call that takes a `Channel` runs as a task on both backends (no inline note in `--opt-report`).
    - Fixed on the way: `init`, `take` and `drop` through a `Pointer<uninitialized<T>>` local whose `T` is a struct named through an import (`String`) failed to lower with `cannot resolve struct layout: String` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererUninitializedTypeHelpers.cpp resolves the pointee name in the scope of the use); a spawned callee that declares a scalar return and returns from a closure call is a `Task` of that scalar.
    - Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_channels.cpp (9 cases: `sum_jobs` prints 4950 on the stepping and fast VM, VM seeds 1 to 8 and `optexe`; 3 producers and 3 consumers move 30000 `String`s and each arrives exactly once on the VM and `optexe`, and 1500 on every VM configuration and seed; heap blocks and string-heap bytes balanced after `String`s are sent, received and left queued; copies, order and capacity 0; send after close and a sender blocked on a full channel that is closed fault with exit 3; the receive deadlock fault; native and Wasm rejection), tests/unit/semantics/calls_and_flow/test_semantics_calls_and_flow_channels.cpp (6 cases), tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_conditions.cpp (8 cases on the opcodes), two cases in tests/unit/ir_pipeline/optimizer/test_ir_optexe_tasks.cpp, and the serialization, CFG and embed fixtures for v37. No test depends on time or on a schedule: totals and exactly-once counts hold under every interleaving.
    - The JIT: the Linux x86_64 JIT of `primevm -O2` (src/native_emitter/NativeJit.cpp) accepts a function only when every opcode is on its list (NativeJitOpcodes.h), and the condition and mutex opcodes are not, so a module with channels runs on the interpreter there rather than being rejected by name. That path is not built on this macOS arm64 machine and was not run.
    - ThreadSanitizer, one manual run: the `optcpp` output of seven channel programs (`sum_jobs`, the 30000 `String` fan, the heap-balance program, send after close, the receive deadlock, a spawned call with a `Channel`, a capacity-1 hand-off) built with `clang++ -std=c++23 -O1 -g -fsanitize=thread -pthread` and run alone: expected output and exit codes, no report.
    - Programs without tasks or sync primitives keep their emitted code: `--emit=optcpp` of benchmarks/aggregate.prime, json_parse.prime and call_fib.prime is byte-identical before and after.
    - A flaky test found by the gate and fixed: the dynamic-discovery case of tests/unit/misc/test_support_stdlib_collection_struct_annotation_detection.cpp wrote its spike file into the real stdlib/std/collections, where the annotation-detection suite running in parallel counted it (one gate run failed on that). The file is now in the test scratch directory and `rediscoverStdlibCollectionsSurfacesForTesting` takes it as an extra file.
    - Gaps found and filed: TODO-5855 (a method call on a `pick` payload of struct type does not lower) and TODO-5856 (`pick` straight on a call that returns a `Maybe` of an owning type does not free the payload; bind the result first).

- [x] TODO-5856: `pick` on a call result frees an owning payload
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: `pick(get()) { none { } some(line) { } }` with `get` returning a `Maybe<String>` leaves the `String`'s bytes allocated on the VM and `optexe` (`stringHeapBytesInUse()` is 32 higher afterwards); `[Maybe<String>] got{get()}` followed by `pick(got)` frees them. A `pick` whose subject is a temporary must destroy the temporary (or the payload it moved out) at the end of the arm. Then drop the "bind the result first" note of `Channel.receive` in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md and pick straight on `receive()` in tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_channels.cpp.
  - implementation_notes: Lowering of `pick` over a call expression (src/ir_lowerer); compare with the bound-local path, which destroys the local at scope end.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the `get()` program leaves `stringHeapBytesInUse()` and `heapLiveBlocks()` unchanged on the VM and `optexe`, also for a `Maybe<vector<i32>>` and a user sum with a `String` variant; the channel heap-balance test passes with `pick(lines.receive())`
    - negative: a payload moved out of the arm (returned, or bound with `move`) is destroyed once, not twice
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: temporaries as `pick` subjects.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: Root cause: `tryEmitPickStatement` and `tryEmitPickExpr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp) evaluated the subject into a pointer local and emitted the arms; nothing owned a subject that was a temporary, so its active payload was never destroyed (a sum local gets a drop entry where it is bound, src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerStatementsBindings.h). Now a `pick` whose subject is a fresh value (a constructor, or a call or method call of a definition that does not return a `Reference` or `Pointer`) opens a cleanup scope of its own with a drop flag and a drop entry for the sum, closed where the arms join; a `return` inside an arm runs it through the function's cleanup of all scopes. The payload binding of an arm carries the drop flag of the sum that owns it (the temporary's, or the sum local's), so `move(line)`, `return(line)` and a `pick` expression that yields the binding release it and the payload is destroyed once, by its new owner.
    - The same family on sum locals was a double free and is fixed by the shared flag: with `[Maybe<String>] got{get()}`, `return(line)`, `[String] owned{move(line)}` and `[String] s{pick(got) { ... some(line) { line } }}` ended the program with `string heap block freed twice`.
    - `Result`: same code path, same leak (`pick(get_text(id))` on a `Result<String, i32>` or `Result<i32, String>`), fixed by the same change and covered by a test. The leak was not measured on the old build for `Result`, only for `Maybe<String>` (32 bytes per `pick`).
    - Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_subjects.cpp (5 cases on the stepping and fast VM at -O0 and -O2 and `optexe`, each printing the heap-block and string-heap differences: a `String` payload in a statement, a loop, an expression, a method-call subject and a constructor subject; a struct with a `String` field, a `Maybe<vector<i32>>` and a user sum with a `String` variant; a payload yielded, returned, moved and copied out of a `pick` on a call result; the same on a sum local; both variants of a `Result`). tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_channels.cpp picks straight on `lines.receive()`. Native has no heap statistics (TODO-5586) and no `String`, so there is no native case.
    - Docs: docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md states the rule; the "bind the result first" note of `Channel.receive` in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md is gone.
    - Gaps found and filed: TODO-5857 (a `vector<T>` payload binding reads an address as its count), TODO-5858 (`some<Vector<i32>>` of a fresh vector leaves one heap block), TODO-5859 (a `pick` expression cannot yield a vector), TODO-5860 (a moved payload binding poisons a later binding of the same name).

- [x] TODO-5656: Thread-safe `SharedString`: an atomic owner count
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-03
  - phase: Multithreading (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, Heaps; docs/spec/strings-and-views.md, Beyond std::string)
  - parallel_track: string-concurrency
  - scope: `SharedString` (stdlib/std/text/shared.prime, TODO-5638) keeps its owner count as a plain `i32` right after the text's 0 byte, at an unaligned offset, so it may be shared on one thread only. Move the count to an aligned place in the block, count with the atomic opcodes of `/std/sync` (stdlib/std/sync/sync.prime, `atomicAdd`; `add` on copy, `add(-1)` on destroy, the last owner frees), and mark the type `[sendable]`. Freeing on a thread other than the allocating one is the heaps' remote free (TODO-5794 on `optexe`, TODO-5800 on native). Update the strings-and-views status table.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: 4 tasks each copy and destroy one `SharedString` 100000 times and `owners()` is 1 at the end on the VM (all seeds) and `optexe`; a `SharedString` passes as a spawn argument and through a `Channel`; the single-threaded `SharedString` tests are unchanged
    - negative: a `SharedString` made inside a `StringArena` still cannot be sent (`string escapes its arena via spawn`)
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the owner count and sendability; native follows with TODO-5800.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-10
  - result: A `SharedString` is `[sendable]` and counts its owners atomically.
    - Design: the count is one heap slot of its own beside the text block, not a place in the block as the scope worded it: byte regions are not 8-byte aligned and the atomic opcodes take heap slot addresses only. `fill` stores 1, `Copy` adds 1, `Destroy` adds -1 and frees the text and the count when the old value was 1; all through `/std/sync/__psatomic_*_i64`, which need no effect. TODO-5861 puts the count in the block again.
    - Semantics: `SharedString` left the non-sendable leaf list (src/semantics/SemanticsSendable.cpp), and a `SharedString` made in an open `StringArena` is tracked like a `String` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidateSliceLoans.cpp), so spawn and `send` reject it by name.
    - Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_shared_strings.cpp (4 tasks of 100000 copies leave `owners()` 1; spawn argument and `Channel<SharedString>`; made on one task and freed on another), on VM step, VM -O0/-O2, seeds 1 to 8 and `optexe`, each with balanced heap counts; two semantics cases in test_semantics_calls_and_flow_sendable.cpp. `owners()` reads 1 only after the scope of the `Task` handles ends, since a handle keeps its argument copies.
    - Cost on `optexe` (instructions retired, `/usr/bin/time -l`): 5000000 copy and destroy rounds 8.98e9 before, 4.49e9 after; 1000000 `sharedFrom` and destroy rounds 3.01e9 before, 4.67e9 after (the second allocation).
    - ThreadSanitizer: the optimized C++ of the first two test programs, built by hand with `-fsanitize=thread`, ran clean.

- [x] TODO-5802: `serveWorkers`: one thread polls and accepts, N workers answer
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-09
  - phase: Multithreading and Web apps (docs/MultithreadingPrototype.md, The Server; docs/WebAppsPlan.md, Concurrency)
  - parallel_track: mt-server
  - scope: In `/std/http/server` add `serveWorkers(handler, config)` and `serveWorkersUntil(handler, config, stop)` (effects `net_io`, `heap_alloc`, `io_err`, `task`) and `ServerConfig.workers` (0 = one per core); `/std/os` gets `cpuCount()` and a way for a worker thread to block `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`; `/std/net` gets a wake pipe. The poller watches the listener, the open connections and the wake pipe, and hands a readable connection's slot to the workers over a `Channel`; a worker receives, parses, calls the handler inside a `Mutex` that holds the whole handler, serializes, sends and closes, and returns the slot (or returns an incomplete request to be watched again). `Router`, `AppRouter<State>` and `WebApp<State>` keep their plain `Function` handlers, sendable by construction (TODO-5791), so an app is sendable when its `State` is; the `Mutex<Handler>` element check (TODO-5797) is what rejects an app that is not. `serve` and `serveUntil` stay as they are.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: an `optexe` test with 4 workers answers 16 concurrent clients sending 50 requests each, and a counter app's count equals the number of POSTs; a client that sends half a request and stalls does not delay the others with 1 worker; a VM test serves 2 workers deterministically on the default schedule; examples/apps/todo_web and hello_button compile with `serveWorkers` without changing their handlers
    - negative: a `WebApp<State>` whose `State` has a `Pointer<u8>` field passed to `serveWorkers` is `Mutex element WebApp<State> is not sendable: field `state` of WebApp<State>, field `bytes` is Pointer<u8>` (a handler capturing a `Pointer` never gets this far: TODO-5791 rejects it where it is written); SIGTERM stops the server within `acceptTimeoutMs` while all workers are busy
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the library server; switching the examples and measuring is TODO-5803.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: stdlib/std/http/server.prime has `serveWorkers(handler, config)` and `serveWorkersUntil(handler, config, stop)` and `ServerConfig.workers` (0 = `cpuCount()`, at most 64), on `optexe`. The poller (`poll_slots`) accepts and watches the listener, the slots and a `/std/net` `wakePipe()`; a readable slot goes to the workers over `Channel<SlotJob>` and comes back over `Channel<SlotDone>` plus an `Atomic<i32>` count and `wake` (both channels hold one entry per slot, so no `send` waits). A worker (`work_slot`) does one `recv`, parses, and for a complete request calls the handler inside `Mutex<Function<void, Request, Reference<Response>>>`, serializes, sends and closes; only the handler call holds the mutex. The slots' bytes are one block shared through a `[sendable]` struct. Workers call the new `/std/os` `blockStopSignals()`. On stop the poller closes the listener and the waiting connections and closes the job channel; the call returns when each worker has finished its request. Differences from the scope: the effects are `net_io, heap_alloc, io_err, task, lock, channel, atomic` (the primitives' effects are not hidden); the workers are not generic, so the handler is erased into a `Function` and an app that is not sendable is rejected by the `Function` rule, ``closure cannot be a Function: capture `inner`, field `state` of WebApp<Borrowed>, field `bytes` of Borrowed is Pointer<u8>``, not by the `Mutex` element check; route tables keep their literal names in the new sendable `LiteralText` (`/std/text/view`) instead of `StringView`. Not done: the VM test with 2 workers, because the VM rejects the `/std/net` host calls (TODO-5862). Compiler fixes it needed: `pick(...)` and `wait(task)` lower inside a namespace (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererHelpers.cpp), and a templated method call on a receiver whose type is a rooted path resolves inside a namespace (src/semantics/TemplateMonomorphMethodTargets.cpp). Tests: tests/unit/backend_host/test_http.cpp (ctest `PrimeStruct_http_workers`: 16 clients x 50 POSTs with 4 workers count 800; a stalled half request beside 1 worker; SIGTERM with the only worker held inside a handler: the idle connection ends and new connects are refused before the held request is answered, exit 0; the not-sendable diagnostic; hello_button and todo_web compile with `serveWorkers`), none of which measures time, and a namespace case in tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_channels.cpp. The fixture server ran clean under ThreadSanitizer by hand. Gaps found and filed: TODO-5863 to TODO-5866.

- [x] TODO-5867: A string heap that alternates between block sizes keeps its chunks
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Multithreading and Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md, Concurrency; found measuring `serveWorkers`)
  - parallel_track: text-heap
  - scope: The string heap (`VmTextHeap`, include/primec/runtime/VmTextHeapBody.inc) keeps one chunk whose blocks are all free and returns any other at once. A thread that holds no other strings and builds one that grows through two block sizes (32 then 64 bytes: a `String` with four short `append`s) therefore empties two chunks per string, returns one and builds it again for the next string: a 256 KiB region, zeroed, with a free list of up to 8192 entries. Measured with `optexe` on arm64 (200000 strings, instructions retired): 279e9 on a spawned task against 2.2e9 on the main thread, where the program's other strings keep the chunks; with three strings of 20, 40 and 70 bytes alive in the task, 2.4e9. Every `serveWorkers` worker is such a thread: a small POST costs 157 us of user CPU per request with 1 worker against 37 us under `serve`, and 62 us when each worker keeps one string per block size alive (5751 against 12282 requests/s, `serve` 14224). Stop the rebuild without holding much more memory per thread: candidates are one free chunk kept per block size with small first chunks (as arenas have: 4 KiB, doubling), or a few free chunks kept regardless of size and re-cut for the size asked for.
  - implementation_notes: `releaseOwn` (the `emptyChunks_ > 1` branch) and `allocateOwn`; tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_text_heap.cpp pins today's policy ("an emptied chunk is returned except one kept for reuse") and the memory limit (`heldBytes`). Not measured yet: whether `parseRequest` of a small request (550000 instructions on the main thread) and the todo_web list page (21 us per item) pay the same cost.
  - acceptance:
    - a unit test counts chunk regions taken from the heap core: a thread's heap that builds and drops 1000 strings growing through 32, 64 and 128 bytes takes no new region after the first string (today it takes one or more per string)
    - the same loop as an `optexe` program on a spawned task retires at most 2x the instructions of the main-thread run (measured by hand, recorded in the result note)
    - the memory limit still refuses what it refused, and the result note records the bytes a heap holds after freeing everything, before and after
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the free-chunk policy of the string heap; the slot heap is unchanged.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: New free-chunk policy in include/primec/runtime/VmTextHeapBody.inc (VM and `optexe` share it). A size class's first chunk is 4 KiB (`FirstChunkBytes`; one block for a class with larger blocks) and each further chunk is as large as the class's chunks together, up to 256 KiB (4, 4, 8, 16 ... 256 KiB). Of the chunks whose blocks are all free each class keeps one, the smaller of two (the lower of two alike), and the heap keeps at most `FreeChunkLimit` = 1 MiB of them, returning the largest first (the first chunks of all 45 classes are 506 KiB together, so they all fit). Bound on retained memory: a heap whose blocks are all free holds at most one chunk per size class used and at most 1 MiB; before it held one 256 KiB chunk. After the acceptance loop (strings through 32, 64 and 128 bytes) it holds 12 KiB (three first chunks) where it held 256 KiB. The memory limit still counts the chunks and large blocks held and fails with `string memory limit exceeded`; under a limit the free chunks are now returned before an allocation is refused. Because chunks start small, a limit that refused a request only because the first small string had taken a whole 256 KiB chunk now grants it: `setStringMemoryLimit(300000)` with `tryReserve(200)` then `tryReserve(100000)` refused the second before (262144 + 100001) and grants it now (4096 + 100001); tests/unit/language/test_string_owned.cpp pins the same case with a limit of 102000, and that the large reserve alone fits. `TextHeapStats` gained `chunkBytes`, `freeChunkBytes` and `chunkRegions` (C++ only; `__pstext_stat` is unchanged). Tests (tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_text_heap.cpp): chunk sizes 4 KiB doubling to 256 KiB; one free chunk per class, the smaller of two; a class at a chunk boundary keeps the chunk it empties; the first chunks of all classes are kept together; free chunks beyond 1 MiB are returned largest first; a main-thread heap and a thread arena's heap that build and drop 1000 strings through 32, 64 and 128 bytes take 3 regions for the first string and none after; the limit counts a class's growing chunks and returns free chunks before refusing; the random cycle test and tests/unit/runtime/test_vm_heap_arenas.cpp pin the new bound in place of "at most one chunk". Measured by hand on arm64 (11 processors, load average 8 to 20), instructions retired by a loop of 200000 strings (a 3-byte text and four `append`s, 77 bytes), before -> after: `optexe` main thread 544.2e9 -> 3.19e9, `optexe` spawned task 553.2e9 -> 3.39e9 (1.06x the main thread; the scope's 2.2e9 for the main thread was a program that kept other strings alive, this one keeps none and rebuilt chunks on the main thread too); VM without tasks 554.8e9 -> 13.4e9, VM spawned task 694.7e9 -> 153.4e9, where the 140e9 that remain are not the heap: a VM program that uses tasks runs on the step kernel (TODO-5870). Guards: `optexe` instructions of benchmarks/strings before -> after: short_strings 4.623e9 -> 4.638e9, request_heap 5.325e9 -> 5.332e9, append_chunks 1.259e9 -> 1.245e9, request_arena 5.505e9 -> 5.501e9; peak resident bytes: short_strings 1,900,544 -> 1,835,008, request_heap 65,208,320 -> 65,437,696, append_chunks 56,262,656 -> 55,394,304, request_arena 2,113,536 -> 2,097,152, the loop on the main thread 3,670,016 -> 1,851,392 and on a task 2,752,512 -> 1,982,464. `scripts/benchmark.sh` with the baseline: every time check passes before and after and the 40 medians moved between 0.91x and 1.16x, the two largest moves in the C and Rust entries; it exits 1 before and after for the `primestruct_native` artifact sizes only (16538 bytes against 4300 or 8601 allowed). `scripts/benchmark_strings.py --budget` is over budget before and after under this load (short_strings 30.7x -> 32.1x of `std::string`, request_heap 16.4x -> 16.2x, append_chunks 7.1x -> 6.9x, append_bytes 6.7x -> 6.6x). Startup: no code was added to a startup path, and a program's first string of a class now takes and zeroes a 4 KiB region where it took 256 KiB. ThreadSanitizer, one manual run: an `optcpp` program with three tasks running the loop, two tasks returning a `String` the main thread frees and the loop on the main thread, built with `clang++ -std=c++23 -O1 -g -fsanitize=thread -pthread`: expected output, no report.

- [x] TODO-5869: `serveWorkers` with 1 worker is within 10% of `serve`
  - owner: ai
  - status: done
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Multithreading and Web apps (docs/WebAppsPlan.md, Concurrency)
  - parallel_track: mt-server
  - scope: The measurements of benchmarks/http (docs/WebAppsPlan.md, Concurrency) missed two targets: a 32 KB form with a small handler reaches 0.50x of `serve` with 1 worker (target 0.9x) and 1.60x with 4 (target 2.5x). Most of the gap was the string heap rebuilding chunks on worker threads (TODO-5867, done). Measure again, then remove what is left: the worker makes a new `Response` per request where `serve` reuses one, and the polling thread wakes three times per request (accept, readable, slot handed back).
  - acceptance:
    - `python3 benchmarks/http/run_http_benchmarks.py` on an idle machine with at least 6 processors: the 32 KB form at least 0.9x `serve` with 1 worker and at least 2.5x with 4; the numbers replace the table in docs/WebAppsPlan.md
    - no gate test measures time; full release gate green
  - stop_rule: the two form targets; the list page waits for read-only handlers (TODO-5804).
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: One target met, one missed and refiled. Measured with `python3 benchmarks/http/run_http_benchmarks.py --build-dir build-release` on the 11-processor arm64 machine, load average 5 to 8 (not idle; right after a gate the first rerun saw 32 to 68). 32 KB form, requests per second: before this task and after the string heap change `serve` 5598, 1 worker 2926 (0.52x), 4 workers 11290 (2.02x); after it `serve` 5527, 1 worker 5660 (1.02x, target 0.9x met), 4 workers 13329 (2.41x, target 2.5x missed; 2.41x to 2.45x over four runs). List page 249 / 238 (0.96x) / 237 (0.95x); 1 ms handler 961 / 964 / 976. Cause of the 1-worker gap, measured as user time per request of the server process (`getrusage`): 327 us against 146 us for `serve`, because the worker parsed the request in the slot block the polling thread allocated and on `optexe` a byte of another thread's block is checked on an out-of-line path (`byteAtSlow`); `work_slot` now parses a copy in a block the worker owns (160 us). The check itself is TODO-5872. Each worker keeps one `Response`. Wakes: a new connection goes straight to a worker and a worker that closes a connection wakes the poller only while every slot is taken (`full`), so the poller wakes once per request, not three times; system time per form with 1 worker 44 us to 39 us, throughput unchanged within noise. Tried and dropped because they measured nothing: reading again in the worker after an incomplete request, and accepting every waiting connection per round. Why 4 workers miss 2.5x: every server here stops near 14,600 new connections a second whatever the request costs (8-byte POST: `serve` 15,450; 1, 2 and 4 workers 14,620, 14,718, 14,656; the form with 1, 2, 3, 4, 8 workers 5570, 10,411, 12,585, 13,167, 13,457; the same with 8, 16 or 32 clients), so the form with 4 workers is at nine tenths of the ceiling. What sets the ceiling was not measured; refiled as TODO-5871. No gate test measures time. Full release gate green.
