  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: In `pick(held) { some(box) { box.twice() } }` with `held` a `Maybe<Box>` and `twice` a method of the struct `Box`, lowering stops with `missing semantic-product method-call target: /main -> twice`; the same happens for a `String` payload (`line.byteAt(0i32)`). Field access on the payload and passing it to a function work, and so does the method after `[Box] owned{box}`. Publish the method-call target for a receiver that is a `pick` payload binding.
  - implementation_notes: The payload binding has no binding fact the method-target publisher finds; compare how a `[Box] owned{box}` local gets one. Reproduce with a `[struct] Box` that has `[return<i32>] twice()` and `[Maybe<Box>] held{[some] Box{}}`.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the `Box` program prints 6 on the VM and `optexe`; `line.byteAt(0i32)` on a `Maybe<String>` payload prints the byte; a payload of a user sum with a struct variant works the same way
    - negative: a method the struct does not have is still `unknown method`
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: method calls on `pick` payload bindings.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: the walk that publishes method-call targets with the locals in scope (`forEachLocalAwareSnapshotCall`, src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorSnapshotLocals.cpp) did not know a `pick` arm's payload binding, so a call whose receiver is one got no target and lowering stopped; a `[Box] owned{box}` local is a binding statement, which the walk records. Now the walk gives each arm of a `pick` its payload binding (`pickArmPayloadBindings`, src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprPick.cpp, the binding the arm's validation uses), for a `pick` statement and a `pick` expression.
    - Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_subjects.cpp, 3 cases: a struct payload of `Maybe<Box>` and of a user sum, in a `pick` statement and a `pick` expression, on every backend of the matrix (VM, C++, `optexe`, native); a `String` payload (`line.byteAt(0i32)`, `line.count()`) of a sum local and of a call result and a struct payload of a call result on the VM and `optexe` with the heap statistics balanced; a method the struct does not have is still `unknown method: /Box/thrice`.
    - Workarounds removed: the copy-first locals (`[Window] target{w}`, then `target.close()`) in examples/native_ui/hello_window.prime and tests/fixtures/ui/screenshot/{toolbar,file_types,settings}_demo.prime, and the copy-first note and example lines of "Native UI" in docs/spec/stdlib-reference.md.
    - This was the first half of the older TODO-5544 (found with native UI), which now keeps only `Type.staticHelper()` through `import`.

- [x] TODO-5857: A `vector<T>` payload of a sum reads its count and elements in a `pick` arm
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: With `get_values` returning a `Maybe<vector<i32>>` that holds two elements, `[Maybe<vector<i32>>] got{get_values(4i32)}` and `pick(got) { none { } some(values) { print_line(vectorCount<i32>(values)) } }` prints 896 (an address) on the VM and `optexe` instead of 2; `[vector<i32>] own{values}` then `own.count()` prints the same number and `own[1i32]` prints 2, the count. A `Maybe<Vector<i32>>` payload reads 2. The payload is destroyed correctly in both spellings. Make the payload binding of a `vector<T>` variant a vector the arm can read.
  - implementation_notes: The payload is stored as the specialized vector record (`resolveVectorPayloadStructPath`, src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpers.cpp) and `makePickPayloadLocalInfo` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp) binds it as a struct pointer, while the arm's uses are typed `vector<i32>`.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the program prints 2 on the VM and `optexe`, `values[1i32]` prints the second element, and `[vector<i32>] own{values}` copies the two elements; the heap statistics stay balanced
    - negative: a `Maybe<Vector<i32>>` payload keeps reading 2
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: `vector<T>` payload bindings of `pick` arms.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Three causes. The sum's slot layout counted a `vector<T>` payload as one slot (`resolveStructSlotLayoutFromDefinitionFieldIndex`), so a returned or copied sum lost the vector record and the arm read an address as the count. The binding-fact walker of the semantic product did not bind pick-arm payloads, so `values[1i32]` had no collection fact. And the sum did not own a `vector<T>` payload: a payload from a place shared the source's buffer and nothing destroyed it, so a `Maybe<vector<i32>>` returned from a function dangled. The layout now sizes the payload by its record, the walker binds arm payloads (`pickArmPayloadBindings`), `LoweredSumPayloadStorageInfo::lifecyclePath` routes the payload's copy and destroy through the builtin vector helpers, and `makePickPayloadLocalInfo` binds the payload as a vector local the sum keeps owning. Tests: three cases in tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_subjects.cpp (VM and optexe, heap balanced; a stdlib `Vector` payload still reads 2). Found and filed: TODO-5873 (a sum temporary passed as an argument is not destroyed), TODO-5874 (assigning to a `mut` sum local leaks the old payload) and TODO-5875 (`count()` on a local declared `Vector<i32>` does not lower).

- [x] TODO-5858: `some<Vector<i32>>(vector<i32>(1i32, 2i32))` frees the vector it copies from
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: `if(true) { [Maybe<Vector<i32>>] got{some<Vector<i32>>(vector<i32>(1i32, 2i32))} }` leaves `heapLiveBlocks()` one higher on the VM and `optexe`, with or without a `pick` on it: the vector passed to `some` is copied into the sum and the original's buffer is never freed. Take the fresh vector over, or free it after the copy.
  - implementation_notes: Compare with a `[vector<T> copy]` parameter, which takes a fresh vector over (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md), and with `some<String>(text)`, which is balanced. tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_lifecycle.cpp has the `Maybe<Vector<i32>>` case without a heap check.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the block leaves `heapLiveBlocks()` unchanged on the VM and `optexe`, also when the `Maybe` is returned from a function and picked
    - negative: `some<Vector<i32>>(kept)` of a named vector leaves `kept` usable and frees both at scope end
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: a fresh `Vector` passed as a sum payload.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: the builtin constructor `vector<T>(...)` has no definition, so `isOwnedTemporaryArgumentExpr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) did not count it as a temporary argument, and a parameter typed `Vector<T>` (the stdlib struct, here `some`'s `[T] value`) got no drop entry: `[some] value` copied the vector and the original was never freed. Any definition with a read-only `Vector<i32>` parameter leaked the same way. The constructor now counts as an owned temporary, so the callee's scope destroys it. Test: "a fresh Vector argument belongs to the callee" in tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_subjects.cpp (VM and optexe, heap balanced; a named vector stays usable; a `Maybe` returned from a function and picked).

- [x] TODO-5859: A `pick` expression yields a vector
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: `[vector<i32>] kept{pick(get_values(7i32)) { none { vector<i32>{} } some(values) { values } }}` with `get_values` returning a `Maybe<vector<i32>>` stops lowering with `stale semantic-product pick aggregate result metadata: /std/maybe/Maybe__.../none` on the VM and `optexe`. A `String` or struct result works. Publish the aggregate result of a `pick` whose arms yield a vector and lower it; the yielded payload is taken over, not copied (as for a `String`).
  - implementation_notes: `tryEmitPickExpr` and `inferPickAggregateResult` in src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp; the yielded payload binding is a vector local the sum owns (`makePickPayloadLocalInfo`).
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the program prints `kept.count()` 2 and its elements on the VM and `optexe`, with the heap statistics balanced
    - negative: arms that yield a vector and an `i32` are still a type error
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: vector results of `pick` expressions.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: `resolveSemanticProductPickAggregateResultStructPath` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp) resolved an arm's published type to a scalar or a struct only, so `vector<i32>` was reported as stale metadata. A vector result is now the five-slot vector record: `tryEmitPickExpr` copies the arm's record into it and takes a fresh vector over, releases the drop flag of an owning local or, for the payload binding, of the sum that owns it (taken over, not copied), and copies any other place with the builtin vector helpers; `isFreshBuiltinVectorExpr` counts a `pick` expression as fresh, so the binding or parameter that receives it owns it. Tests: three cases in tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_subjects.cpp (VM and optexe, heap balanced; a `none` arm, a sum local, a `mut` binding, an argument, `vector<String>`; vector and `i32` arms rejected). Found and filed: TODO-5876 (`return(pick(...))` of a vector and assignment from a `pick` expression are rejected) and TODO-5877 (a field as an arm's value is not copied: a `String` field is freed twice).

- [x] TODO-5860: Moving a `pick` payload binding does not poison a later binding of the same name
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-10
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: `pick(get(5i32)) { none { } some(line) { [String] owned{move(line)} } }` followed by a second `pick(get(6i32)) { none { } some(line) { [String] copied{line} } }` in the same function is rejected with `use-after-move: line was moved at ...`: the move is recorded for the name, not for the binding of the first arm. Naming the first binder differently compiles. End a payload binding's move state with its arm.
  - implementation_notes: The use-after-move check of src/semantics; compare with a `[String] line{...}` declared in two sibling `if` blocks, which is accepted.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the two picks compile and print both bytes on the VM; so do two arms of one `pick` that bind the same name and one of which moves it
    - negative: `move(line)` followed by a use of `line` in the same arm is still `use-after-move`
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: move state of `pick` payload bindings.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: moves are recorded by name in `movedBindings`, and `pick` validation (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprPick.cpp) neither removed a payload binding's name when its arm ended nor tracked arms as separate paths, so a move in one arm poisoned the same name in a later arm or `pick`, and a move of an outer binding in one arm made a sibling arm's read a use-after-move. Each arm of a statement or expression `pick` now starts from the move state before the `pick`, the payload binding's entry is dropped at the arm's start and end, and after the `pick` a binding is moved when an arm that falls through moved it (as for `if` branches). Tests: three cases in tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_subjects.cpp (two picks and two arms binding `line` with one moving it, statement and expression form, an outer binding moved in one arm and read in the other, VM and optexe, heap balanced; use after the move in the same arm and use of an outer binding after the `pick` whose arm moved it are still rejected).

- [x] TODO-5866: A closure captures a `pick` payload binding
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md; found with `serveWorkers`)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: In `pick(held) { some(job) { [mut] show{[job]([i32] more) { return(job + more) }} ... } }` with `held` a `Maybe<i32>`, the capture is `unknown capture: job`. Copying the payload to a local first (`[i32] amount{job}`, then `[amount]`) works. Let a capture list name a payload binding of the enclosing `pick` arm.
  - implementation_notes: the capture lookup does not see payload bindings.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the program prints 5 on the VM and `optexe`; a `String` payload captured by value and by `ref` works
    - negative: a capture of a name that is bound in another arm is still `unknown capture`
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: captures of `pick` payload bindings.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: the closure rewrite (`rewriteExpr` of src/semantics/SemanticsValidateLambdaClosures.cpp) collects the names a lambda may capture from bindings and parameters only, and a `pick` arm's payload binding is neither, so it was never visible. The rewrite now treats a `variant(payload) { ... }` arm of a `pick` as a scope that binds `payload` for that arm's body alone. Tests: two cases in tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_subjects.cpp (an `i32` payload of a sum local prints 5; a `String` payload of a call result and of a sum local captured by value and by `ref`, and in a `pick` expression, VM and optexe, heap balanced; a capture of another arm's binding is still `unknown capture`). Found and filed: TODO-5878 (a `[move name]` capture does not compile for any binding).

- [x] TODO-5877: A field an arm of a `pick` expression yields is copied
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: With `[Holder mut] holder` holding a `[String] name` field, `[String] kept{pick(get(3i32)) { none { label(0i32) } some(line) { holder.name } }}` ends the program with `string heap block freed twice` on the VM: the result shares the field's block and both are destroyed. An arm value that is a place other than a local (a field, an element) must be copied with the type's `Copy` helpers. A `[vector<i32>] items` field as the arm value is rejected instead with `pick branches must return compatible types`; accept it and copy it too.
  - implementation_notes: `tryEmitPickExpr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp) disarms a temporary and releases a local's drop flag, and copies a vector place already (`emitStructCopyHelpersFromPtrs` with the vector path); the struct branch has no copy for a place. The branch type check is in src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprPick.cpp.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: both programs print the field's value on the VM and `optexe`, the holder's field stays readable, and the heap statistics are balanced
    - negative: a `String` field and an `i32` arm are still incompatible
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: field and element values of `pick` expression arms.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: the aggregate branch of `tryEmitPickExpr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp) copied the arm value's slots into the result and then only told a temporary and a local apart; a field or an element fell through with no `Copy`, so the result shared its block. The semantic branch check (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprPick.cpp) infers a struct or vector arm as an aggregate and a field or element access as unknown, which is why a vector field was "incompatible". Fix: an arm value that is a place the arm does not end is copied with the type's `Copy` helpers (the same rule as TODO-5879, one root cause, one commit), and an arm whose kind is unknown takes the kind of its inferred binding type when that is a struct or a vector. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_values.cpp, "a pick expression arm copies a field and an element" (a `String` field, a `vector<i32>` field and a `vector<String>` element; the holder stays readable; heap statistics balanced on the VM and `optexe`) and "pick arms that yield a String field and an integer are rejected".

- [x] TODO-5879: A `pick` expression's result owns a value of its own
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: A `pick` expression takes over whatever its arm names. `[vector<i32>] kept{pick(get_values(3i32)) { none { vector<i32>{} } some(values) { mine } }}` with `mine` a local of the enclosing scope leaves `mine` readable after `kept` freed its buffer, and the same for a `String` local. `pick(held) { none { label(0i32) } some(line) { line } }` with `held` a sum local clears the local's drop flag, so a later `pick(held)` reads a block the result freed; with `held` a parameter the caller destroys the payload too (`string heap block freed twice`). A `pick` expression of struct type passed as an argument is never destroyed (32 string-heap bytes a call). The result must own a value of its own: take over a fresh value and a local that ends with the arm, copy every other place, and destroy a result given as an argument after the call.
  - implementation_notes: found testing whether a discarded vector-yielding `pick` expression, or one whose arm yields a borrowed vector, leaks or frees twice; those do not (the callers of `isFreshBuiltinVectorExpr` copy a borrowed place in the arm and free a discarded result).
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the programs print the local's and the payload's value before and after the result ends, on the VM and `optexe`, with the heap statistics balanced
    - negative: covered by TODO-5877 (mismatched arms are still rejected)
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: what a `pick` expression arm's value does to the place it names.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: `tryEmitPickExpr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp) treated an arm value that is a name as a local the arm may give away: it cleared the drop flag of whatever the name was (an outer local kept its buffer and its flag for a vector, a sum local lost its flag for a payload binding, a borrowed subject has no flag at all), so two owners freed one block or one owner read a freed one. `isOwnedTemporaryArgumentExpr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) did not count a `pick` expression as a temporary, so a struct result given as an argument was never destroyed. Fix: one rule in `tryEmitPickExpr` for struct and vector results: a fresh value is taken over; a local declared in the arm, and the payload binding of a subject the `pick` itself owns (a call result), is taken over by clearing its flag; every other place is copied with the type's `Copy` helpers. A `pick` expression is an owned temporary argument. The risk that started it is tested and absent: a discarded vector-yielding `pick` expression and arms yielding a vector parameter, a vector local or an element are balanced. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_values.cpp (8 cases, VM step and fast kernels and `optexe`, heap statistics in every one). Spec: docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md. Found and filed: TODO-5880 (a payload moved out of a sum local leaves it readable; a payload of a borrowed sum can be moved or returned) and TODO-5881 (`some<T>(...)` with a call to `some<T>` inside its argument is "recursive" on the VM).

- [x] TODO-5880: A payload moved out of a sum local leaves the local moved-from
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: In `pick(held) { none { } some(line) { [String] taken{move(line)} } }` with `[Maybe<String>] held`, the arm takes the payload's storage and `held` stays readable: a later `pick(held)` reads a block that `taken` freed. Passing `line` to a `[String move]` parameter does the same. With `held` a parameter or a field (a sum the definition only borrows), `move(line)` and `return(line)` end the program with `string heap block freed twice`: the owner destroys the payload as well. Moving a payload binding that owns resources out of a sum local must leave that local moved-from; a payload of a borrowed sum must not be moved or returned.
  - implementation_notes: the payload binding shares the subject's drop flag (`bindPickPayload`, src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp), so the lowering is right for an owned subject; the semantic validator (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprPick.cpp) erases the binder's moved state at the end of the arm without telling the subject.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: a payload moved out of a sum local and one passed to a `move` parameter print their value on the VM and `optexe` with the heap statistics balanced
    - negative: a `pick` on the sum local after the move is `use-after-move`; `move(line)` and `return(line)` of a payload of a sum parameter and of a sum field are rejected
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: moves and returns of `pick` payload bindings.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: the validator of a `pick` arm (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprPick.cpp) forgot the payload binding's moved state when the arm ended and never asked who owns the payload, so the sum local stayed readable after its payload's storage went to a new owner, and a payload of a sum the definition only borrows could be moved or returned although its owner destroys it too. A second cause hid half of it: `bindingOwnsResources` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorStatementContainerHelpers.cpp) did not resolve a struct name the file imports, so outside `/std/text` a `String` (binding or payload) passed to a `move` parameter was not moved-from at all. Fix: `pickPayloadOwner` classifies the subject of a `pick` for a payload that owns resources: a local or a `copy`/`move` parameter is moved-from at the end of an arm that moved the payload out (`move(line)`, a `move` parameter), in `pick` statements and expressions; the payload binding of a read-only parameter, `this`, a `Reference`/`Pointer` local, a field, an element or another lent payload is lent, and `move(line)`, a `move` parameter and `return(line)` are rejected with `borrowed pick payload cannot be moved: line` / `borrowed pick payload escapes via return: line`. A fresh subject, a builtin `vector<T>` payload (its binding is a view that is copied) and `[unsafe]` bodies are untouched. `bindingOwnsResources` resolves imported struct names. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_values.cpp, "a payload moved out of a sum local is destroyed by its new owner" (VM and `optexe`, heap statistics balanced), "a sum local whose payload was moved out is moved-from" and "the payload of a borrowed sum is not moved or returned". Spec: docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md.

- [x] TODO-5873: A sum temporary passed as an argument is destroyed after the call
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: With `[return<i32>] first_of([Maybe<String>] held)` that picks `held` and returns a byte of the payload, `print_line(first_of(get(8i32)))` (`get` returns a `Maybe<String>` holding a fresh `String`) leaves `stringHeapBytesInUse()` 32 higher on the VM and `optexe`; the same call with a `Maybe<vector<i32>>` leaves `heapLiveBlocks()` one higher. Passing a named sum local is balanced. A sum returned by a call and passed straight to a read-only parameter has no owner: destroy its active payload when the call returns, as for a fresh `vector<T>` passed to a read-only parameter (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md).
  - implementation_notes: `emitActiveSumPayloadDestroyFromSumPtr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp) destroys the payload of a sum local and of a fresh `pick` subject; the inlined-call argument setup in src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerInlineCallsBody.h frees a fresh builtin vector argument.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: both calls leave the heap statistics unchanged on the VM and `optexe`
    - negative: a named sum local passed to the same parameter is still usable after the call and destroyed once
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: sum temporaries passed to read-only parameters.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: the parameter loop of an inlined call (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerInlineCallsBody.h) registers a drop entry for a struct or builtin vector the callee owns (a `copy`/`move` parameter, a temporary argument) and had no branch for a sum, so a sum nobody names was bound to the parameter and never destroyed; a sum given to a `move` parameter leaked the same way once the caller's drop flag was released. The validator had the matching gap: `bindingOwnsResources` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorStatementContainerHelpers.cpp) did not know a sum type, so a sum local passed to a `move` parameter was not moved-from, and `return(held)` of a read-only sum parameter handed the caller's payload to a second owner (`string heap block freed twice` once the result was destroyed). Fix: a sum parameter whose payloads need destroying gets a drop entry in the callee when its argument is a temporary (a call result, a constructor, a `pick` expression) or the parameter is `move`; a sum owns what its payloads own in `bindingOwnsResources`, so a sum local given to a `move` parameter is moved-from and `return` of a read-only sum parameter is rejected with `borrowed parameter escapes via return`. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_values.cpp, "a sum temporary passed as an argument is destroyed by the callee" (the two calls of the acceptance and a named local read twice; VM and `optexe`, heap statistics balanced), "a sum handed to a move parameter is destroyed by its new owner" and "a sum that owns a payload is moved-from and does not escape a borrow". Spec: docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md. Found and filed: TODO-5882 (a vector of sums does not destroy the payloads of its elements).

- [x] TODO-5874: Assigning to a `mut` sum local destroys the payload it held
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: `[Maybe<String> mut] held{get(4i32)}` followed by `held = get(6i32)` and `held = none<String>()` leaves `stringHeapBytesInUse()` 64 higher at scope end on the VM and `optexe`: neither assignment destroys the payload the local held. The same with a `Maybe<vector<i32>>` leaves two heap blocks. `assign` of an owning struct value destroys the old value first (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md); do the same for a sum local.
  - implementation_notes: Sum assignment is lowered in src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersConstructEmit.cpp (`emitExistingSumValueIntoLocal` and the constructor paths write into the local's slots); `emitActiveSumPayloadDestroyFromSumPtr` destroys an active payload.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the block leaves the heap statistics unchanged on the VM and `optexe`, for a `String`, a struct with `Destroy` and a `vector<T>` payload
    - negative: `held = held` keeps the payload readable
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: assignment to sum locals.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: `assign` to a struct-shaped local goes through the owned-assign callback of src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerOperatorsConversionsAndCalls.h, which asks `structNeedsDestroyHelpers` about the target's type; a sum path is no struct with destroy helpers, so the new sum was copied slot by slot over the old one: the old payload leaked, and a sum assigned from another local shared that local's payload (`string heap block freed twice` when both were destroyed). Fix: the callback has a branch for a sum whose payloads need destroying: the new value is taken over from a temporary (a call, a constructor, `move(other)`) or, from a place, given a payload of its own by the new `SumHelpersContext::emitActiveSumPayloadCopyFromSumPtrs` (the payload's `Copy` helpers, a vector's buffer); then the payload the local held is destroyed if the local owned one, and the local owns the new value. The copy is made first, so `held = held` is valid. A `mut` sum local built from a place now has a drop flag that starts cleared, so an assignment can give it a value of its own, and a sum local initialized with `move(other)` takes over `other`'s drop flag (it leaked before). Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_values.cpp, "assigning to a mut sum local destroys the payload it held" (`String`, `vector<i32>` and a struct with `Destroy`; `held = held`) and "a sum local assigned from a place gets a payload of its own", on the VM and `optexe` with the heap statistics balanced. Spec: docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md. Not covered: a target without a drop flag (a `mut` sum parameter, a `Reference` to a sum, a sum field) is still overwritten slot by slot.

- [x] TODO-5883: Assigning to a `mut` builtin vector local destroys what it held and copies a place
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: Found while reproducing TODO-5876 (assignment from a `pick` expression). `[vector<i32> mut] kept{make_values(1i32)}` followed by `kept = make_values(3i32)` leaves one heap block live at scope end on the VM and `optexe` (`heapLiveBlocks()`), and `kept = other` for a `[vector<i32>] other` ends with `VM error: invalid heap free address in IR`: the assignment stores the source's record pointer in the local, so the vector it held is never freed and a place assigned to it has two owners.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: assignment from a call, a constructor, a place, itself and in a loop leaves the heap statistics unchanged on the VM and `optexe`, for scalar and struct elements; the place keeps its own elements
    - negative: the source of `kept = other` is unchanged by a later `kept.push(...)`
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: builtin `vector<T>` locals that own their record.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: `assign` to a name (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererOperatorCollectionMutationHelpers.cpp) handled struct-shaped locals with ownership and fell through to `emitExpr(rhs); StoreLocal` for everything else; a builtin vector local holds a pointer to its record, so the assignment repointed it at the source's record. Fix: a builtin vector local with a drop flag goes through the owned-assign callback (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerOperatorsConversionsAndCalls.h, `BuiltinVectorAssignPath`): the new record is slot-copied into a temporary, given a buffer of its own unless the right side is a fresh vector (`isFreshBuiltinVectorExpr`: a constructor, a user call, a `pick` expression), then the vector the local held is destroyed through its drop entry (the scope-end code, `emitBuiltinVectorDrop`), and the new record is installed in a record of the assignment's own, so a loop that assigns again does not overwrite the old record before it is destroyed. The drop entry carries the element copy path (`DropEntry::builtinVectorElementCopyPath`), set for a declared `vector<T>` local and an inferred vector of scalars. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_container_copies.cpp, "assigning to a mut vector local destroys what it held and copies a place" (VM and `optexe`, heap statistics balanced) and "a vector local assigned from another keeps its own elements on every backend". Spec: docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md. Left open as TODO-5884: a `mut` vector parameter and an inferred vector-of-struct local.

- [x] TODO-5876: `return(pick(...))` of a vector and assignment from a `pick` expression
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: In a definition with `return<vector<i32>>`, `return(pick(get_values(first)) { none { vector<i32>{} } some(values) { values } })` is rejected with `return type mismatch: expected vector`; binding the `pick` to a local and returning the local works, and so does the same `return` with `String` arms. And `kept = pick(get(3i32)) { none { label(0i32) } some(line) { line } }` for a `[String mut] kept` (the same for a `vector<i32>`) is rejected with `unable to infer implicit template arguments for /std/maybe/some`, though no `some` call is in the statement. Accept both; the returned or assigned value is the `pick`'s own (docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md), and the assignment destroys what the local held.
  - implementation_notes: The return check is in src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorStatementReturns.cpp (the return kind of a `pick` expression with collection arms); `pick` branch typing is in src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorExprPick.cpp. `tryEmitPickExpr` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerSumHelpersPickAndDiagnostics.cpp) builds the result.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: both programs print the picked value on the VM and `optexe` with the heap statistics balanced, for a `String` and a `vector<i32>`
    - negative: `return(pick(...))` with `i32` arms from a `return<vector<i32>>` definition is still a return type mismatch
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: `pick` expressions as return and assignment values.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Two root causes. (1) Return: the return check (src/semantics/SemanticsValidatorStatementReturns.cpp) normalized the returned binding's whole type text to a collection path; for a `pick` expression the inferred binding names the collection with its element type in the type name (`vector<i32>`) and an empty template argument, so the text did not normalize and the value was not seen as a vector. The check now falls back to the binding's type name. (2) Assignment: the operator rewrite of the text filter (src/text_filter/TextFilterPipelineOperatorRewrite.cpp, `parsePrimaryExpression`) ended the right operand of `=` at the `)` of `pick(subject)`, because a `{` after a space is not a call suffix, so `kept = pick(x) { arms }` became `assign(kept, pick(x)) { arms }`: a `pick` call without arms followed by a block, which the implicit-template inference then reported against `/std/maybe/some`. A `pick(...)` operand now takes its arm block (on the same line) with it, and the arms are rewritten like any other body. The assigned value is the `pick`'s own (fresh), so the `String` path and the vector path of TODO-5883 take it over and destroy what the local held. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_values.cpp, "a pick expression is returned as a vector and as a String", "a pick expression assigned to a mut local replaces what it held" (VM and `optexe`, heap statistics balanced) and "a returned pick expression with integer arms is not a vector". Spec: docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md. Found and left open as TODO-5885: a vector temporary used only as the receiver of `count()` or an index is not freed.

- [x] TODO-5875: `count()` on a local declared `Vector<T>` lowers
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: With `import /std/collections/*`, `[Vector<i32>] local{vector<i32>(1i32, 2i32)}` followed by `print_line(local.count())` stops with `VM lowering error: vm backend only supports entry argument indexing`; `vectorCount<i32>(local)` prints 2, and the same binding declared `vector<i32>` reads its count with `local.count()`. The payload binding of a `Maybe<Vector<i32>>` in a `pick` arm fails the same way. Make the method spelling resolve like the helper call.
  - implementation_notes: The error is the fallback of the entry-argument access path (src/ir_lowerer), reached when the `count` method call has no collection or method target; compare the facts published for the `vector<i32>` spelling with `--dump-stage`.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: `local.count()` and `local[1i32]` print 2 on the VM and `optexe` for the `Vector<i32>` local and for a `Maybe<Vector<i32>>` payload binding
    - negative: `local.missing()` is still `unknown method`
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: method calls on bindings declared with the stdlib `Vector<T>` spelling.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: `tryEmitCountAccessCall` (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererCountAccessHelpersEmit.cpp) takes every unqualified `count` call with one argument as a builtin count and rejected a receiver whose type text is neither a string nor a builtin collection with the entry-argument fallback error; the receiver of `local.count()` on a `Vector<i32>` local is the monomorphized stdlib struct (`/std/collections/vector/Vector__t...`), for which the semantic product has a resolved method target (`/std/collections/vector/count`). The rejection now applies only when no method target is resolved, so the call lowers as the ordinary method call it is. `local[1i32]` worked already. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_values.cpp, "count and an index read a local and a pick payload declared Vector" (a local, the payload of a fresh `Maybe<Vector<i32>>` and of a sum local; VM and `optexe`, heap statistics balanced) and "a method a Vector local does not have is an unknown call target" (the diagnostic is `unknown call target: missing`, not `unknown method` as the acceptance guessed). Found and left open as TODO-5886: `push` on a `Vector<T>` local is `unknown call target: push`.

- [x] TODO-5878: A `[move name]` lambda capture compiles
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: `[i32 mut] n{4i32}` followed by `[mut] show{[move n]([i32] more) { return(n + more) }}` is rejected with `unable to infer implicit template arguments for /main__lambda0_make`; the same for a `String`, a `vector<i32>` and a `pick` payload binding. docs/spec/value-lifecycle.md lists `[move name]` as a capture form, and no test uses it. Make the capture move `name` into the closure, so that `name` is moved-from afterwards and the closure destroys the value.
  - implementation_notes: `desugarLambda` (src/semantics/SemanticsValidateLambdaClosures.cpp) passes `move(name)` to the closure's factory; the factory's template inference does not see through the `move` call.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: the program prints 5 on the VM and `optexe`; a moved `String` and a moved `vector<i32>` print their value with the heap statistics balanced
    - negative: a use of `name` after the lambda is `use-after-move`
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: `[move name]` captures.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root cause: `desugarLambda` turns the lambda into a call of a generic factory and passes `move(name)` for a `[move name]` capture; `inferBindingTypeForMonomorph` (src/semantics/TemplateMonomorphImplicitTemplateInference.cpp), which gives the factory's type parameters their types from the arguments, knew `location(x)` (a `[ref x]` capture) and not `move(x)`, so no type was inferred for the capture. It now gives `move(x)` the type of `x`. The move itself, the moved-from state of the name and the closure's ownership of the value were already right once the factory was instantiated. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_pick_values.cpp, "a move capture hands the value to the closure" (an `i32`, a `String`, a `vector<i32>`, the payload binding of a fresh and of a named sum; VM and `optexe`, heap statistics balanced) and "a name captured by move is moved-from after the lambda" (`use-after-move` for a `String` and a vector).

- [x] TODO-5884: Assigning to a `mut` vector parameter or an inferred vector-of-struct local keeps one owner
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: Two builtin `vector<T>` targets that `assign` still only repoints. (1) In `swap_in([vector<i32> mut] values) { values = make_values(8i32) ... }` called with a temporary, the assignment leaves two heap blocks live after the call on the VM (`heapLiveBlocks()`): the parameter's drop entry has no element copy path, so the vector it held is not destroyed and the new one has no owner. (2) `[mut] names{make_named(1i32)}` (the type inferred, the element a struct with a `String` field) leaves `stringHeapBytesInUse()` 32 higher per element at scope end even without an assignment: the drop entry of an inferred binding has no element struct path, so the buffer is freed and the elements are not destroyed; an assignment to it stores the source's record pointer (a second owner for a place, a leak for a temporary). A local declared `[vector<T> mut]` and an inferred vector of scalars are right.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: both shapes leave the heap statistics unchanged on the VM and `optexe`, with and without an assignment from a temporary and from a place
    - negative: a `[vector<i32> mut]` parameter that is not assigned still writes through to the caller's vector
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: builtin vector parameters and inferred builtin vector locals.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: Root causes, one per shape. (1) The owned assignment looked its target up among the drop entries by drop flag and needed the entry's element copy path; a parameter's entry had none and a `mut` parameter was skipped by the parameter loop altogether, so the assignment only repointed the callee's local. (2) The binding lowering read the element type from the binding's own `vector<T>` transform, which an inferred binding does not have, so the drop entry had no element path. Found on the way: a `[vector<T> copy mut]` parameter was not copied (the callee pushed into the caller's vector), a `[vector<T> move]` parameter was never freed, and a temporary vector of structs passed to any parameter destroyed only its first element (the entry's element stride was one slot). Fix: the element path now lives on the local (`LocalInfo::vectorElementLifecyclePath`), set where the element type is known - a declared or inferred binding (the type semantics inferred), a parameter - and the owned assignment (`BuiltinVectorAssignPath` in src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerOperatorsConversionsAndCalls.h) reads it from the target instead of searching the drop entries. The parameter rule (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererLowerInlineCallsBody.h): a `copy` parameter is copied unless the argument is a fresh vector or `move(x)`; a `move` parameter and a fresh vector given to any parameter belong to the callee (drop entry, element slots from the element's layout); a `mut` parameter given a place is a borrow, and assigning to it destroys the caller's vector and writes the new record over the caller's record, so the caller sees and later destroys the new one. An inferred `[mut] b{a}` of a vector of structs is now a copy, as the declared form was. Tests: tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_vector_owners.cpp (new suite, 5 cases: scalar and struct `mut` parameters with temporary, place, field and nested-borrow arguments; write-through without assignment on every backend; inferred locals; `copy mut` and `move` parameters), heap statistics balanced on the VM and `optexe`.

- [x] TODO-5882: A vector of sums destroys the payloads of its elements
  - owner: ai
  - created_at: 2026-11-11
  - phase: Result and control flow (docs/spec/errors-and-file-io.md)
  - parallel_track: pick-payloads
  - scope: `[vector<Maybe<String>> mut] all{vector<Maybe<String>>{}}` followed by `all.push(get(5i32))` (`get` returns a `Maybe<String>` holding a fresh `String`) leaves `stringHeapBytesInUse()` 32 higher per element when `all` goes out of scope, on the VM and `optexe`: the vector frees its buffer and does not destroy the active payload of each element. A named sum local pushed into the vector is moved-from (it owns a payload), so its payload is lost the same way.
  - acceptance:
    - positive: pushing a sum temporary and a named sum local into a `vector<Maybe<String>>` and letting it go out of scope leaves the heap statistics unchanged on the VM and `optexe`; so do `pop` and `clear`
    - negative: a `vector<Maybe<i32>>` still runs and frees only its buffer
    - full release gate green
  - stop_rule: builtin `vector<T>` whose element is a sum with a payload that needs destroying; if copies and removal are larger than one commit, split them.
  - finished_at: 2026-11-11
  - result: the struct slot layout of a sum now lists each variant whose payload is a struct or a builtin vector (tag and lifecycle path), and the generic copy and destroy helpers (src/ir_lowerer/IrLowererFlowControlHelpers.cpp) dispatch on the tag, so a sum met as a vector element, as an element of a nested vector or behind a vector field is copied and destroyed by the same rule as a struct. Push takes over a temporary or a named local and copies another place; pop, clear, scope exit, vector copies, assignment and `copy` parameters keep one owner per payload. Tests: three cases in tests/unit/program_matrix/test_program_matrix_vector_owners.cpp (`Maybe<String>` and a user sum with struct and vector payloads, VM and `optexe`, heap statistics balanced; `vector<Maybe<i32>>` frees only its buffer). `remove_at`/`remove_swap` on a vector of sums do not compile yet (TODO-5887).
